# Perumal Tirumoli

## 1.1. Iruliriyac cudarmanigal

*iruḷiriyac cudarmaṇigaḷ imaikkum neṟṟi     inattutti aṇipaṇam āyirangaḷ ārnda aravarasap perunjōdi anantan ennum     aṇi viḷangum uyar veḷḷai aṇaiyai mēvit tiruvarangap perunagaruḷ teṇṇīrp ponni     tiraikkaiyāl adi varudap paḷḷi koḷḷum karumaṇiyaik kōmaḷattaik kaṇḍu koṇḍu en     kaṇṇiṇaigaḷ enṟu kolō kaḷikkum nāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tiruvarangap perunagaruḷ – in the big town known as śrī rangam; iruḷ iriya – for darkness to get scattered; sudar maṇigaḷ – effulgent carbuncles; imaikkum – shining brightly; neṟṟi – forehead; inattutti aṇi – appearing such that two divine feet have joined together; paṇangaḷ āyiram ārnda – having a thousand hoods in full; aravu arasan – being the king of snakes; perum jōdi – being splendorous; anantan ennum – tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); aṇi viḷangum – with a high degree of beauty; uyar veḷḷai aṇaiyai – distinguished, white coloured divine mattress; mēvi – fitting well; teḷ nīr ponni – kāviri, which has clear water; tirai kaiyāl adi varuda – to massage the divine feet with waves, which are like hands; paḷḷi koḷḷum – having divine sleep; karumaṇiyai – like a bluish gemstone; kōmaḷattai – periya perumāḷ who has unmatched tenderness; en kaṇ iṇaigaḷ – my eyes; kaṇḍu koṇḍu – vorśipping without a break; kaḷikkum nāḷ – that happy day; enṟu kolō – when is that day?

**Перевод:**

**periya perumāḷ, who has unmatched tenderness and is like a bluish gemstone, is fitting well with, and having a divine sleep, on the great, white coloured mattress of anantāḻvān, in the huge city of śrī rangam. The splendorous anantāḻvān is the king of all snakes, with a thousand hoods spread out fully, with forehead shining brightly and with each of his heads appearing like a pair of divine feet having joined together. periya perumāḷ’s divine feet are gently massaged by the river kāviri, with her waves which are like her hands. When will my pair of eyes worship such perumāḷ, without a break?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction for this pāsuram) In kauśīdakī upaniśat, in the paryanka vidyai chapter, when emperumān is about to awaken from his sleep atop the mattress of anantāḻvān, also called as ādiśēśan, how a mumukśu (a samsāri, desirous of attaining mōkśam (liberation)), is longing to experience him has been described. In the same way, āḻvār is longing to experience periya perumāḷ in śrī rangam and describes this in the first pāsuram. When a jīvātmā (one born in samsāram) gets liberated and goes to mōkśam, kauśīdakī upaniśat says in ślōkam 1-51 śrīrūpabarhaṇam tasmin brahmāstē I tamittamvit pādē naivādyārōhati īī On that mattress of ādiśēśan, pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) sits like an embankment for emperumān’s divine feet. On that mattress, śrīman nārāyaṇan, parabrahmam, is reclining. The liberated jīvātmā, knowing this, climbs onto brahmam, through the divine feet. The muktātmā (liberated ātmā) steps on that mattress of ādiśēśan; emperumān asks him “Who are you?” The muktātmā responds saying, just like he is a prince, “aham brahmāsmi” (I am an attribute for brahmam).*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**iruḷ iriyac cudar maṇigal imaikkum neṟṟi** – Through the first part of the pāsuram, āḻvār describes how emperumān is mercifully reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān, who is having forehead emitting a brightness which scatters darkness.

**inattutti aṇi paṇam āyirangaḷārnda** – the term *inattutti* refers to the divine feet which are joined together. Each head of ādiśēśan appears like a pair of divine feet which are joined together with *tirumaṇkāppu* (the divine mark carried on the forehead of śrīvaiṣṇavas). Who has such hoods which are a thousand?

**aravarasap perunjōdi** – nāgarājan – one who has an effulgence affirming that he is the king of all snakes.

**anantan ennum** – In line with his name of *anantan* (one who is without an end), he has such a divine form and auspicious qualities that he contains within himself even emperumān, who pervades all entities.

**ennum** – there are other great snakes such as sumukhan, dakśan, vāsuki et al. anantāḻvān is closer to emperumān than all these other snakes.

**aṇi viḷangum uyar veḷḷai aṇaiyai mēvi** – fitting well atop ādiśēśan, who is having eminent beauty, and who has a distinguished whiteness without any trace of other colours.

**tiruvarangap perunagaruḷ** – It has been said in śāstras (sacred texts) that emperumān is enjoyed by nityas (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam) and muktas (those who reached śrī vaikuṇṭam after being liberated from samsāram). śaiva purāṇam ślōkam brings this out clearly as in *vaikuṇṭētu parēlōke śriyā śardam jagatpati:* I  
*āste viṣṇur acintyātmā bhaktair bhāgavatais saha* īī (śrī mahāviṣṇu, who is the *svāmy* (one who owns) and whose basic form is beyond anyone’s comprehension, mercifully resides in the distinguished śrī vaikuṇṭam along with śrīdēvi, bhaktas (those who are totally engaged with emperumān’s auspicious qualities) and bhāgavatas (those who are totally involved in carrying out kainkaryam (service) to emperumān)). āḻvār longs for that experience in this tiruvarangam itself. This verse refers to śrī rangam which is a magnificent town. Further, āḻvār describes as to how emperumān mercifully resides in this place.

**teṇṇīrp ponni tiraikkaiyāl adivaruda** – river kāviri, who flows with clear water, gently massages the divine feet of emperumān with her waves, which are like her hands.

**paḷḷi koḷḷum karumaṇiyai** – one who is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān like a blue gemstone

**kōmaḷattai** – he is such tender that his divine form will not tolerate if one were to even look at him constantly.

**kaṇḍu koṇḍu** – for his followers, just looking at him is the ultimate benefit, just like a famished person would look at food.

**en kaṇṇiṇaigaḷ** – my eyes, which are famished.

**enṟu kolō kaḷikkum nāḷē** – taittirīya upaniśat, bhruguvalli 10-6 says “*aham annam aham annam aham annam* I  
*aham annādō’hamannādō’ham annāda*: īī” (I am an enjoyable entity (for emperumān), like food. I am the enjoyer of food (enjoying the happiness of emperumān)). āḻvār is wondering as to when he will attain the happiness of having darśan of perumāḷ in śrī rangam, just like the muktātmā enjoys after attaining mōkśam.

In the next article, we will take up the 2nd pāsuram of this decad.

## 1.3. Em manbil ayan

*em māṇbil ayan nāngu nāvinālum    eduttētti īriraṇḍu mugamum koṇḍuemmādum eḻil kaṇgaḷ ettinōdum    toḻudētti inidu iṟainja ninṟa sem ponammān tan malarkkamalam koppūḻ tōnṟa    aṇi arangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷī koḷḷumammān tan adi iṇaikkīḻ alargaḷ ittu angu    adiyavarōdu enṟu kolō aṇugu nāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

em māṇbil ayan – brahmā, who has greatness in all ways; nāngu nāvinālum – with his four tongues (which were created to praise emperumān); eduttu ētti – vorśipping, appropriate to his eagerness; īriraṇḍu mugamum koṇḍu – with the four faces (created to recite the four vēdams); emmādum – on all sides; eḻil kaṇgaḷ ettinōdum – with eight beautiful eyes; toḻudu – with folded hands; ētti – praising; inidu irainja ninṟa – one who stood to prostrate (at the divine feet of emperumān, to worship him); sem pon ammān than – golden hued emperumān who is most desirable, his; kamala malar – lotus flower’s; koppūḻ tōnṟa – divine navel to appear; aṇi arangattu – in the temple which is like an ornament for samsāram (materialistic realm); aravu aṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum – reclining atop tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); ammān than – the supreme being’s; adi iṇaikkīḻ – under the divine feet; alargaḷ ittu – proffering flowers; angu adiyavarōdu – with \[emperumān’s\] followers, there; enṟu kolō – when is it?

**Перевод:**

**Golden-hued emperumān is reclining on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān, with his divine navel, which is like a lotus flower, appearing, inside the temple at śrī rangam which is like an ornament for samsāram. brahmā, who has four tongues with which to praise emperumān, four faces with which to recite the four vēdams, eight eyes which appear on all sides and with folded hands, is waiting to prostrate (at the divine feet of such emperumān). When will I approach that emperumān to proffer flowers at his divine feet and be together with his followers?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction)In this pāsuram, āḻvār expresses his longing to be with the followers of emperumān and proffer flowers at emperumān’s divine feet.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**em māṇbil ayan** – brahmā, who has greatness in all ways, viz. he is without any shortcoming in praising and vorśipping emperumān. āḻvār describes this further…

**nāngu nāvinālum** – with four tongues which are the implements for praising emperumān.

**eduttētti** – to praise emperumān appropriate to his level of eagerness and desire.

**īriraṇḍu mugamum koṇḍu** – with four faces which have been formed to recite the four vēdams.

**emmādum** – in the front, back as well as on all the sides

**eḻil kaṇgaḷ ettinōdum** – he realised the benefit of having many eyes to enjoy \[emperumān’s\] beauty.

**toḻudētti inidu irainja ninṟa** – (brahmā) stood to worship, praise and prostrate to emperumān, urged by his love towards emperumān

**sem pon ammān than malark koppūḻ tōnṟa** – being desired just like reddish gold, emperumān is splendorous with his lotus like navel, which is responsible for the creation of all entities in samsāram

**aṇi arangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum ammān than** – śrī rangam is like a jewel for samsāram. emperumān is lying atop tiruvanantāḻvān inside that śrī rangam. His ….

**adi iṇaik kīḻ alargaḷ ittu** – offering flowers at his divine feet. Offering flowers is representative of carrying out other kainkaryams (services) such as offering fragrance sticks, lamp etc.

**angu adiyarōdu enṟu kolō aṇugu nāḷē** – When will I stand together with other devotees of emperumān who carry out kainkaryams? While nammāḻvār desired to be with followers in śrī vaikuṇṭam, just as he had said in tiruvāimoḻi 2-3-10 “*adiyārgaḷ kuḻāngaḷai udan kūduvadu enṟu koḷo*” (when will I be together with groups of followers?), this āḻvār expresses his desire to be with followers in *bhūlōka vaikuṇṭam* (śrī rangam is referred to as śrī vaikuṇṭam on earth).

We will next take up the fourth pāsuram in this decad.

## 1.4. Mavinai vay pilandu

*māvinai vāy piḷandu uganda mālai vēlai    vaṇṇanai en kaṇṇanai van kunṟam ēndiāvinai anṟu uyakkoṇḍa āyar ēṟṟai    amarargal tam talaivanai am tamiḻ inbappāvinai av vadamoḻiyaip paṟṟaṟṟārgaḷ    payil aravangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷumkōvinai nāvuṟa vaḻutti enṟan kaigaḷ    koymmalar tūy enṟu kolō kūppu nāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

māvinai vāy piḷandu – tearing the mouth of the demon kēśi (who came in the form of a horse); uganda – feeling happy (that an enemy of his followers has been destroyed); mālai – having affection towards his followers; vēlai vaṇṇanai – having the complexion of ocean; en kaṇṇanai – being kriṣṇa, who is easy to approach for me; anṟu – on that day (when indra created a hailstorm); val kunṟam ēndi – lifting the mighty gōvardana hill; āvinai uyak koṇḍa – one who protected cows; āyar ēṟṟai – head of herds-people; amarargaḷ tam talaivanai – lord of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam); am tamiḻin – with beautiful tamiḻ; inbap pāvinai – most enjoyable, similar to aruḷicceyal (divya prabandam); av vada moḻiyai – being sweet like śrī rāmāyaṇam in samskruta language; paṟṟu aṟṟārgaḷ payil – the place where those who have given up attachment towards worldly pursuits, live permanently; arangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum – one who is reclining atop tiruvanantāḻvān, inside śrī rangam; kōvinai – śrī ranganāta, the lord; nāvuṟa vaḻutti – praising him till scars form on the tongue; en than kaigaḷ koy malar tūy – proffering the flowers plucked with my hands; kūppum nāḷ – the day when I can perform anjali (cup the palms together to worship); enṟu kolō – when will it be?

**Перевод:**

**śrī ranganātan, the lord, is reclining atop tiruvanantāḻvān, inside the temple in śrī rangam. He is the one who had torn apart the demon kēsi and felt happy that he had destroyed the enemy of his followers; he protected cows and covherds, as their leader, by lifting a strong mountain, gōvardanam. He has the complexion of an ocean; he is the lord of nityasūris. He is most enjoyable through the divine hymns in tamiḻ as well as in samskrutam. śrī rangam is the place where his followers, who have given up attachment with worldly materials, reside permanently. When will I go there, sing his praises until scars form on my tongue and proffer flowers, which were plucked by my hands, at his divine feet?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction)In this pāsuram, āḻvār longs to praise emperumān, proffer flowers and perform anjali.*

**māvinai vāy piḷandu uganda mālai** – emperumān showed that he is one with deep affection towards his followers by killing a demon who came in the form of a horse by tearing his mouth effortlessly and feeling happy that he was able to destroy the enemy of his followers.

**vēlai vaṇṇanai** – even if he nurtures the enemies of his followers instead of destroying them, he makes his followers to be deeply engaged with him through his cool, ocean-like complexion.

**en kaṇṇanai** – not only does he have mere beauty of form, he is also submissive towards me.

**van kunṟam ēndi āvinai anṟu uyakkoṇḍa āyar ēṟṟai** – *van kunṟam ēndi* – this could be construed either as a strong hill which would not move when indran caused hail storm or as a strong hill which will remove the ennui of his followers. *āvinai anṟu uyak koṇḍa*– he is the one who protected cows which do not even know how to express gratitude. *āyar ēṟṟai* – he is highly distinguished when compared with other herds-people who are of his age.

**amarargaḷ tam talaivan** – he is greater than nityasūris who have eminence similar to his eminence. He is their lord.

**andamiḻin inbappāvinai** – he is sweet like the aruḷicceyal pāsurams (divine hymns) as brought out by āḻvār’s first pāsuram *iruḷ iriyac cudar maṇigaḷ imaikkum neṟṟi*.

**avvadamoḻiyai** – he is sweet like śrī rāmāyaṇam which has been written in samskrutam language and which is praised by everyone. Through these two terms, āḻvār clearly brings out the fact that emperumān is more beautiful and brings more happiness than aruḷicceyal pāsurams and great grantams (samskruta compositions) such as śrī rāmāyaṇam

**paṟṟaṟṟārgaḷ payilarangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷikoḷḷum kōvinai** – he is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān, as the *svāmy* (lord) of his followers who have held on only to him, after rejecting all the worldly pursuits as useless, and who have taken residence in śrīrangam.

**nāvuṟa vaḻutti** – praising him until the tongue gets scarred.

**endan kaigaḷ koy malar tūy enṟu kolō kūppu nāḷē** – when will it be that I can proffer flowers and other materials for his divine worship until my hands get scarred and I am able to cup my palms together in *anjali* posture?

We will take up the 5th pāsuram in this decad, in the next article.

## 1.5. Inaiyilla innisai yal

*iṇaiyillā innisai yāḻ keḻumi inbat    tumburuvum nāradanum iṟainji ēttatuṇaiyillāt tonmaṟai nūl tōttirattāl    tonmalarkkaṇ ayan vaṇangi ōvādēttamaṇimāda māḷigaigaḷ malgu selva    madiḷ arangattaravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷummaṇivaṇṇan ammānaik kaṇḍu koṇḍu en    malarccenni enṟu kolō vaṇangu nāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

inbat tumburuvum nāradanum – tumburu and nārada maharishi (celestial entities) who have attained happiness (through their music); iṇaiyillā innisai – having music which is unmatched and sweet; yāḻ keḻumi – stringing their vīṇās (stringed musical instruments); iṟainji ētta – to praise (after falling at the divine feet of emperumān); tuṇai illā – without any match; tol maṟai nūl tōttirattāl – with hymns from the sacred vēdas (religious texts) which are timeless; tol malar kaṇ ayan – brahmā, who is residing on the divine navel which is always permanent; vaṇangi ōvādu ētta – to keep praying continuously (at the divine feet); maṇi māda māḷigaigaḷ – mansions which are studded with gemstones; malgu selvam – having opulence in abundance; madiḷ arangattu aravaṇaiyuil paḷḷī koḷḷum – reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān inside śrī rangam temple, fortified with protective walls; maṇivaṇṇan ammānai – sarvēśvaran (lord of all) who is having a beautiful form; kaṇḍu koṇḍu – prostrating before him, without break; en malar senni – my head, which is decked with flowers; vaṇangum nāḷ enṟu kolō – when will I worship him at his divine feet?

**Перевод:**

**śrī ranganātan is vorśipped and praised by celestial entities such as tumburu and nāradar, with their stringed instruments, which are without any match. brahmā, who resides on the divine navel of that emperumān, is vorśipping him through hymns from the most sacred texts, vēdas, without any break. That emperumān is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān, inside the temple at śrī rangam. śrī rangam is a town, where mansions are studded with gemstones, and has abundant opulence. When will I worship the divine feet of that emperumān, who has a beautiful form, with my head?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction)āḻvār is longing to worship emperumān with his head, in this pāsuram.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**iṇaiyillā innisai yāḻ keḻumi** – playing on the music instrument *yāḻ* (a stringed instrument, similar to vīṇā), which has no match, and which produces sweet music

**inbat tumburuvum nāradanum iṟainji ētta** – tumburu and nārada maharishi (celestial entities) who are in a blissful state because of the music, fall at the divine feet of emperumān and praise him.

**tuṇaiyillā tonmaṟai nūl tōttirattāl** – with hymns from śāstrams (sacred texts) such as the ancient vēdams which have no match, and itihāsas and purāṇas which explain vēdams. The opinion is that all these sacred texts were created to praise emperumān.

**tonmalarkkaṇ ayan vaṇangi ōvādu ētta** – as brahmā, who was created first from (emperumān’s) divine navel, prostrated at the divine feet of emperumān and praised him without a break.

**maṇi māda māḷigaigaḷ malgu selvam** – having mansions and palaces which are embedded with invaluable ruby stones, and which possess opulence

**madiḷ arangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum** – one who is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān, inside the huge temple in śrī rangam, which is protected with huge walls.

**maṇivaṇṇan ammānaik kaṇḍu koṇḍu** – after having darśan (seeing) emperumān who has a beautiful divine form, like a bluish gemstone.

**en malarccenni enṟu kolō vaṇangu nāḷē** – āḻvār is longing to know as to when his head, which is decorated with flowers, will bow down to the divine feet of such emperumān.

## 1.6. Ali malarmel ayan

*aḷimalar mēl ayan aran indiranō(du) ēnai    amarargaḷ tam kuḻuvum arambaiyarum maṟṟumteḷi madisēr munivargaḷ tam kuḻuvum undit    tisai tisaiyil malar tūvic cenṟu sērumkaḷi malar sēr poḻil arangat(tu) uragam ēṟik    kaṇ vaḷarum kadal vaṇṇar kamalak kaṇṇumoḷi madi sēr tirumugamum kaṇḍu koṇ(du) en    uḷḷa miga enṟu koḷo urugu nāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

aḷi malar mēl ayan – brahmā who is sitting atop a lotus flower which is teeming with beetles; aran – Sivan; indiranōdu ēnai amarargaḷ tam kuḻuvum – other groups of celestial entities along with indran; maṟṟum – further; teḷi madir sēr munivargaḷ tam kuḻuvum – groups of wise sages such as sanaka et al, with clear knowledge (that śrīman nārāyaṇa is the supreme lord for us all); undi – crowding each other; tisai tisaiyil – in all the directions that one could see; malar tūvi senṟu sērum – strewing flowers and approaching; kaḷir malar sēr poḻil arangattu – in śrī rangam surrounded by gardens which have flowers which overflow with honey; kadal vaṇṇar – having the form of an ocean; kamalak kaṇṇum – having lotus-like eyes; oḷi madi sēr tirumugamum – having a divine face which resembles moon with cool rays; kaṇḍu koṇḍu – vorśipping \[such emperumān\]; en uḷḷan – my mind; miga urugum nāḷ enṟu kolō – when will it melt fully?

**Перевод:**

**emperumān is reclining on top of ādiśēśan in śrī rangam, which is surrounded by gardens where flowers are overflowing with honey. Such emperumān has the form of an ocean, lotus-like eyes and a divine face which resembles moon with cool rays. He is vorśipped by barhmā, Sivan, various celestial entities including indran, celestial women such as rambā, wise sages such as sanaka et al, who come from all the sides, strewing flower all along the way, as they approach śrī rangam. When will I worship such emperumān such that my mind will melt fully?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār longs to worship emperumān such that his mind will melt.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**aḷi malarmēl ayan aran indiranōdu** – brahmā who is sitting on top of a lotus flower which is swamped by beetles, together with his son Siva and indra who is mentioned along with these two in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 “*sa brahmā sa Siva sēndra*:” (brahmā, Siva and indra are possessions of śrīman rārāyaṇan)

**ēnai amarargaḷ tam kuḻuvum** – the groups of other celestial entities, apart from brahmā, Sivan and indran

**arambaiyarum** – celestial women such as rambā et al

**maṟṟum teḷi madi sēr munivargaḷ tam kuḻuvum** – jīvātmās are classified as *brahmabhāvanā niśtar* (those who are deeply engaged with the supreme being), *karmabhāvanā niśtar* (those who are deeply engaged with carrying out their *karma* (activities appropriate to their past virtues and sins) and *ubhayabhāvanā niśtar* (those who are engaged with both supreme being and their *karma*). Until now, āḻvār had spoken about *karmabhāvanā niśtar* and *ubhaya bhāvanā niśtar*. Now he is speaking about those who are deeply engrossed with the supreme being alone – sages such as sanaka, sanandana, sanātana, sanatkumāra et al, who have clear knowledge (about supreme being).

**undi** – all these entities are fighting with each other to take precedence over the rest (in approaching emperumān).

**tisai tisaiyin malar tūvic cenṟu sērum** – they keep strewing flowers on all the sides and are approaching śrī rangam

**kaḷi malar sēr poḻil arangattu uragam ēṟi** – getting on top of tiruvanantāḻvān inside the temple at śrī rangam, which is surrounded by gardens where beetles are swarming on flowers

**kaṇ vaḷarum kadal vaṇṇar kamalakkaṇṇum** – one who is reclining in a divine posture, having the complexion of ocean and having lotus-like eyes

**oḷi madi sēr tirumugamum kaṇḍu koṇḍu** – having darśan of the divine face which has the splendour and coolness of moon

**en uḷḷam miga enṟu kolō urugu nāḷē** – when will my mind melt (after seeing such emperumān)?

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 1.7. Maram tigalum

*Word-by-vord-Meaningsmaṟam tigaḻum – full of qualities suc as murder, anger, crueltymanam oḻittu – removing from te mind, vit teir tracesvanjam māṟṟi – getting rid of falsehoodaim pulangaḷ adakki – controlling te five senses suc tat tey do not enter vorldly pursuitsidar bāram tunbam tuṟandu – getting rid of te burden of karma (results of virtues and vices from previous birts) vhic engender sorrovirumuppḻodu ētti – vorśipping five times in a dayellai illāt tol neṟīkkaṇ nilai ninṟa – not slipping from te ancient limits vhic are countless toṇḍarāna – being follovers (of emperumān)aṟam tigaḻum manattavar tam – for śrīvaiṣṇavas (follovers of emperumān) vho have compassionate mindsgadiyai – te distinguiśed goalponni aṇi arangattu – in beautiful śrī rangam vhic is surrounded by te river kāvēriaravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum – one vho is reclining on ādiśēśanniṟam tigaḻum – one vho is beautifulmāyōnaik kaṇḍu – to get to vorśip te amazing entityen kangaḷ nīr malga – vit my eyes śedding tears of joyniṟkum nāḷ enṟu kolō – vhen vill ī stand?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Simple Translation emperumān is lying atop ādiśēśan, inside the beautiful śrī rangam which is surrounded by the river kāvēri. He has a beautiful form and is an amazing entity. He grants superior refuge to his followers who have removed from their minds evil qualities such as murder, anger, cruelty, falsehood etc, along with their traces, who have controlled their senses such that they do not graze on worldly pursuits, who have got rid of karma which is a huge burden resulting in sorrow, who have not slipped from the good paths of yore, who have compassion towards others and who worship emperumān five times in a day. When will I stand in front of that emperumān and worship him, with tears of joy in my eyes?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār longs to look at emperumān, with tears in his eyes. maṟam tigaḻum manam oḻittu vanja māṟṟi    aim pulangaḷ adakki idar bārat tunbamturandu irumuppoḻudu ētti ellai illāt    tol neṟikkaṇ nilai ninṟa toṇḍarānaaṟam tigaḻum manattavar tam gadiyaip ponni    aṇi arangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷumniṟam tigaḻum māyōnaik kaṇḍu en kaṇgaḷ    nīr malga enṟu kolō niṟku nāḷē*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maṟam tigaḻum manam oḻittu** – the term *maṟam* refers to qualities such as murder, anger, cruelty etc towards someone. Getting rid of these qualities, with their traces, from the mind.

**vanjam mārri** – getting rid of falsehood. Through these two verses, āḻvār has spoken about getting rid of bad qualities from the mind. Next, he speaks about controlling the shortcomings in the five senses.

**aim pulangaḷ adakki** – periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-2-3 says “*vanpulac cēvai adakki*” – controlling five senses which are like strong bulls which stray into all lands for grazing.

**idarp pārat tunbam tuṟandu** – amalanādipirān 5 says “*bāramāya paḻavinai paṟṟaṟuttu*” (severing connection with the dreadful load of old sins). Old deeds (both virtuous and sinful) always create sorrow. The opinion expressed here is that we should get rid of our connection with all the old deeds and overcome all the bad qualities mentioned above with the mercy of emperumān.

**iru muppoḻudu ētti** – A day is divided into five parts – *upādānam, abhigamanam, ijjyā, svādyāyam and yōgam* – starting from around 4 in the morning, each portion is of roughly of 2 ½ hours duration. During these times, keep praying to emperumān appropriately, as *pancakāla pārāyaṇar* (vorśipping emperumān on five occasions). Alternatively, a day is considered as morning, afternoon and evening. This will be *muppoḻudu* (three occasions). *irumuppoḻudu* will mean ‘the great three occasions’ and the meaning for this verse will be – praying to emperumān during the great three times – in other words, vorśipping him constantly.

**ellaiyillāt tonnerikkaṇ nilai ninṟa toṇḍarāna** – śrī vaiṣṇavas, who cannot be afflicted by anyone when they are engaged with the customary, ancient method of carrying out *tiruvaradanam* (divine worship of emperumān).

**aṟam tigaḻum manattavar tam gadiyai** *–* one who is considered as the supreme refuge by these (śrī vaiṣṇavas) who show mercy towards others as their basic nature.

**ponni aṇi arangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum** – one who is reclining on top of the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān inside śrī rangam which is surrounded on all sides by the river kāvēri, just as it has been mentioned in tiruccanda viruttam 55 “*ponni sūḻ arangam*” (śrī rangam which is surrounded by kāvēri)

**aṇi arangam** – for this samsāra maṇḍalam, also called as leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm), śrī rangam is like a decorative jewel.

**niṟam tigaḻum māyōnaik kaṇḍu** – looking at the amazing entity who is beauty personified. By the term *niṟam tigaḻum*, we can say that the quality ‘beauty’ too becomes great because it is holding on to him. The term *māyōn* refers to one who has amazing activities.

**en kaṇgaḷ nīr malga enṟu kolō niṟku nāḷē** – when will I see his beautiful and divine form, feel ecstatic and shed tears of joy?

We will take up the 8th pāsuram of this padigam, next.

## 1.8. Kolarnda nedum sarngam

*kōlārnda nedum sārngam kūnaṟcangam    kolaiyāḻi kodundaṇḍu koṟṟa oḷ vāḷkālārnda gatikkarudan ennum venṟik    kadum paṟavai ivai anaittum puṟam sūḻ kāppasēlārnda nedungaḻani sōlai sūḻnda    tiruvarangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷummālōnaik kaṇḍu inbak kalavi eydi    valvinaiyēn enṟu kolō vāḻu nāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kōl ārnda – being together with arrows; nedum sārngam – \[emperumān’s\] bow, śrī śārngam, which appears huge; kūn nal sangam – śrī pāncajanyam, the divine conch, which is bent because of the pride attained due to its experience with emperumān; kolai āḻi – tiruvāḻi āḻvān (divine disc, called as sudarśan) which annihilates enemies; kodum taṇḍu – the mace kaumōdakī, which creates fear among enemies; koṟṟam oḷ vāḷ – the sword, nandakam, which is splendorous in its victories over enemies; kāl ārnda kadum gati – having fierce speed just like the speed of wind; garudan ennum – called as garudan; venṟi paṟavai – pakśirājan (the king among birds) who is ever victorious; ivai anaittum – all these; puṟam sūḻ kāppa – while they protect, surrounding on all sides; sēl ārnda nedum kaḻani – with fields filled with fish; sōlai sūḻnda – surrounded by gardens; mālōnai – emperumān who is supreme; valvinaiyēn – I, who had committed great sins; kaṇḍu inbam kalavi eydi – vorśipping him and attaining unmatched bliss; vāḻum nāḷ enṟu kolō – when will I uplift myself?

**Перевод:**

**emperumān, who is lying atop ādiśēśan inside the huge temple in śrī rangam is protected on all sides by his divine weapons: the bow, śrī śārngam, which is huge and is together with arrows, the divine conch, śrī pāncajanyam, which is curved due to pride of being associated with emperumān constantly, the divine disc, sudarśan, which annihilates enemies, the mace, kaumōdakī, which creates fear among enemies and the sword, nandakam, which glitters in its victorious deeds. He is also protected by garudan, the king among birds, who has the speed of wind and is always victorious. śrī rangam, is surrounded by fields which are full of fish, and is also surrounded by gardens. When will I, who had committed great sins, worship that emperumān, who is supreme, and attain unmatched bliss, uplifting myself?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār longs to see emperumān, attain great happiness and sustain himself.*

**kōlārnda nedum sārngam** – The term *kōl* refers to arrows. śārngam is the long bow, together with its arrows. Since the bow is always together with arrows, the opinion expressed here is that emperumān holds the bow in a ready status, in order to destroy enemies. Just as it has been mentioned by nammāḻvār in periya tiruvandādi pāsuram 87, “*eppōdum kai kaḻalā nēmiyān*” (being ever ready to use, he holds the disc in his hand, always).

**kūn nal sangam** – due to the pride of tasting the nectar-like saliva of emperumān always, śrī pāncajanyam, the divine conch, appears to be bent in obedience. The term *nal sangam* shows that it has lot of *bhagavad anubhavam* (being always involved with emperumān)

**kolai āḻi** – beginning with this weapon, four weapons are described here. Since they do not involve with emperumān like pāncajanyam but are engaged for killing enemies, they do not carry the adjective *nal* (good).

**kolai āḻi** – the opinion here is that sudarśan, the divine disc, spends all the time available, by splitting the enemies into two parts.

**kodum taṇḍu** – the way emperumān is holding his mace, kaumōdakī, will create fear among his enemies and they will tremble and fall down.

**koṟṟa oḷ vāḷ** – the divine sword, nāndakam, manifests the opulence of emperumān viz, he is the emperor for all the worlds. *koṟṟa* refers to being constantly victorious.

**kālārnda gadi garudan ennum venṟīk kadum paṟavai** – having the power to fly at the speed of a cyclone, having the divine name of garudan (periya tiruvadi, the senior divine feet) \[when only the term *tiruvadi* is used, it refers to hanuman; the adjective periya is added to differentiate between the two\], he exhibits harshness towards enemies. ḍidn’t nammāḻvār too mention mercifully in his tiruvāīmoḻi 2-6-8 “*pāṟippāṟi asurartam pal kuḻāngaḷ nīṟeḻa pāy paṟavai onṟu …*” (various groups of demons were blown away to ashes by garudan who was attacking)!

**ivai anaittum puṟam sūḻ kāppa** – śrī rāmāyaṇa yudda kāṇḍam 25-33 ślōkam says “*rāma lakśmaṇa guptā sa sugrīvēṇa ca vāhinī* I *babhūva dūrdarśatarā sēndrairapi surāsurai*: īī” (The army of monkeys was protected by śrī rāma, lakśmaṇa as well as by sugrīva. Even armies of celestial entities led by indra and armies of demons cannot oppose this army of monkeys). Just as śrī rāma was protecting, along with lakśmaṇa, the army of monkeys on the shores of lankā, periya perumāḷ, who was vorśipped by that śrī rāma, was protected by periya tiruvadi (garuda) and the five divine weapons, since periya perumāḷ was reclining on the mattress of ādiśēśan.

**sēl aṟnda nedum kaḻani sōlai sūḻnda tiruvarangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum** – the huge temple in śrī rangam is surrounded by fields which are filled with fish; gardens also surround the temple. emperumān is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān and is greater than everyone else; *māl* – great. iḷangōvadigaḷ, who composed the tamiḻ epic silappadigāram, says “*periyavanai māyavanaip pērulagamellām viri kamal undi udai viṇṇavanai*” (emperumān, who is great, who is an amazing entity, who has a divine navel which is like a fully blossomed lotus and who is the lord of nityasūris).

**kaṇḍu inbak kalavi eydi** – being together with periya perumāḷ and attaining unmatched bliss

**val vinaiyēn enṟu kolō vāḻu nāḷē** – one has to leave this samsāram (materialistic realm), attain nitya vibhūti (spiritualistic realm) which is totally different from samsāram, in order to have darśan of emperumān. However, that supreme entity is reclining here itself, in nearby śrī rangam. āḻvār says “I have done so many sins. When will I get to see that emperumān and uplift myself?” His ministers were preventing him from proceeding to śrī rangam since he was the king.  Could he refer to carrying out his regal duties as sins? The opinion here is that any activity which prevents one from experiencing emperumān is a sinful activity only.

In the next article, we will take up the 9th pāsuram of this padigam.

## 1.9. Turada manakkadal

*tūrāda manak kādal toṇḍar tangaḷ    kuḻām kuḻumit tiruppugaḻgaḷ palavum pādiārāda manakkaḷippōdu aḻuda kaṇṇīr    maḻai sōra ninaindurugi ētti nāḷumsīrārnda muḻavōsai paravai kāttu    tiruvaragattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷumpōrāḻi ammānaik kaṇḍu tuḷḷip    pūdalattil enṟu kolō puraḷu nāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tūrāda kādal manam – having a mind which desires, without any shortcoming (in matters relating to emperumān); toṇḍar tangaḷ kuḻām – in the assembly of śrī vaiṣṇavas; tiruppugaḻgaḷ palavum pādi – reciting the divine names (of emperumān) appropriate to his auspicious qualities; ārāda manakkaḷippōdu – with the insatiable happiness (due to that) in the mind; aḻuda kaṇ nīr – with tears of joy shed from the eyes; maḻai sōra – growing like a rainfall; ninaindu urugi ētti – thinking (of emperumān) (and due to that), praising him with a melting heart; nāḷum – every day; sīr ārnda muḻavu ōsai – sound from musical instruments (an indication of opulence); paravai kāttum – will point out to the sound from ocean; tiruvarangattu paḷḷi koḷḷum – one who is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān inside śrī rangam; pōr āḻi ammānai – sarvēśvaran (lord of all) who holds the divine disc which is ever ready for battles; kaṇḍu – vorśipping him; tuḷḷi – (due to that joy) dancing, head over heels; bhūdalattil puraḷum nāḷ – the day that I roll over, on the earth; enṟu kolō – when is it?

**Перевод:**

**āḻvār says that he wishes to be in the assembly of śrī vaiṣṇavas, reciting the divine names of emperumān, appropriate to each of his auspicious qualities, with an insatiable happiness in his mind. He wishes to go to śrī rangam, listen to the music from various instruments, with the sound being similar to the sound from an ocean, with tears of joy flowing from his eyes, which will increase to a rainfall. He wishes to dance in joy, and roll on the floor in that happiness. He is wondering as to when all these would happen.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār longs to see emperumān in śrī rangam, dance joyfully and roll on the ground in ecstasy.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tūrāda manakkādal toṇḍar tangaḷ kuḻām kuḻumi** – nammāḻvār in his tiruvāimoḻi pāsuram 5-8-6 had said “*tūrakkuḻi tūrttu enai nāḷ aganṟiruppan*” lamenting that he was like samsāris (dwellers in materialistic realm), not satiated by his senses, despite experiencing through the five senses of *śabda* (hearing), *sparśa* (touching), *rūpa* (seeing) *rasa* (tasting) and *ganda* (smelling) continuously, for a long time. kulaśēkhara āḻvār says in this pāsuram that he wishes to be in the assembly of śrī vaiṣṇavas, who are not satiated despite repeatedly experiencing emperumān.

**tiruppugaḻgaḷ palavum pādi** – reciting the many divine names of that emperumān which denote each of his auspicious qualities.

**ārāda manakkkaḷippōdu aḻuda kaṇṇīr maḻai sōra** – reciting his divine names brought insatiable happiness to the mind, leading to tears of joy which turned into a flood.

**ninaindurugi ētti** – the act of reciting the divine names of emperumān in the assembly of śrī vaiṣṇavas made the heart to melt, thinking of emperumān. Hence, those who saw this would say “words which came out, due to melting of the heart”. Worśipping this say…

**nāḷum sīrārnda muḻavōsai paravai kāttum tiruvarangattaravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum pōrāḻi ammānaik kaṇḍu** – The sound of musical instruments played in the temple, which would appear to say “He is the possessor of the wealth of both leelā vibhūti and nitya vibhūti”, would remind one of the sound of an ocean. Worśipping such emperumān who is reclining on the mattress of ādiśēśan and holding the divine disc, which is ever ready to wage a battle….

**tuḷḷi** – just as nammāḻvār had said in tiruvāīmoḻi 3-5-4 “*kumbidu nattamittādi*” (bowing down and dancing), dancing  head over heels.

**bhūdalattil enṟu koḷo puraḷum nāḷē** – āḻvār says “When will I forget sitting on the throne with pride and roll down on earth in great joy?”

We will take up next, the 10th pāsuram in this padigam.

## 1.10. Perumal tirumoli-1.10 van peru vanagam

*van peru vānagam uyya amarar uyya    maṇṇuyya maṇṇulagil manisar uyyāttunbamigu tuyar agala ayarvonṟu illāc    cugam vaḷara agamagiḻum toṇḍar vāḻaanbodu ten tisai nōkkip paḷḷi koḷḷum    aṇiyarangan tirumuṟṟattu adiyār tangaḷinbamigu perum kuḻuvu kaṇḍu yānum    isaindudanē enṟu kolō irukku nāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

val – not destroyed (even during naimittika (that which happens for a cause) deluge); peru vānagam uyya – for the ethereal worlds such as satyalōkam (where brahmā resides) to get uplifted; amarar uyya – for celestial entities (such as brahmā et al) to get uplifted; maṇ uyya – for earth to get uplifted; maṇṇulagil manisar uyya – for the cētanas (jīvātmās) who were born on earth, to get uplifted; migu tunbam tuyar agala – for sins, which create deep sorrow, to disappear; ayarvu onṟu illā sugam vaḷara – for joy, which does not have any trace of sorrow, to grow; agam magiḻum – having the joy (resulting from engagement with bhagavat anubhavam (matters relating to emperumān)); toṇḍar vāḻa – for śrī vaiṣṇavas to prosper; anbodu – with affection (towards vibhīśaṇāḻvān); then disai nōkki – looking towards the southern side; paḷḷi koḷḷum – one who is reclining; aṇi arangan tiru muṟṟattu – in the divine courtyard inside śrī rangam temple; migu inbam – blissful; adiyār tangaḷ – śrī vaiṣṇavas’; perum kuḻuvu kaṇḍu – looking at the huge assembly; yānum isaindu – I too, being together with them; udanē irukkum nāḷ – vorśipping with them; enṟu kolō – when is that day

**Перевод:**

**emperumān is reclining inside the temple in śrī rangam, in order to uplift (a) ethereal worlds such as satyalōkam etc, (b) all the celestial entities (such as brahmā et al) who are living in those words, (c) earth, (d) all the cētanas who are living on earth; he is lying, looking in the southern direction, in order to make sins which create huge sorrow, to disappear, to nurture joy, without any trace of sorrow, so that his followers, the śrī vaiṣṇavas, can prosper, having the joy which results from their attachment towards emperumān. When will I be together with such śrī vaiṣṇavas, inside the divine courtyard of that śrī rangam temple and worship emperumān?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār is longing to be in the assembly of śrī vaiṣṇavas in the divine courtyard of the śrī rangam temple.*

**van peru vānagam uyya amarar uyya** – worlds such as *brahmalōkam* (the place where brahmā resides) are such strong that even during *naimittika praḷayam* (deluge which happens during every *catur yugam* (four epochs which together account for 43,20,000 years in our measurement)) they remain unaffected. For uplifting these worlds and the celestial entities who live in such worlds. In śrīranga mahātmyam (great narration about śrī rangam), it has been written that brahmā and other celestial entities in those worlds had vorśipped śrī ranganātan, the presiding deity in śrī rangam under praṇavākāra vimānam (the canopy over the garbhagraham), in that world \[before he was brought to earth by king ikśvāku\].

**maṇṇuyya maṇṇulagil manisaruyya** – from there, he came down to the earth, being vorśipped by kings of ikśvāku clan, until the time of śrī rāma. Later, śrī rāma, as a token of his gratitude, gave periya perumāḷ to vibhīśaṇāḻvān. perumāḷ decided to stay in śrī rangam, as brought out by periyāḻvār in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi pāsuram 4-9-2 “*mannudaiya vibidaṇaṟkā madiḷilangai tisainōkki malarkkaṇ vaitta en tiruvarangar*” (periya perumāḷ turned his face towards the southern side, looking, with his lotus-like eyes, at the fortified lankā for the sake of vibhīśaṇa, who was opulent). Thus, showering his grace on vibhīśaṇāḻvān, emperumān came from ayōdyā to śrī rangam so that this world and all those who live here, would get uplifted.

**tunbamigu tuyar agala** – in order to eliminate the sins which the jīvātmās (those who live in samsāram) endure, and which are the reason for their sorrow.

**ayarvonṟillāc cugam vaḷara** – in order for the jīvātmās to lead a life in joy, which does not have even a trace of any sorrow

**agamagiḻum toṇḍar vāḻa** – for śrī vaiṣṇavas, who attain great bliss only by experiencing emperumān, to get uplifted

**anbodu then disai nōkkip paḷḷi koḷḷum aṇiyarangan tirumuṟṟattu** – in the divine courtyard of the temple in śrī rangam inside which periya perumāḷ is reclining, looking in the direction of lankā where vibhīśaṇāḻvān is residing , after being crowned as the king there.

**aṇiyarangan** – the beautiful śrī rangam which periya perumāḷ has taken as his dwelling place.

**adiyār tangaḷ inbamigu perunguḻuvu kaṇḍu yānum isaindu** – \[kulaśēkhara āḻvār says\] ‘Not considering myself as a king who has been crowned but considering myself as one among them’

**udanē enṟu kolō irukkum nāḷē** – getting rid of the status where I am the king and my subjects come to worship me, when will I get into the assembly of śrī vaiṣṇavas when I can worship them and periya perumāḷ?

It is pertinent here to recall the following ślōkam from śrī ranga mahātmyam which says that periya perumāḷ reclined in śrī rangam to uplift all the people:

*bhuvō bhūtyai bhūbhujām bhūsurāṇām*  
*divō guptyai śrēyasē dēvatānām* l  
*śriyai rājyām cōḷavamśōdbhavānām*  
*śrīmadrangam sahyajāmajagāma*  īī

(In order to uplift earth, kings who rule the earth and brāhmaṇas, in order to protect the world of celestial entities, for the benefit of dēvatās (heads of celestial entities) and in order to bestow inseparable opulence to the kings in the clan of cōḻas, the śrī ranga vimānam (turret of the sanctum sanctorum having periya perumāḷ) reached the banks of the divine river kāvēri)

We will take up the last pāsuram of this padigam, in the next article.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 1.11. Tidar vilangu karaip ponni

*tidar viḷangu karaipponni naduvu pāttut    tiruvarangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷumkadal viḷangu karumēni ammān tannaik    kaṇṇārak kaṇḍu ugakkum kādal tannālkudai viḷangu viṟaltānaik koṟṟa oḷ vāḷ    kūdalar kōn kodaikkulasēkaran soṟceydanadai viḷangu tamiḻ māḷai pattum vallār    nalam tigaḻ nāraṇan adikkīḻ naṇṇuvārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tidar viḷangu karai – having bank with mounds \[of sand\] visible; tiruvarangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum – reclining atop ādiśēśan, in śrī rangam; kadal viḷangu karumēni ammān tannai – sarvēśvaran (lord of all) who resembles an ocean, removing the ennui of the beholder; kaṇ Ara kaṇḍu ugakkum kādal tannāl – with the desire “should see with the eyes and enjoy”; kudai viḷangu – displaying the white canopy of royalty (signifying that he is the king); viral tānai – having a victorious army; koṟṟam oḷ vāḷ – having a sword which is effulgent and is always victorious; kūdalar kōn – being the chief of madurai (having won over pāṇḍiya king); kodai kulasēkaran – kulaśēkharap perumāḷ who has magnanimity without any shortcoming; soṟceyda – mercifully composed; nadai viḷangu – shining due to its style; tamiḻ mālai pattum vallār – those who can recite these ten pāsurams written in tamiḻ; nalam tigaḻ nāraṇan – periya perumāḷ who has all the overflowing auspicious qualities; adikkīḻ naṇṇuvārē – will attain the divine feet

**Перевод:**

**emperumān is reclining on top of ādiśēśan inside the śrī rangam temple, which is amidst the river kāvēri, having mounds on its bank. He has a divine form which resembles an ocean, removing the ennui of those who behold it. kulaśēkhara āḻvār composed ten pāsurams on this emperumān with the desire to see him and enjoy him with his eyes. āḻvār has a victorious army, a white canopy, a shining sword which is always victorious and is magnanimous, without any shortcoming. Those who can recite these ten pāsurams, composed in tamiḻ, which shine due to their style, will attain the divine feet of emperumān who has overflowing auspicious qualities.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār recounts the benefits which will accrue to one who is able to recite this padigam and brings it to an end.*

**tidar viḷangu karaipponni naduvu pāttu** – shining in the midst of river kāvēri, which has mounds of sand visible on its bank

**tiruvarangattu aravaṇaiyil paḷḷi koḷḷum kadal viḷangu karumēni ammān tannai** – one who is reclining on top of ādiśēśan inside periya kōyil (the big temple) in śrī rangam, one who has a bluish divine form, similar to that of an ocean, removing the ennui of those who look at it, his

**kaṇṇārak kaṇḍugakkum kādal tannāl** – with the desire to see and enjoy his divine form

**kudai viḷangu viral tānaik koṟṟa oḷ vāḷ kūdalar kōn** – one who has the white canopy \[umbrella\] signifying royalty, one who has victorious army, one who has a sword displaying wealth, one who is the chief of madurai which is the capital city of pāṇḍiya nādu (region of pāṇḍiya kings)

**kodaikkulasēkaran soṟseyda** – mercifully composed by kulaśēkharap perumāḷ who gives everything, magnanimously

**nadai viḷangu tamiḻ mālai pattum vallār** – those who recite these ten pāsurams which are like a garland and have been composed such that one need not analyse the underlying meanings of words, one need not look at the beauty of how the words have been strung together, where the pāsurams themselves attract hearts towards them

**nalam tigaḻ nāraṇan adikkīḻ naṇṇuvārē** – \[the people mentioned in the previous verse\] will get to approach, as they desired, the divine feet of periya perumāḷ, who has the auspicious qualities of *sauśeelyam* (quality of a superior person being affable with an inferior person), *saulabhyam* (being easy to approach) etc, as well as the qualities such as *sarvasvāmitvam* (lord of all) etc, who has *vātsalyam* (motherly forbearance which treats faults too as desirable quality) towards his followers, and will get to carry out *kainkaryam* (service) to his divine feet. *nāraṇan* – one who manifests the quality of *vātsalyam* towards his followers.

īnte next article, we will take up the *pravēśam* (introduction) for the second decad.

## 2.1. Tettarum tiral teninai

*tēttaram tiṟal tēninait ten aranganait tirumādu vāḻvāttamil vanamālai mārvanai vāḻtti māl koḷ sindaiyarāyāttamēvi alandaḻaittu ayarveydum meyyadiyārgaḷ tamīttam kaṇḍidak kūdumēl adu kāṇum kaṇ payan āvadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tiṟal – one who gives sufficient strength to experience himself; tēninai – one who is most enjoyable, like honey; then aranganai – one who dwells permanently in the beautiful śrī rangam; tiru mādu vāḻ – being appropriate for periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) to dwell permanently; vāttam il vanamālai mārvanai – emperumān who has donned on his divine chest, the divine garland which is beautiful and is always fresh; vāḻtti – praising them (so that there will be no ‘evil eye’ in their union); māḷ koḷ sindaiyarāy – having a mind which is affectionate towards emperumān; due to that; āttam mēvi – engaging in dance; alandu – feeling distressed (due to excessive dancing); aḻaittu – reciting (the divine names of bhagavān, as an outlet for having been provided tongue); ayarvu eydum – becoming oblivious; mey adiyārgaḷ tam – followers who do not desire other benefits (than bhagavān); īttam kaṇḍidak kūdumēl – if we are able to see the assemblies; adu kāṇum kaṇ payan āvadē – is that not the benefit of having been provided with eyes?

**Перевод:**

**Is it not the benefit of having been provided with eyes if we are able to see the assemblies of emperumān’s followers who are praising emperumān, who is difficult to seek and attain, who gives sufficient strength to (them to) enjoy him, who is enjoyable like honey, who resides permanently in śrī rangam, who is appropriate for periya pirātti to dwell on his divine chest on which he has donned the beautiful and ever fresh garland? His followers have a mind which is affectionate towards him; due to this affection, they engage in dance; feeling distressed due to excessive dancing, they keep reciting his divine names, becoming oblivious to everything else; they do not desire any benefit other than emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān’s followers become desirable people for us, merely because of the fact that they are deeply engaged with his auspicious qualities. When a person among samsāris (dwellers of materialistic realm) has lot of wealth and lot of properties, many take refuge under him. In a similar way, merely because they are engaged with emperumān, others take refuge under such followers. When āḷavandār (grandson of śrīman nātamunigal and one of our important preceptors) set out to praise emperumān (in his prabandam, stōtra ratnam), he started by praising his ācāryar nātamunigaḷ in the first ślōkam: namōçhintyādbhutākliśta jñāna vairājña rāśayē lnātāya munayē’gāda bhagavad bhakti sindavē    ll (I bow down to nātamunigaḷ who is a treasure of knowledge and detachment, which are beyond one’s comprehension (that they are like this), which are amazing and which he attained easily (with the mercy of bhagavān). He was constantly meditating on bhagavān and he was an ocean of deep devotion towards bhagavān). Did not āḷavandār praise nātamunigaḷ’s distinguished knowledge and devotion? In the same way, kulaśēkhara āḻvār too says that the purpose of his having vision is only to look at the assembly of the followers of śrī ranganātan.*

**tēttarum….** – emperumān is such an entity that no one can see him, with one’s own efforts unless he shows himself up. Just as it has been mentioned in yajur vēdam, kātakam 7-5-35 *ya ātmadā baladā* (that supreme entity who offers himself to his followers and gives them the strength to experience him), he offers himself and gives his followers the strength to enjoy him He is also sweet, like honey.

**tēninait then aranganait tirumāduvāḻ vāttamil vanamālai mārvanai vāḻtti** – he constantly resides in śrī rangam, which is desired by everyone. On the divine chest where his consort, periya pirāttiyār dwells, he has a divine garland which not only does not wither away, just because he is donning it, but also becomes more and more fresh as time goes by. Just as it has been mercifully mentioned in tiruppaḷḷāṇḍu 2 “*vadivāy nin valamārbinil vāḻginṟa mangaiyum pallāṇḍu*” (long live your divine consort who dwells beautifully on your right chest), praising him on his being together with her

**māl koḷ sindaiyarāy** – having a mind which is deeply affectionate towards him; *māl* – deep love.

**āttam mēvi** – without the thought of having to dance, not being able to remain in the place where one is, engaging in dance.

**alandu aḻaittu** – becoming distressed due to excessive dancing, reciting his divine names in order to reap the benefit of having been provided a tongue.

**ayarvu eydum meyyadiyārgaḷ tam** – his followers, not waiting patiently with the thought ‘let us wait for the appropriate time when he calls us’, losing their knowledge, becoming oblivious to everything around them, engage with him, without looking for any benefit from him other than emperumān himself.

When asked “How many such people would you like to see?” āḻvār says

**īttam kaṇḍida** – he desires to see huge assemblies of such followers.

**kūdumēl adu kāṇum kaṇ payanāvadē** – if one could see such assemblies, there cannot be any benefit for the eyes better than this.

**kāṇum payan** – instead of waiting for the benefit in śrī vaikuṇṭam, āḻvār says that this is the benefit that he needs in this birth itself.

We will take up the 2nd pāsuram in the next article.

## 2.2. Todula malarmangai

*tōdulā malar mangai tōḷiṇai tōyndadum sudar vāḷiyālnīdu māmaram seṟṟadum nirai mēyttadum ivaiyē ninainduādippādi arangavō enṟu aḻaikkum toṇḍar adippodiāda nām peṟil gangai nīr kudaindu ādum vētkai ennāvadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōdu ulām malar mangai – periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi), who has as her residence, the lotus flower which has abundant petals; tōḷ iṇai tōyndadum – embracing her two divine shoulders; sudar vāḷiyāl – with arrow having splendour; nīdu māmaram seṟṟadum – piercing the distinguished seven trees; nirai mēyttadum – grazing herds of cows; ivaiyē ninaindu – recalling these activities; ādi pādi – dancing and singing, with overflowing joy; O aranga enṟu aḻaikkum – calling out “aranganē”; toṇḍar – śrī vaiṣṇavas, who are his followers, their; adippodi – on the fine particles of dust present on their feet; nām Ada peṟil – if we are able to engage; gangai nīr – in the waters of river gangā; kudaindu ādum vētkai – the desire to plunge and have a bath; en āvadu – for what purpose?

**Перевод:**

**śrī vaiṣṇavas, the followers of emperumān, keep recalling the amazing activities of emperumān such as (1) embracing the divine shoulders of periya pirātti who dwells on a lotus flower with abundant petals, (2) piercing seven huge trees with a single arrow which was splendorous, (3) grazing herds of cows etc. Due to overflowing joy, they dance and sing, calling out to emperumān as “Oh rangā!” If we are able to engage with the fine dust particles sticking to their feet, will it not be greater than having a dip in the pious waters of river gangā?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān’s followers recall his divine activities during his incarnation as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al and dance, calling out his divine name “rangā! rangā!” āḻvār says that having a dip in the pious river gangā is not greater than being engaged with the divine dust at the feet of such followers.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tōdulā malar mangai tōḷiṇai tōyndadum** – embracing the two divine shoulders of periya pirātti who resides on a lotus flower which has abundance of petals. āḻvār hints that only emperumān has the capability to carry out such rare acts of swimming in a huge flood, embracing the divine shoulders of periya pirātti etc.

**sudar vāḷiyāl** – with a single arrow which was splendorous

**nīdu māmaram seṟṟadum** – shooting down seven huge trees which had grown tall. The underlying opinion here is that emperumān shot the arrow and felled the seven huge and strong trees as if the arrow went through holes which were already existing in these seven trees. Was this not an activity which emperumān carried out in order to bring in confidence in his devotee, sugrīva’s  mind to make him believe that he is capable of destroying his enemy vāli? Until now, emperumān’s greatness had been described. Next, he describes the simplicity of emperumān…

**nirai mēyttadum** – though he is the owner of both nitya vibhūti (śrī vaikuṇṭam) and leelā vibhūti (samsāram), emperumān incarnated as kriṣṇa, in the clan of herds-people and grazed cows, holding a stick in his hand.

**ivaiyē ninaindu** – meditating on these qualities of greatness and simplicity. That they think of nothing else other than these, since they do not desire any benefit, is emphasised by the long syllable in *ivaiyē* (the E-kāram, the long syllable in that word).

**ādippādi** – due to excessive joy, being unable to remain in the places where they were, they indulge in dancing and singing.

**arangavō enṟaḻaikkum** – due to the distress resulting from their desire to see emperumān, they call out the divine names of periya perumāḷ.

**toṇḍaradippodi Ada nām peṟil** – if we are able to dance in the fine dust coming out of the feet of those śrī vaiṣṇavas who were attracted by the auspicious qualities of emperumān. āḻvār desires to enter and dance in the dust of those śrī vaiṣṇavas who have constant connection with emperumān instead of entering and dancing in rivers such as gangā and kāvēri which had connection once upon a time with emperumān, since those are more pious than these rivers.

**gangai nīr kudaindādum vētkai ennāvadē** – what is the benefit in entering and dancing in the waters of gangā, even though it originated from the divine feet of emperumān, since it also entered the matted hair of people such as rudra (Sivan) et al and became contaminated? That gangā had connection with people such as rudra, brahmā et al has been brought out by pāsurams such as periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-7-2 “*nalam tigaḻ sadaiyān mudikkonṟai malarum nāraṇan pādattuḻāyum kalandiḻi punalāl pugarpadu gangai*” (the river gangā is splendorous as it falls from the sky towards earth due to its connection with the flower decorating the matted head of rudra, who himself is looking radiant (because he took on his head the water which touched the divine feet of emperumān) and with the tuḷasi flower on the divine feet of emperumān) and periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-7-3 “*cadumugan kaiyil caduppuyan tāḷil sangaran sadaiyinil tangi kadir mugamaṇi koṇḍu iḻipunal gangai*” (the river gangā is looking radiant and is flowing with distinguished gemstones since it has connection with the hands of brahmā, who has four faces, with the divine feet of emperumān, who has four divine shoulders and with the matted hair of rudra).

We will next take up the 3rd pāsuram.

## 2.3. Eradarttaddum

*ēṟadarttadum ēnamāy nilam kīṇḍadum mun irāmanāymāṟadarttadum maṇṇaḷandadum sollippādi vaṇ ponnippērāru pōl varum kaṇṇanīr koṇḍu arangan kōyil tirumuṟṟamsēṟu sey toṇḍar sēvadicceḻum sēṟu en sennikku aṇivanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēṟu adarttadium – (for the sake of nappinnaip pirātti) killing seven bulls; ēnamāy nilam kīṇḍadum – (for the sake of bhūmippirātti) as a huge, wild boar, digging the earth out with his tusks and releasing her; mun irāmanāy – incarnating as the son of daśarata cakravarti, when rāvaṇa separated pirātti (sītā) from him; māṟu adarttadum – killing rāvaṇa, the enemy; maṇ aḷandadum – (for the sake of indra (the lord of celestial entities), when mahābali seized his territories) measuring earth with three steps, as trivikrama; all these amazing activities; sollippādi – recounting to their hearts’ content, and singing these, as an outlet for their overflowing joy; vaṇ pēr ponni āru pōl varum – like river kāvēri who flows with a huge flood; kaṇṇa nīr koṇḍu – with tears from their eyes; arangan kōyil tirumuṟṟam – in the divine courtyard of śrī rangam temple; sēṟu sey toṇḍar – those who make as a muddied mass, such śrī vaiṣṇavas’; sē adi seḻu sēṟu – the beautiful mud which has been stomped upon by their divine feet; en sennikkku aṇivan – I will apply on my forehead

**Перевод:**

**The followers of emperumān, while being in the divine courtyard inside śrī rangam temple, recount the activities of emperumān during his several incarnations, such as (1) killing seven bulls (for marrying nappinnaip pirātti, during his kriṣṇāvatāram), (2) digging out bhūmippirātti from the walls of universe with his tusks, and releasing her (during his varāhāvatāram), (3) killing rāvaṇa, after being born as the son of daśarata cakravarti (during his rāmāvatāram), (4) measuring all the worlds with his three steps (during his trivikramāvatāram). As they recount these activities, they shed tears of joy from their eyes. These tears soon swell into a huge flood. Combined with the dust from their divine feet, this becomes a muddy mass. kulaśēkhara āḻvār desires to apply this mud on his forehead.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The followers of emperumān are recalling his amazing activities during his various incarnations such as varāha, trivikrama, śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al through their songs and conversation. While doing so, the tears of joy which swell in their eyes, come down as a flood. Added to the divine dust from their feet, this leads to muddied mass in the divine courtyard of śrī rangam temple. In this pāsuram, āḻvār desires to apply this mud, which has been stepped upon by the divine feet of followers, on his forehead.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ēṟadarttadum** – In order to win over the hand of nappinnaip pirātti, seven bulls were kept as a wager to show one’s strength. \[Incarnating as kriṣṇa\] emperumān killed these bulls.

**ēnamāy nilam kīṇḍadum** – when deluge had submerged bhūmippirātti fully, making it impossible for emperumān to converse with her, he incarnated as a huge wild boar, dug her out from the walls of the universe with his tusks, and released her.

**mun irāmanāy māṟadarttadum** – incarnating earlier as śrī rāma, he killed his enemy rāvaṇa when the demon separated sītāppirātti from him.

**maṇṇaḷandadum** – just as emperumān removed hurdles to unite with his consorts in these three incarnations, for the sake of his follower indra, he measured and got released the earth which was seized by mahābali, with his three steps in trivikrama avatāram.

**sollippādi** – recounting these amazing activities during the four incarnations, and as an outlet for their joy, singing

**van ponnip pērāṟu pōl varum kaṇṇa nīr koṇḍu** – just as tirumangai āḻvār says in periya tirumoḻi 5-4-1 “*sandinōdu maṇiyum koḻikkum punal kāviri*” (river kāvēri enriches herself with sandalwood and gemstones as she comes down from mountains), similar to the huge river kāvēri coming down from the sahyādri mountain, with the uncontrollable tears of joy which form a flood

**arangan kōyil tirumuṟṟam sēṟu sey toṇḍar** – while the divine courtyard of śrī rangam temple has been cleaned up to be appropriate for emperumān to come mercifully in procession, his followers make that place muddied with their tears.

**sēvadic cēṟu en sennikku aṇivanē** – people (the king’s servants) have decorated king kulaśēkharar’s head with fragrant materials. In order to get rid of the blemish in such inauspicious materials since they do not have any connection with bhāgavatas (śrī vaiṣṇavas, emperumān’s followers), kulaśēkhara āḻvār says that he will apply the auspicious mud, which has connection with the divine feet of śrī vaiṣṇavas, on his forehead.

We will take up the 4th pāsuram, next.

## 2.4. Toytta tan tayir

*tōytta taṇ tayir veṇṇey pāl udanuṇḍalum udanṟāycci kaṇḍuārtta tōḷudai empirān en aranganukku adiyārgaḷāynāttaḻumbu eḻa nāraṇā enṟu aḻaittu mey taḻumbat toḻuduētti inbuṟum toṇḍar sēvadi ētti vāḻttum en nenjamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōytta taṇ tayir – curd which had been set and cooled; veṇṇey – and butter; pāl – along with milk; udan uṇḍalum – after eating these simultaneously; āycci kaṇḍu – being caught red-handed by yaśōdāp pirātti; udanṟu – becoming angry; ārtta tōḷudai – (later) having his hands being tied by her; empirān – being my svāmy (lord); adiyārgaḷāy – being his servitors; nā taḻumbu eḻa – for the tongue to become scarred; nāraṇā enṟu aḻaittu – calling out ‘Oh nārāyaṇā’; mey taḻumba toḻudu – vorśipping him such that scars form on the body; ētti – praising him; inbuṟum – feeling blissful; toṇḍar sē adi – the divine feet of śrī vaiṣṇavas; en nenjam – my heart; ētti vāḻttum – will praise and carry out mangaḷāśāsanam (wish for their well-being)

**Перевод:**

**During his incarnation as kriṣṇa, emperumān had simultaneously eaten cooled and set curd, butter and milk. He was caught red-handed in such acts by his mother yaśōdāp pirātti, who became angry and tied him to a mortar. śervitors of such emperumān would call out to him, and worship him such that scars would form on their tongues (due to calling out his name) and on their bodies (due to vorśipping him constantly). āḻvār says that his heart will praise the divine feet of such followers and carry out mangaḷāśāsanam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that his heart will praise the divine feet of followers of emperumān who are servile to kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) when he was tied down to a mortar with tiny bits of ropes.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tōytta tayir veṇṇey pāl** – butter which had been churned from curd, curd which had been set (in order to take butter out of it) and milk, the causative material for curd

**udan uṇḍalum** – eating all these simultaneously such that no one else can eat them

**udanṟu** – when these materials disappeared, yaśōdāp pirātti became angry. Just as it has been said in siṟiya tirumadal “*tīrā veguḷiyaḷāyc cikkena vārttadippa*” (she tied him to a mortar and hit him with a rope, out of anger, which in turn was born out of her love for kriṣṇa), yaśōdā became very angry. Since she had unbounded affection towards him, she became angry thinking “What harm will eating all these, cause him?”

**āycci kaṇḍu** – Even as he was in the act of stealing all these and eating them, he was caught red-handed by yaśōdā.

**ārtta tōḷudai empirān** – As soon as she saw him in the act, yaśōdā tied his hands.

**empirān** – By manifesting his inability to sustain himself unless he ate the curd, butter and milk which had had the connection with his followers, he made me exist only for him.

**en aranganukku adiyārgaḷāy** – in order to remove the grievance of those who did not get to see him (and his activities) during his incarnation as kriṣṇa, he came as an epitome of simplicity and lay down in śrīrangam as periya perumāḷ so that everyone can enjoy his simplicity. Those who engaged themselves with him and became his followers, being servile to him.

**nāttaḻumbu eḻa** – such that their tongues became scarred

**nāraṇā enṟu aḻaittu** – just as a child will call out “Oh mother!” reciting the divine names of that emperumān, such as nārāyaṇan et al, even in a disorderly manner

**mey taḻumaba toḻudu** – due to prostrating before emperumān repeatedly, their physical forms will carry scars. Worśipping him in such a manner. peryacāccān piḷḷai had said that an ācāryar, known by the divine name *siṟiyāttān*, used to carry such scars on his physical form.

**ētti** – vorśipping, after recounting such activities \[of emperumān\]

**inbuṟum toṇḍar sēvadi** – his followers would normally worship emperumān by visualising him in their minds. When they get to see him inside the temple, their joy knows no bounds. The divine feet of such śrīvaiṣṇavas’

**toṇḍar sēvadi ētti vāḻttum en nenhamē** – \[āḻvār says\] my mind will not engage with the activity of stealing and eating butter which the minds of these śrī vaiṣṇavas would be engaging with. Instead, my mind will engage in praising and vorśipping such śrī vaiṣṇavas who engage with that emperumān and his activities.

**ētti vāḻttum** – my mind will praise these śrī vaiṣṇavas thinking “These śrī vaiṣṇavas are involved with the amazing activities of emperumān”. It will pray that this engagement with emperumān continues forever.

In the next article, we will take up the 5th pāsuram.

## 2.5. Poy silaikkural

*poysilaikkural ēṟṟu eruttam iṟuttup pōr aravam īrtta kōnseysilaiccudar sūḻoḷit tiṇṇa māmadiḷ tennaranganāmmeysilaikkarumēgam onṟu tam nenjil ninṟu tigaḻappōymey silirppavar tammaiyē ninanidu en manam mey silirkkumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

poy silai kural – making a deceitful and angry bellow; ēṟu eruttam iṟūttu – one who broke the humps (of seven bulls); pōr aravu – the snake kāḷiyan, who came to wage a war; īrtta kōn – one who drove away and became the head (of gōkulam); silai sey – made of stone; sudar oḷi – having radiance; tiṇ – having strength; mā – being tall; madiḷ sūḻ – surrounded by fortified walls; then aranganām – periya perumāḷ who is permanently residing in śrī rangam; mey silai karumēgam onṟu – a bluish cloud which has a bow on its form; tam nenjil ninṟu tigaḻappōy – to shine in their hearts; mey silirppavar tammaiyē – śrī vaiṣṇavas, who get goose bumps on their physical forms; en manam ninaindu – my heart will think about; mey silirkkum – will have goose bumps

**Перевод:**

**emperumān, during his incarnation as kriṣṇa, destroyed seven bulls, which were making a deceitful and angry bellow, by breaking their humps. He also drove away the snake, kāḷiyan, who came to wage a war and thus became the head of the clan of herd-people. He is now residing permanently inside śrīrangam, fortified by walls made of stone, which have a radiance and strength. śrīvaiṣṇavas think about such emperumān who is like a bluish cloud with a bow on its form and get goose bumps on their bodies. āḻvār says that he will get goose bumps, by thinking about those śrī vaiṣṇavas.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says that his heart will get goose bumps by looking at the followers of emperumān who get goose bumps by engaging with periya perumāḷ who had performed amazing activities when he incarnated as kriṣṇa.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**poy silaik kural ēṟṟam eruttam iṟuttu** – this verse should be split into two parts to know the meaning: *poyyāna ēṟu* and *silaikkural ēṟu*.The term *poy* means deceit. Since demons had entered the bulls, they had the deceit of causing harm to kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa). They were bellowing with deceitful anger. The term *silai* refers to anger. The seven bulls were a hurdle to marrying nappinnaip pirātti and were ready to wage a war \[on kaṇṇan\]. kaṇṇan broke the humps of the bulls which were emitting thunderous bellowing.

**pōr aravu īrtta kōn** – river yamunā in *tiruvāyppādi* (śrī gōkulam) was the main source of water for the herd as well as the herd-folks. kāḷiyan, the snake, had taken residence there, making the water poisonous and rendering yamunā, unfit for drinking. kriṣṇa waged a war on such kāḷiyan by entering the river and made him to run away. Hence, he became the leader of herd-folks.

**sey silaic cudar sūḻoḷit tiṇṇa māmadiḷ tennaranganām** – being made of stone, having the ability of not being won over by anyone in any battle due to its strength, having height, the fortified walls surrounded the town of śrī rangam. śrī ranganātan (periya perumāḷ) was reclining inside such temple.

**mey silaik karumēgam** – The term *mey* could be either taken as referring to the (physical) body, in which case the meaning for the verse would be: periya perumāḷ is like a bluish coloured cloud having bow in its body, or it (*mey*) could be taken as referring to truth, in which case the meaning would be: periya perumāḷ is truly like a bluish cloud, having a bow.

**onṟu tam nenjil ninṟu tigaḻappōy** – Just as it has been said in the śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 38-15 ślōkam *śōbhayan dandakāraṇyam dīptō svēna tējasā* l *adruśyata tatō rāmō bālacandra ivōdita*:  ll (śrī rāma appeared like a just risen full moon, decorating the entire daṇḍakāraṇyam (forest) with his effulgence), just as a blue gemstone kept inside a carbuncle box will emit its shine outside, the śrī vaiṣṇavas emit shine outside since they have kept periya perumāḷ inside themselves.

**mey silirppavar** – thinking constantly that they have mercifully kept śrī ranganātan inside, they will have goose bumps on their physical forms.

**tammaiyē ninaindu en manam mey silirkkume** – whatever consequences they face by constantly thinking about periya perumāḷ, my heart too faces the same consequences by constantly thinking about them. Their bodies, which face the consequences, can be seen but my heart, which faces the same consequences, cannot be seen. Here, reference to goose bumps is applicable to shedding tears of joy etc.

We will take up the 6th pāsuram, next.

## 2.6. Adi andam anantam

*ādi andam anantam aṟpudamāna vānavar tambirānpādamāmalar sūdum battiyilāda pāvigaḷ uyndidattīdil nanneṟi kātti engum tirindu arangan emmānukkēkādal sey toṇḍarkku eppiṟappilum kādal seyyum en nenjamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ādi – being the causative entity; andam – living even during deluge, in the same state; anantam – pervading all entities; aṟpudam – being an amazing entity; Ana vānavar tam pirān – tiruvaranganātan, being the lord of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam), his; māmalarp pādam – the divine feet which are like nonpareil lotus-flower; sūdum batti ilāda – those do not have the devotion (towards him) to bear those divine feet on their heads; pāvigaḷ uyndida – for uplifting even such sinners; engum tirindu – wandering everywhere; tīdu il nal neṟi kātti – demonstrating the good paths, without any deficiency; emmān aranganukkē – towards my lord, śrī ranganātan; kādal sey toṇḍarkku – towards followers who are affectionate; en nenjam – my mind; eppiṟappilum – in all births; kādal seyyam – will desire (to be servile)

**Перевод:**

**śrī ranganātan is the amazing entity who is the causative entity for all creations; he exists even during deluge, in the same state; he pervades all entities; he is the lord of nityasūris. His followers roam everywhere, demonstrating the true path to uplift even those sinners who do not have the devotion to don the divine feet of emperumān. My mind will desire to be affectionate, in every birth, towards such śrī vaiṣṇavas, who are affectionate towards śrī ranganātan.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār longs to be affectionate, in any birth, towards emperumān’s followers who are affectionate towards emperumān.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ādi** – one who is the cause for the worlds

**andam** – Just as it has been mentioned in cāndōkya upaniśat 6-2-1 “*sadēva sōmya idam agra āsīt ēkamēva advitīyam*” (during the time of deluge, only brahmam (supreme entity) exists, which has as its attributes all *cētanācētana* entities (those who have knowledge and those which do not have any knowledge) which are in subtle form (without a form and name), and \[that brahmam\] which is also called as *sat* (that which exists)), the supreme entity exists, and has as its body, all the *cētanācētana* entities in subtle form.

**anantam** – during both the causative state which is called as *sat* and in the effect state when brahmam has *cētanācētana* in their gross state (when they have forms and names), brahmam pervades all the *cētanācētana* entities.

**aṟpudam** – in both the subtle and gross states, as detailed above, brahmam exhibits an amazing characteristic of pervading every entity, a feature which is not exhibited by anyone else.

**Ana vānavar tambirān** – He is the lord of nityasūris too, who exist both during the time when this samsāram exists in its gross state and when it is in an integral state with emperumān without manifesting itself.

Thus far, through the first four terms, emperumān has been explained as the lord of leelāvibhūti (materialistic realm) and in the fifth term, he has been explained as the lord of nityavibhūti (spiritualistic realm), making him the lord of both vibhūtis.

**pāda māmalar sūdum battiyilāda** – those who have knowledge, should have the devotion of keeping the divine feet, which are like a blossomed lotus, of such emperumān, on their heads. Those who do not have that devotion…

**pāvigaḷ uyndida** – those who indulge in sinful activities and end up as being without devotion. taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6 says *asannēva sa bhavati* l *asad brahmēti vēda cēt* l *asti brahmēti cēd vēda santamēnam tatō vidu*: ll (one who does not have knowledge about brahmam (supreme being) is non-existent; if he has knowledge about brahmam, for that reason alone, he is known to be existing). As mentioned here, in order to uplift those who are considered to be non-existent, by seeking them out and to create devotion in them \[towards emperumān\]

**tīdil nanneṟī kātti** – by demonstrating the correct path to be followed through their personal actions, which is distinct from the path shown by bhāhyas and kudruśtis, who vehemently deny existence of vēdas and who interpret the vēdas wrongly, respectively.

**engum tirindu** – entering all the places where such people would be present

**arangan emmānukkē kādal sey toṇḍarkku** – to those śrī vaiṣṇavas who have devotion towards the divine feet of that periya perumāḷ alone, who made them exist only for himself

**eppiṟappilum kādal seyyum en nenjamē** – my heart desires that in all different births, it should serve them with affection.

We will take up the 7th pāsuram of this decad, next.
