## 2.7. Karinam purai meni

*kārinam purai mēni naṟkadir muttaveṇṇagaic ceyya vāyāramārvan arangan ennum arum perunjudar onṟinaiccērum nenjinarāgic cērndu kasindu iḻinda kaṇṇīrgaḷālvāra niṟpavar tāḷiṇaikku oru vāram āgum en nenjamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kār inam purai mēni – divine form matching a group of clouds; nal kadir – beautiful radiance; muttam veṇ nagai – having white teeth, looking like pearls; seyya vāy – having a reddish mouth; āram mārban – having a divine chest with pearl necklace; arangan ennum – known as śrī ranganātan; arum perum – being rare to obtain and having greatness; sudar onṟīnaoi – the unique splendour; sērum nenjinar āgi – having a mind which desires to attain and experience; sērndu – approaching him; kasindu iḻinda – (due to overflowing devotion) breaking out and growing; kaṇṇīrgaḷ – tears; vāra niṟpavar – those who remain, with (the tears) flowing; tāḷ iṇaikku – for the two divine feet; en nenjam – my heart; oru vāram āgum – will be affectionate (without expecting anything in return)

**Перевод:**

**śrī ranganātan is having a divine form, matching a group of clouds. He has white teeth, matching pearls, which are beautifully radiant. He has reddish mouth and a divine chest with pearl necklace. He is great and is rare to attain. My heart will be affectionate towards the two divine feet of those who have a mind which desires to attain and experience \[that emperumān\], and approach him, with tears overflowing from their eyes.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that his heart is pouring out affection at the divine feet of emperumān’s followers, without expecting any recompense.*

**kār inam purai mēni** – through the first line, āḻvār describes the divine form of emperumān. Just as nammāḻvār had mercifully described emperumān in his tiruvāīmoḻi 8-5-8 “*tokka mēgap pal kuḻāngaḷ*” (his divine form matches groups of clouds) and toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār in his tirumālai 22 “*kārttiraḷ anaiya mēni*” (emperumān’s divine form matches a group of dark clouds), āḻvār says that emperumān has a divine form which is like a group of clouds. This describes the divine form of emperumān as a whole. Next, he describes the individual limbs…

**nal kadir mutta veṇṇagaic ceyya vāy** – the row of his white teeth is having beautiful radiance and is like a pearl, attracting people such that they cannot go past it. To add colour to this, his mouth is reddish, says āḻvār.

**Ara mārvan** – just as it has been said in mūnṟām tiruvandādi “*periya varai mārvil pērāram pūṇḍu*” (donning a great necklace on his huge, divine chest which is like a mountain), āḻvār says that in order to manifest that he is the *sarvalōka* *cakravarti* (emperor of all the worlds), emperumān has donned chains, which are worn by great kings, on his divine chest. This describes the beauty of his ornaments.

**arangan ennum arum perum sudar onṟinai** – one who is known famously as śrī ranganātan and as periya perumāḷ in both vibhūtis (materialistic and spiritual realms) and who has matchless splendour.

**sērum nenjinarāgi** – having a mind which desires to attain periya perumāḷ. Having an affection towards emperumān such that when he comes on his own to uplift (his followers, they will) not shun him.

**sērndu kasindu iḻinda kaṇṇirgaḷāl vāra niṟpavar tāḷiṇaikku** – they approach him, with matchless devotion and stand in front of him, with tears of joy flowing from their eyes.  Knowing that due to their deep devotion towards him, they are no more under their control, instead of being servile to both emperumān and to his followers, being servile only to the divine feet of his followers.

**oru vāramāgum en nenjamē** – āḻvār says that his heart will consider their divine feet alone as the benefit and not anything else.

We will take up the 8th pāsuram of this decad, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 2.8. Malai urra kadal

*mālaiyuṟṟa kadal kidandavan vaṇḍu kiṇḍu naṟunduḻāymālaiyuṟṟa varaip perundirumārvanai malarkkaṇṇanaimālaiyuṟṟu eḻundu ādippādit tirindu arangan emmānukkēmālaiyuṟṟidum toṇḍar vāḻvukku mālaiyuṟṟadu en nenjamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mālai uṟṟa – (being joyous due to its divine form being touched by emperumān), throwing out waves; kadal kidandavan – one who is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); vaṇḍu kiṇḍu – being probed by beetles; naṟu tuḻāy mālai uṟṟa – one who is donning the divine tuḷasi garland; varai peru tiru mārvanai – one who is having a broad, divine chest similar to a mountain; malarkkaṇṇanai – in the matters relating to emperumān who has eyes similar to red lotus; mālai uṟṟu – having affection; eḻundu ādip pādi – instead of remaining in one place, roaming around, dancing and singing; arangan emmānukkē – only for ranganātan, my svāmy (lord); mālai uṟṟidum – becoming delirious; toṇḍar vāḻvukku – for the śrīvaiṣṇava śrī (wealth of kainkaryam) of śrī vaiṣṇavas (followers of śrī ranganātan); en nenjam mālai uṟṟadu – my mind has affection

**Перевод:**

**emperumān is reclining on tiruppāṟkadal which is throwing out waves due to its happiness of being constantly touched by emperumān. He is donning a divine tuḷasi garland which is probed by beetles \[for drinking honey\]. He has a divine, broad chest which is like a mountain. He has eyes similar to a red lotus. His followers, due to affection which they have for śrī ranganātan, being unable to remain in one place, becoming delirious, are roaming around, singing and dancing. My mind will have affection for the wealth of kainkaryam which such śrī vaiṣṇavas have.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that his heart has affection towards the followers of emperumān who have śrīvaiṣṇavaśrī (wealth of kainkaryam)*

**mālaiyuṟṟa kadal kidandavan** – emperumān is reclining on tiruppāṟkadal, which is throwing out waves due to the bliss of being constantly touched by that emperumān.

**vaṇḍu kiṇḍu naṟum tuḻāy mālai uṟṟa varai perum tirumārvanai** – He has a broad, divine chest which is decorated by a tuḷasi garland, which has just then blossomed; beetles are humming around that garland, probing \[for honey\]. Was it not said mercifully in mūnṟām tiruvandādi 56 “*maipōl neduvarai tāḻum aruvipōl tār kidappa*” (the garland was like a river flowing on his wide chest which was looking like a dark, huge mountain)!

**malarkkaṇṇanai** – one who has divine eyes which look like a just-blossomed lotus.

**mālaiyuṟṟeḻundu ādippādit tirindu** – (his followers) owing to the deep affection that they have towards emperumān, will roam around everywhere, being unable to remain in one place, dancing and singing.

**arangan emmānukkē mālaiyuṟṟidum toṇḍar vāḻvukku** – manifesting his simplicity towards everyone, periya perumāḷ made me his servitor. Due to the affection that they have for that emperumān, his followers become delirious and roam around. For the wealth of their service to emperumān. The opinion here is that being a śrī vaiṣṇava is in itself a great wealth.

**mālai uṟṟadu en nenjamē** – my heart became affectionate.

We will take up the 9th pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 2.9. Moyttuk kanpani sora

*moyttuk kaṇpani sōra meygaḷ silirppa ēngi iḷaittu ninṟueyttuk kumbidu nattamittu eḻundu ādippādi iṟainji enattaan accan aranganukku adiyārgaḷāgi avarukkēpittarāmavar pittarallargaḷ maṟṟaiyār muṟṟum pittarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇ pani moyttu sōra – with the eyes shedding tears of joy, like a rainfall; meygaḷ silirppa – with the (physical) bodies exhibiting goose bumps; ēngi iḷaittu ninṟu – (meditating on emperumān), remaining in a forlorn way, becoming tired; eyttu – becoming bewildered; kumbidu nattam ittu – rolling and dancing; eḻundu ādip pādi iṟainji – getting up, dancing, singing and vorśipping; en attan – being my father; accan – being the lord; aranganukku – for śrī ranganātan; adiyārgaḷ āgi – becoming servitors; avarukkē – only for that periya perumāḷ; pittarām avar – those who become mad (due to deep devotion); pittar allargaḷ – are not mad; maṟṟaiyār muṟṟum – those who are not as devoted \[to emperumān\] as these people; pittarē – are indeed mad

**Перевод:**

**The followers of emperumān shed tears of joy, like a rainfall; they develop goose bumps; they remain in a forlorn way and become tired (since they keep meditating on emperumān). They become bewildered, dance, roll all over, get up again, dance, sing and worship. Such people, who are servitors for only that śrī ranganātan, who is my father and my lord, are not mad. Only those, who do not exhibit such qualities, are indeed mad.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) When āḻvār was asked “Should you roam around in a mad way behind those who are roaming around as mad people?” āḻvār says “In matters related to one’s basic characteristic (being subservient to emperumān), if one becomes mad, it is not madness at all. Only those, who are not in such a state, are said to be mad”*

**moyttuk kaṇ pani sōra** – with tears flowing down from the two eyes, like pouring rain

**meygaḷ silirppa** *–* for the body to develop goose bumps

**ēngi iḷaittu ninṟu eyttuk kumbidu nattamittu eḻundu ādippādi iṟainji** – constantly meditating on emperumān, if one were unable to experience him, that person would become forlorn and tired. He (or She) will become bewildered and stunned, not being able to carry out any activity. Not being able to remain in this state too, for long ….

**kumbidu nattam ittu** – cupping the palms in *anjali* posture, dancing upside down.

**iṟainji** – prostrating on the floor

**en atttan accan** – one who created me and who is my lord

**aranganukku adiyārgaḷāgi** – becoming servitors of those who are servitors of no one other than periya perumāḷ. The opinion is that the quality of being a servitor to emperumān will be established only if one is a servitor to emperumān’s followers.

**avarukkē pittarām avar pittarallargaḷ** – being affectionate towards them, becoming mad as a result of that affection, and roaming around – such people are not mad.

**maṟṟaiyār muṟṟum pittarē** – only all those who do not become mad as a result of this devotion, are said to be mad. Those who remain calm, without such bewilderment, are said to be mad even if they are wise such as sanaka, sananda et al \[sons of brahmā born through his mind\].

In the next article, we will take up the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 2.10. Alli mamalar

*aālli māmalar mangai nādan – te consort of periya pirātti vho resides in a distinguiśed lotus flover, vhic has inner petalsarangan – śrī ranganātan’smeyyadiyārgaḷ tam – true folloversellaiyil adimaittiṟattinil – in te services vhic tey render vitout any boundaryenṟum mēvu manattan ām – one vho alvays engages vit divine mindkolli kāvalan – one vho is te king of kolli nagarkūdal nāyagan – one vho is te king of maduraikōḻi kōn – one vho is te king of uṟaiyūrsollin – composed of vordsin tamiḻ mālai vallār – tose vho can learn te tamiḻ pāsurams vhic are togeter vit musical notestoṇḍar toṇḍargaḷ āvarē– vill become te follovers of \[emperumān’s\] follovers.*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**The true followers of emperumān render service, without any boundary, to śrī ranganātan who is the consort of periya pirātti, who resides in a distinguished lotus, with inner petals. kulaśēkhara āḻvār, who is the king of kolli nagar, the king of madurai as well as of uṟaiyūr, engages constantly with such followers with his divine mind. Those who can learn these pāsurams which have been composed with sweet tamiḻ words, and set to music, will become followers of emperumān’s such followers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār brings the decad to its completion by stating the benefit for those who recite these ten pāsurams – that they will become servitors of emperumān’s servitors. alli māmalar mangai nādan arangan meyyadiyārgaḷ tamellaiyil adimaittiṟattinil enṟumēvu manattanāmkolli kāvalan kūdal nāyagan kōḻikkōn kulasēkaransollin intamiḻ māḷai vallavar toṇḍar toṇḍargaḷ āvarē Word-by-Word-Meanings*

**alli māmalar mangai nādan arangan** – the consort of periya pirātti, periya perumāḷ, his

**meyyadiyārgaḷ tam** – śrī vaiṣṇavas who carry out services to him without expecting any benefit from him, other than his happiness

**ellaiyil adimaittiṟattinil enṟum mēvu manattanām** – one who has the divine mind which always engages with unbounded kainkaryam, just as it has been mentioned in viṣṇutatvam “*svōjīvanēccā yadi tē svasattāyām spruhā yati* l *ātmadāsyam harē: svāmyam svabhāvanca sadā smara* ll” (If you desire in your getting uplifted, if you desire in your existence, always think that ātmā’s servitude and hari’s \[emperumān\] lordship are natural).

**kolli kāvalan kūdal nāyagan kōḻiyar kōn** – kolli is the capital of cēra nādu (the land of cēra kings); kūdal is the capital of paṇḍiya kings; kōḻi, also known as uṟaiyūr, is the capital of cōḻa kings. Since he is the head of all three, āḻvār mentions that all the three kingdoms are under his control. While he says in his pāsurams “*arasai vēṇḍēn*” (I do not desire kingdoms), could he claim that he is the head of all the kingdoms? If we consider a few words of śrī rāma, we can understand the *śrīsūktis* (divine compositions) of kulaśēkharap perumāḷ. śrī rāma tells lakśmaṇa as they prepare to set out for forest, in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 22-29 “*mā ca lakśmaṇa*! *santāpam kārśīr lakśmyā viparyayē* l *rājyam vā vanavāsō vā vanavāsō mahōdaya*: ll” (Oh lakśmaṇa! Do not grieve for kingdom slipping out. Between kingdom and living in forest, living in forest appears better). However, after reaching forest, after experiencing separation from sītāppirātti and after seeing the death of jatāyu, forgetting all the bliss that he had experienced in forest, he says in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 67-24 “*rājyād bhramśō vanē vāsa*: *sitā naśtā dvijō hata*: l *īdruśīyam mamā’lakśmīr nirdahēdapi pāvakam* ll” (The sorrows which happened to me such as losing kingdom, living in forest, losing (separation from) sītā, jatāyu being killed, will burn fire too), stressing on the fact that these sorrows appeared huge for him, hurting him, as he had lost kingdom and had to take up living in the forest. In this way, dwelling in forest, which appeared to be better for śrī rāma at one point of time, appeared cruel to him later. In the same way, kulaśēkharap perumāḷ celebrated when royalty gave him an opportunity to serve followers of emperumān, while in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 4.1, he had said “*unēṟu sevattu udaṟpiṟavi yān vēṇḍēn*” (I will not covet this birth which entails this human body gaining wealth and becoming fat day by day) and in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 4-5 “*inbamarum selvamum ivvarasum yān vēṇḍēn*” (I will not covet this kingdom and also opulence which gives various comforts). Thus, the kingdom, which was apt to be given up for him at one point of time, was also desired by him at another point of time.

**sollin intamiḻ mālai vallavar** – those who learn well this decad, which is composed of pāsurams in tamiḻ, with sweet words

**toṇḍar toṇḍargaḷ āvarē** – āḻvār says that they will get all the benefits as listed below, and will remain blissful, and he brings this decad to its end:

- Seeing the groups of followers
- Dancing in the dust from the divine feet of followers
- Applying the mud from the divine feet of followers, on forehead
- Praising the divine feet of followers
- Thinking about the followers, and getting goose bumps
- Desiring to be affectionate towards the followers in all ways
- Considering the divine feet of followers as the benefit
- Being affectionate towards the wealth of kainkaryams of followers
- Being mad about the followers.

In the next article, we will take up pravēsam of 3rd decad.

## 3.1. Meyyil valkkaiyai

*meyyil vāḻkkaiyai meyyenak koḷḷumivvaiyandannodum kūduvadiilai yānaiyanē! arangā! enṟu aḻaikkinṟēnmaiyal koṇḍoḻindēṇ endan māḷukkē*

**Пословный перевод:**

meyyil vāḻkkaiyai – life on this earth which is unstable; meyyanak koḷḷum – considering it as stable; ivvaiyam tannodum – with the people of this world; yān kūduvadu illai – I do not mingle; aiyanē – Oh my relative (without any reason)!; arangā – Oh śrī ranganāta!; enṟu aḻaikkinṟēn – I am calling out, like this; en than mālukkē – towards emperumān who is affectionate with me; maiyal koṇḍu oḻindēn – I remain affectionate

**Перевод:**

**I do not unite with those people who consider this unstable life on earth as a stable one. I am deeply affectionate towards śrī ranganātan, my lord and who is my relative without there being any reason for that relationship; he is affectionate towards me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says “I do not mingle with those ignorant people who think that this unstable life (in samsāram) is stable. I am affectionate towards emperumān who is very affectionate towards me”*

**meyyil vāḻkkaiyai meyyenak koḷḷum** – the term *ivvaiyam* is a distinguished term for this world. This world considers that life, which is unstable, is very much stable. The meaning for the term *meyyil vāḻkkai* is not the falseness which is the belief of bauddas \[those who do not believe in vēdams\] and *māyāvādis* \[those who think that life is illusory; those who interpret vēdas wrongly\]; this is so because other pāsurams of this āḻvār are completely different from those philosophies. Thus, the term *mey* here refers to stable entity and the term *meyyil vāḻkkai* refers to unstable life on earth. This world believes in holding on to *acētana* entities (those which do not have knowledge) while entities such as *ātmā* (souls) and *paramātmā* (supreme entity; emperumān) are stable.  Alternatively, the term *mey* could be taken as referring to physical body. This would then refer to all the worldly pursuits which are enjoyed by the body, which in itself, is a transformation of *mūlaprakruti* (primordial matter).

**ivvaiyam tannodum kūduvadillai yān** – I do not have any connection with those who consider that “*Body is ātmā*”.

Through the last two lines āḻvār says that he is aware of *paramātmā* who is the indvelling soul of *ātmā*, who in turn, is different from the physical body.

**aiyanē** – one who is naturally related to me, unlike other relatives who are related because of *karma* (effect of previous deeds).

**arangā** – that emperumān is naturally related to us is not through hearing from śāstras (sacred texts) but through his physical presence in śrī rangam where he has taken residence in order to uplift all *cētanas* (those who have power to think) and is easily approachable.

**enṟu aḻaikkinṟēn** – without considering any other benefit by calling out to him, I am reciting his divine name considering that in itself, as the benefit.

**maiyal koṇḍu oḻindēn endan māḷukkē** – considering that he is deeply affectionate towards me, I too am deeply affectionate towards him.

Next, we will take up the second pāsuram of this decad.

## 3.2. Nuliner idaiyar

*nūḷinēr idaiyār tiṟattē niṟkumjñālam tannodum kūduvadillai yānāliyā aḻaiyā arangā! enṟumāl eḻundu oḻindēn endan mālukkē*

**Пословный перевод:**

jñālam tannodum – with the people of this world; yān kūduvadillai – I do not mingle; āliyā – (with overflowing affection) dancing; arangā enṟu – reciting the divine names of periya perumāḷ; aḻaiyā – calling out; en than mālukkē – towards emperumān who is affectionate towards me; māḷ eḻundu oḻindēn – I developed affection

**Перевод:**

**I do not unite with the people of this world who are involved with ladies who have slender waist. Instead, I dance, with overflowing affection, call out the divine names of periya perumāḷ and develop affection towards him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that he does not involve with those who are involved in worldly pursuits. He involves himself with emperumān alone.*

**nūlinēr idaiyār tiṟattē niṟkum** – those who are involved with ladies who have thread-like slender waist.

**niṟkum** – this term (meaning ‘remaining’) indicates that once they are involved with one limb of a person’s physical form, they would remain involved with that limb and not move on further to other limbs of the form.

**jñālam tannodum kūduvadillai yān** – āḻvār says that he does not have any connection with those who, instead of showing involvement with the ordained *bhagavat viśayam* (matters relating to emperumān), show involvement with matters which are not congruent with their *svarūpam* (basic nature – being totally subservient to emperumān alone).

In the subsequent two lines, āḻvār says that he shows involvement, which the others show towards worldly pursuits, towards the ordained matter.

**āliyā arangā enṟu aḻaiyā** – due to excessive joy, being unable to remain in one place, dancing; reciting the divine name *arangā* of periya perumāḷ, even if it is in a disorderly manner.

**māl eḻundoḻindēn endan mālukkē** – just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 16-16 “*sarvēśām hi sa darmātmā varṇānām kurutē dayām* l *caturṇām hi vaya:stānām tēna tē tamanuvratā*: ll” (that śrī rāma, who is the personification of righteousness, showers his mercy on the four classes of people (*brāhmaṇa* (priests)*, kśattriya* (warriors)*, vaiśya* (business people)and *śūdra* (workers)) in four stages of their lives (*śaiśava* (infant), *bālyam* (playing), *yauvanam* (youthful) and *vārdānam* (old)); hence they too are affectionate towards him), seeing the affection that emperumān has towards me, I too developed affection towards him.

We will take up the 3rd pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 3.3. Maranar vari

*maranār vari venjilaikku ātceyyumpārinārodum kūduvadillai yānāramārvan arangan anantan nalnāraṇan naragāndagan pittanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

māranār – manmatan’s (cupid’s); vari vem silaikku – for the beautiful, cruel bow; āḷ seyyum – those who roam around, under its control; pārinārodum – with the people of this world; yān kūduvadillai – I do not mingle; āram mārvan – one who has chains (of pearl) on his divine chest; arangan – having the divine name of śri ranganātan; anantan – one who cannot be known through any measurement; nal nārāṇan – distinguished abode for jīvātmās (souls) to attain; naragāndagan – one who protects his followers from falling into hell

**Перевод:**

**I do not mingle with the people of this world, who roam around under the control of the beautiful, but cruel, bow of cupid’s. I have developed affection towards śrī ranganāta, who has chains of pearl on his chest, who cannot be estimated in any way, who is the final abode for his followers and who protects his followers from falling into hell.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that he does not mingle with the people of this world who roam around, under the thumb of cupid (lord of love). He says that he is affectionate towards emperumān alone.*

**māranār vari venjilaikku ātceyyum pārinārodum kūduvadillai yān** – this could be construed in two ways: *parinārodum* (with the people of this world) and *pāril ārodum* (with anyone in this world). In the first case, the meaning would be – I do not move with the people of the world who are roaming around, being won over by the beautiful, and cruel, but inescapable bow of cupid’s. In the second case, the meaning would be – I do not move with anyone in this world, who is roaming around, being won over by the beautiful, and cruel, but inescapable bow of cupid’s. In the explanatory notes for this pāsura vyākhyānam, it has been mentioned that the term *pāril ārodum* refers to lowly people who do not have any place of their own.

**Ara mārvan arangan** – emperumān who dons various divine ornaments such as chains etc in order to attract everyone. This term *āram* (chain) is representative of other divine ornaments such as crown etc

**anantan** – when emperumān incarnated as a human being such as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al, he showed his quality of *paratvam* (supreme entity). In the same way, even in the form of *arcai* (form of *vigraha* or idol form) while he manifests his quality of simplicity, he shows his quality of inestimable superiority.

**nal nāraṇan** – The term *nāraṇan* refers to his quality of showering his affection towards the collection of human beings who are his possession. Since he is one who will not give up on his possession, even if they are born with faults, and shows affection towards them, he is referred to as *nal nāraṇan* (the good emperumān).

**naragāndagan** – this can be construed as one who annihilated narakāsuran (a demon) or as one who protects his followers from falling into hell. periyāḻvār in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-4-4 says, of emperumān, “*naraga nāsanai*” (one who prevents the entry of his followers into hell). tirumangai āḻvār says in his periya tirumoḻi 10-9-4 “*naraganait tolaitta karadalattamaidiyin karuttō*” (is it due to the strength in your hands to kill the demon, narakan?), following similar meaning. The opinion here is that when emperumān, who is nāraṇan, having *vātsalyam* (motherly forbearance) towards jīvātmās, his possession, falls on them to protect them, all hurdles will disappear.

**pittanē** – āḻvār says that he is affectionate towards one who is like this.

In the next article, we will take up the 4th pāsuram of this decad.

## 3.4. Undiye udaiye

*uṇḍiyē udaiyē ugandodum immaṇḍalattodum kūduvadillai yānaṇḍavāṇan arangan van pēy mulaiuṇḍavāṇan tan unmattan kāṇminē*

**Пословный перевод:**

uṇḍiyē – food; udaiyē – and clothes; ugandu ōdum – desirously running after; immaṇḍalattodum – with the people of this world; yān kūduvadu illai – I do not mingle; aṇḍavāṇan – being the lord of all those who are in this universe; van pēy mulai uṇḍa vāyan – one who has the mouth with which he drank (life) from the bosom of pūtanā, who had a cruel heart; arangan than – in the matter of śrī ranganātan; unmattan kāṇminē – I am having mad affection

**Перевод:**

**I do not mingle with the people of this world who are running after food and clothes. Instead, I am having deep affection for śrī ranganātan, who is the lord of all the people in this universe and who, during kriṣṇāvatāram, drank the life out of pūtanā’s bosom, with his divine mouth.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says that he does not mingle with people who run after food and clothes. He says that he is one who is involved with emperumān.*

**uṇḍiyē udaiyē ugandodum** – In order to worship and to meditate on emperumān, the physical body should be in a sound condition. Hence, in order to sustain the body, there is nothing wrong in desiring these (food and clothes). However, since most of the people in the world spend on food and clothes, after selling off their possessions, āḻvār calls them as *uṇḍiyē udaiyē ugandodum immaṇḍalam* (people who run after food and clothes).

**immaṇḍalattodum kūduvadillai yān** – The meaning for this is that he does not mingle with the people of this world. nammāḻvār says in his tiruvāimoḻi 6-7-1 “*uṇṇum sōṟu parugu nīr tinnum veṟṟilaiyum ellām kaṇṇan*” – nityavibhūti (spiritual realm) considers emperumān to be sustaining, nurturing and enjoyable entity. In a similar vein, the lowly leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) considers the worldly pursuits as everything to live for. āḻvār calls them as *immaṇḍalam*. Instead of mingling with them, āḻvār says that he is like nammāḻvār, considering kaṇṇan (emperumān) as everything.

In the last two lines, he says that he is involved fully with emperumān.

**aṇḍavāṇan** – lord of those who are in this universe, inside leelāvibhūti.

**arangan** – instead of remaining in śrī vaikuṇṭam, ruling over this materialistic realm, he has entered this, as a simple entity for everyone, residing in śrī rangam, close to us.

**van pēy mulai uṇḍa vāyan than unmattan kāṇminē** – emperumān drank the life out of the bosom of the demon pūtanā, who had a cruel heart \[to kill infant kriṣṇa\]. āḻvār says that he has deep affection towards that emperumān. This episode of annihilation of pūtanā manifests his nature of incarnating here and removing the hurdles of his followers.

**than unmattan kāṇminē** – āḻvār says that just as it is difficult to extricate those who are immersed in the pleasures of this world, it is difficult to extricate him who is madly involved with the auspicious qualities and amazing activities of emperumān, and to unite him with those who are searching for food and clothes.

We will next take up the 5th pāsuram of this decad.

## 3.5. Tidil nanneri

*tīdil nanneṟi niṟka allādu seynīdiyārodum kūduvadillai yānādi āyan arangan andāmaraippēdai mā maṇavāḷan tan pittanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tīdil nanneṟi niṟka – while there are proper paths without any fault; allādu sey nīdiyārodum – those who have taken a vow to keep desiring lowly benefits; yān kūduvadillai – I do not mingle; ādi – being the causative factor for all the worlds; āyan – incarnating as kriṣṇa and being easy to approach for all; am tāmarai pēdai mā maṇavāḷan – being the consort of periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) who resides on a beautiful lotus flower; pittanē – I remained infatuated

**Перевод:**

**āḻvār says that he does not mingle with those who have taken a vow to desire only lowly benefits even though there are proper paths which are without any fault, for them to follow. Instead, he says that he is infatuated with śrī ranganātan, who is the consort of periya pirāttiyār, who dwells on a beautiful lotus flower. He is the causative factor for all the worlds and was easy to approach, during his incarnation as kriṣṇa.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says that he does not mingle with those who are desirous of lowly benefits and engage in improper activities, and that he developed affection only for emperumān.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tīdil nanneṟi niṟka** – there are many ways which are without any fault, to attain emperumān in this world, and to lead a righteous life. However, there are many people who observe various methods in order to attain benefits other than emperumān himself. While looking at the benefits that they attain, it would appear that it would be better not to attain such benefits, and to refrain from indulging in such activities.

**allādu sey nīdiyārodum kūduvadillai yān** – I do not mingle with those people who are pursuing such lowly benefits and who think that those benefits would uplift them.

In the next two lines, āḻvār says that it is because of his infatuation with emperumān that he was not mingling with such people.

**ādi** – one who is the cause for all the worlds. atarvaśikai says “*kāraṇantu dyēya*:” (only that entity who is responsible for the creation of all the worlds is apt to be meditated upon). emperumān is the one who is apt to be vorśipped upon, since he is the causative factor.

**āyan** – though he is apt to be meditated upon, instead of remaining in *paramapadam* (śrī vaikuṇṭam) and being difficult to be seen, he incarnated as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al and was easily accessible to all.

**arangan** – while incarnating, he did not make himself accessible only to those who were there during those times and leave back for *paramapadam* such that those who came in later times could not get any benefit. Instead, he took residence in śrī rangam so that even those who came in later times could worship him and get the benefit of his presence.

**andāmaraip pēdai māmaṇavāḷan than pittanē** – I became infatuated with emperumān who is the loving consort of periya pirātti, who dwells on a beautiful lotus and who is the decorative entity for all women. The verse *andāmaraip pēdai mā maṇavāḷan* indicates the unique greatness of periya pirātti. If the jīvātmā (soul born in the materialistic realm) takes one step towards emperumān (viz. if he becomes a little bit favourable), she magnifies it and removes the hurt caused in emperumān’s heart by the faulty activities of the jīvātmā, by being the *puruśakārabhūtai* (one who recommends the jīvātmā to paramātmā). It is pertinent to recall śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam ślōkam 37-18 here: “*apramēyam hi tattējō yasya sā janakātmajā* l *na tvam samartas tām hartum rāmācāpāśrayām vanē* ll”  
((mārīcan tells rāvaṇan) that splendour of śrī rāma, who, pirātti, the divine daughter of janaka maharāja belongs to, is inestimable. You are not capable of bringing here that pirātti who lives under his bow). The implied meaning here is that emperumān acquires greatness because he is the sweet consort of such pirātti. āḻvār says that he does not mingle with the others because he is infatuated with matters relating to that emperumān.

We will next take up the 6th pāsuram of this decad.

## 3.6. Embarattar allarodum

*em barattar allārodum – tose vho do not carry out service like ī do \[to emperumān\]kūdalan – (ī) vill not mingleumbar vāḻvai – te opulence of celestial entitiesonṟu āga – as a materialkarudilan – vill not consideramararkku tambirān – controller of nityasūris (permanent dvellers of spiritual realm)aranga nagar – one vho has taken residence inside te templeempirānukku – in matters related to periya perumāḷ, my svāmy (lord)eḻumaiyum – at all timespittanē – vill be infatuated*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**I do not mingle with those who do not carry out service to emperumān. I do not consider the opulence of celestial entities as anything worthwhile. I am infatuated, at all times, with my svāmy who has taken residence inside the temple in śrī rangam and who is the controller of nityasūris.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “Not only do I not mingle with the people of this world, I do not consider the wealth of dēvalōkam (land inhabited by celestial entities) as material to be coveted. Irrespective of the number of births that I take, I will remain infatuated with emperumān alone”. embarattar allārodum kūdalanumbar vāḻvai onṟagak karudilantambirān amararkku aranga nagarembirānukku eḻumaiyum pittanē Word-by-Word-Meanings*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**embarattar allārodum kūdalan** – āḻvār refers to those who are like himself as *embarattar*. I will not get attached to those who do not have a system of life similar to mine. nammāḻvār had said in tiruviruttam 1 “*inninṟa nīrmai iniyām uṟāmai*” (we do not wish to suffer all these sorrows which we have to endure once we are born); he had also said in tiruvāimoḻi 3-3-1 “*vaḻuvilā adimai seyyavēṇḍum nām*” (we wish to carry out faultless service (to emperumān)). Thus, āḻvār says that he does not have any attachment towards those who do not have detachment towards worldly pursuits and who do not engage in service for emperumān.

**umbar vāḻvai onṟāgak kurudilan** – even if I not develop detachment towards samsāram and attachment towards *bhagavat kainkaryam* (service to emperumān) and even if I were to get the wealth of brahmā et al, I will consider that to be equivalent to grass.

**tambirān amararkku** – just as nammāḻvār had mercifully said in tiruvāīmoḻi 1-1-1 “*ayarvaṟum amarargaḷ adipati*” (the lord of nityasūris who do not have any ennui), he is the lord of nityasūris too.

**aranganagar empirānukku** – to ensure that the enjoyment which the nityasūris experience is not denied to samsāris, emperumān has taken residence in the huge temple inside śrī rangam, as the epitome of simplicity.

**eḻumaiyum pittanē** – irrespective of the number of births that I have, I will be infatuated to that emperumān, being won over by his simplicity.

We will take up the 7th pāsuram of this decad.

## 3.7. Ettirattilum yarodum

*ettiṟattilum yārodum kūdum accittam tannait tavirttanan sengaṇmālattanē! arangā! enṟu aḻaikkinṟēnpittanāyoḻindēṇ empirānukkē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ettiṟattilum – to attain whatever I desire; yārodum – with any person; kūdum accittam tannai – my mind which will get ruined; tavirttanan – mercifully removed; attanē – Oh lord who controls me!; arangā – Oh śrī ranganāta!; enṟu aḻaikkinṟēn – I called out this way; empirānukkē – for my lord (who removed my involvement with worldly pursuits); pittanāy oḻindēn – I became infatuated

**Перевод:**

**emperumān removed the thought in my mind by which I could attain whatever I desired, with any person \[even if they are not followers of emperumān\]. I called out to that emperumān by crying out “Oh my lord, who controls me! Oh śrī ranganātā!” and became infatuated.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) When asked “How did you get this greatness of not mingling with others?” āḻvār says “This did not happen due to me; it was only emperumān who created this in me”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ettiṟattilum** – even if I am told “By mingling with those who are not followers of emperumān, you would attain all the benefits that you desire”, I will not have any desire in talking to such people.

**yārodum kūdum** – even if I do not get the desired benefit, if I am told that I will attain all the eminence by mingling with *avaiṣṇavas* (those who are not followers of emperumān), I will consider that mingling to be inferior to even the dust on my feet.

**accittam tannai tavirttanan sengaṇmāl** – by looking at me with his reddish, lotus-like eyes, with a comforting glance and displaying his affection towards me, emperumān made my mind to be incongruent with such avaiṣṇavas.

**attanē** – Oh one who is my svāmy (lord)!

**arangā enṟu aḻaikkinṟēn** – I am calling out to him by saying “Oh one who is residing in śrī rangam in order to make it clear that you are the owner and that I am your possession!”

**pittanāy oḻindēn empirānukkē** – I became infatuated with the great benefactor who removed my involvement with worldly pursuits and who removed my connection with those who are involved in worldly pursuits.

We will take up next, the 8th pāsuram of this decad.

## 3.8. Peyare enakku

*pēyarē enakku yāvarum yānum ōrpēyanē evarkkum idu pēsi en?āyanē! arangā! enṟu aḻaikkinṟēnpēyanāy oḻindēn empirānukkē*

**Пословный перевод:**

yāvarum – all the people of this world (who desire the evanescent worldly pursuits); enakku – for me (who desires the eminent benefit); pēyarē – mad only; yānum – I too; evarkkum – for all (of them); pēyanē – am a mad person only; idu pēsi en – what is the purpose of discussing this elaborately?; āyanē – Oh kriṣṇa who incarnated as covherd!; arangā – Oh śrī ranganāta!; enṟu aḻaikkinṟēn – I am calling out this way; empirānukkē – in matters relating to the supreme entity; pēyanāy oḻindēn – I became mad

**Перевод:**

**To me, all the people of this world are mad only. I too am mad for those people. What is the purpose of speaking about this? I am calling out to the supreme entity as “Oh kriṣṇa, who incarnated as covherd!” “Oh śrī ranganāta!” I became mad in matters relating to my lord.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsurams, āḻvār had said that he does not mingle with avaiṣṇavas (those who are not followers of viṣṇu, emperumān). When queried “Even if he \[āḻvār\] gives them all up and does not mingle with them, there may be some among them who would compulsively hold on to him; are there people like that?” āḻvār says in this pāsuram “I made myself such that all of them would give up on me”.*

**pēyarē enakku yāvarum** – I gave up on the people of the world, thinking “Leaving aside the permanent benefit of *bhagavat kainkaryam* (carrying out service to emperumān), these people are desirous of worldly pursuits such as *śabdam* (sensory perceptions such as sound, sight etc). They are mad”.

**yānum ōr pēyanē evarkkum** – those people also gave up on me saying “Instead of enjoying all the worldly materials which can be seen, he is firmly believing that there is an entity which is supreme, which has not been seen by anyone, and is infatuated with it. He is verily mad”.

**idu pēsi en** – what is the purpose of talking elaborately about this?

**āyanē** – He came in person by incarnating as kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa, the covherd) to make everyone see him bodily, disproving the belief that only the wise people, with their spiritual vision, can see the form of emperumān. He manifested his *svarūpam* (basic nature) and his *svabhāvam* (basic characteristics) to me.

**arangā enṟu aḻaikkinṟēn** – to ensure that even those who were not present during his times of incarnation could enjoy him subsequently, he came and reclined in the huge temple at śrī rangam. I called out to such periya perumāḷ.

**pēyanāy oḻindēn empirānukkē** – I transformed myself such that others would think “This person who is mad about the supreme entity, who cannot be seen, is not apt for us, who believe that what is seen is perceptible. He is fit to be given up”.

We will take up the last pāsuram of this dead, next.

## 3.9. Angaiyali aranganadi

*angaiyāḻi aranganadiyiṇaittangu sindait tanip perum pittanāykongar kōn kulasēkaran sonna solingu vallavarkku ēdam onṟu illaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

am kai āḻi – having trivāḻi āḻvān (divine sudarśana cakram, the weapon, disc) on his beautiful, divine hand; arangan – śrī ranganātan, his; adi iṇai – for the two divine feet; tangu sindai – having an engaging mind; tani perum pittanāy – having deep affection; kongar kōn – being the lord of the western direction; kulasēkaran – kulaśēkhara āḻvār; sonna – mercifully said; sol – these pāsurams; vallavarkku – those who have learnt; ingu – in this world; ēdam onṟu illai – no hurdle will happen (in bhagavat anubhavam)

**Перевод:**

**kulaśēkhara āḻvār has an engaging mind as well as deep affection towards the divine feet of śrī ranganātan, who has the divine disc on his beautiful, divine hand. No hurdle will come, in this world \[for experiencing that emperumān\], in the way of those who have learnt these pāsurams, mercifully composed by kulaśēkhara āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says that for those who learn this decad, there will be no hurdle in attaining emperumān, and brings the decad to an end.*

**am kai āḻi arangan adiyiṇai** – towards the divine feet of aḻagiya maṇavāḷap perumāḷ (utsavar vigraham in śrī rangam temple) who is holding the divine disc in his beautiful, divine hand

**tangu sindai** – since this word is in the present tense ‘*tangum sindai*’, it indicates that āḻvār has a mind which will always take refuge at the divine feet of emperumān, not having any other place to go to.

**tanip perum pittanāy** – he is having such infatuation towards that emperumān that it could be said that there is none who is as much affectionate towards that emperumān as āḻvār is and that he cannot be removed from the divine feet ever.

**kongar kōn kulasēkaran sonna sol** – this divine prabandam which has been mercifully composed by kulaśēkharap perumāḷ who is the king of cēra nādu which is on the western side of tamiḻ nādu.

**ingu vallavarkku ēdam onṟu illaiyē** – for those who learn this decad, with its meanings, there is no hurdle in this world. Here the term *ēdam* (hurdle) refers to union with *avaiṣṇavas* (those who are not followers of emperumān) and shortcoming in the involvement \[of those who learn this decad\] with emperumān. Until they live in this world, no such hurdle will come their way.

**ingu illai** – such hurdles will happen on their own in this world, normally. However, for those who learn this decad, with the meanings, such hurdles will not occur in this world too.

We will take up the 4th decad, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 4.1. Uneru selvattu

*ūnēṟu selvattu udanpiṟavi yān vēṇḍēnānēṟēḻ venṟān adimait tiṟam allālkūnēṟu sangam idattān tan vēngadattukkōnēri vāḻum kurugāyp piṟappēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

An ēṟēḻ venṟān – (in order to marry nappinnaip pirātti) emperumān won over seven strong bulls; adimai tiṟam allāl – instead of carrying out servitude to him; Un ēṟu selvattu udal piṟavi – this human birth which has the wealth of increasing the fat; yān vēṇḍēn – I will not desire; kūn ēṟu sangam – śrīpāncajanyam (the divine conch) which is curved; idattān than – one who has on his divine left hand, his; vēngadattu – in tirumalai; kōnēri vāḻum – one that lives in the svāmy puśkariṇi (the divine pond in tirumalai); kurugāyp piṟappēnē – I would like to be born as, at the least, a crane

**Перевод:**

**emperumān had defeated seven strong bulls which were a hurdle to his marrying nappinnaip pirātti. āḻvār says that he does not desire this human birth, which has its wealth of increasing the flesh in the body, instead of carrying out service to emperumān. He desires to be born as, at least, a crane which lives on the banks of the pond in tirumalai, in which emperumān, holding a curved divine conch on his left hand, resides.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says that he has taken a human birth which is capable of knowing that the body, which is a transformation of mūlaprakruti (primordial matter) which in itself has no knowledge, and the body within which ātmā (soul) resides are different from each other. Despite knowing this, he says that if his role as a king, wherein he has to protect his citizens, is going to be a hurdle for carrying out kainkaryam (service) to emperumān, he does not covet such a role. In this pāsuram, he says that he would be happy were he to be born as a crane in the divine hill of tirumalai, which creates a connection with emperumān and his followers.*

**Un ēṟu selvattu udal piṟavi yān vēṇḍēn** – I do not desire this human birth which has the eminence of body getting fatter since the flesh grows day by day. What is the shortcoming which āḻvār sees in this human body? With each passing day, the flesh keeps growing, making the body fatter each day. However, ātmā (soul) does not get the kainkaryam which is its svarūpam (basic nature). Hence, he says that he does not want this birth. Even though the ātma svarūpam is permanent, just as it has been said in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6 “*asannēva sa bhavati* l *asat brahmēti vēda cēt* l *asti brahmēti cēt vēta santamēnam tatō vidu*: ll” (If a person does not have knowledge about brahmam (emperumān) he or she is considered to be non-existent. (S)he is considered to be existing only if (s)he has knowledge about brahmam), due to deficiency in knowledge, soul is considered to be equivalent to *acit* (that which does not have knowledge). Since the human birth can create a deficiency in *svarūpa jñānam* (knowledge about the basic nature of the ātmā), āḻvār says that he does not desire this human birth.

**yān vēṇḍēn** – there are people who will sever their own heads in order to perform sacrificial activities to satisfy lowly deities. Before performing such activities, they will even decorate their bodies with sandalwood paste \[which appears incongruent since the body is going to be sacrificed soon\]. Thus, one who does not have basic knowledge about ātmā and paramātmā (supreme entity, emperumān) may nurture this body. But, āḻvār says that he does not want that since he has the knowledge to distinguish between the two. Just as nammāḻvār has said in tiruvāīmozi 1-1-1 “*mayarvaṟa madinalam aruḷinan*” (emperumān gave knowledge and devotion without any deficiency), emperumān considers that the body a lowly entity and ātmā is dear to him, similar to the kaustuba gem which he dons on his divine chest and that the ātmā belongs to him. Since he received that knowledge from emperumān himself, kulaśēkhara āḻvār says that he does not want this human birth.

**ānēṟēḻ venṟān adimait tiṟam allāl** – in order to marry nappinnaip pirātti, emperumān won over seven strong bulls which were a hurdle to his marrying nappinnaip pirātti. āḻvār says that he desires only to carry out service to that emperumān and does not desire this human birth. This is similar to a son desiring to carry out service to his father who is very affectionate towards his mother.

**adimait tiṟamallāl** – the phrase *adimait tiṟam* could be construed to mean either servitude or just as it has been mercifully said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 31-25 “*bhavāmstu saha vaidēhyā girisānuśu ramsyatē* l  
*aham sarvam kariśyāmi jāgratas svapataśca tē* ll” (your highness will be enjoying with pirātti on the mountain ridges; this servitor would like to carry out all services when your highness is awake or asleep), or in tiruvāīmoḻi 3-3-1 “*oḻivil kālam udanāy manni vaḻuvilā adimai seyya vēṇḍum*” (I would like to carry out faultless service to you without any break in time, at all stages, by being with you always), this could be construed as carrying out service at all places, at all times and in all stages, which will make emperumān to be pleased.

**kūnēṟu sangam idattān** – one who has the curved śrī pāncajanyam (divine conch) on his left hand. This indicates that emperumān accepts nitya kainkaryam (permanent service) from those who are keen on carrying out kainkaryam, just like śrī pāncajanya āḻvān.

**kūnēṟu sangam idattān** – śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 4-42 says “*ityēvamuktō mātrēdam rāmō bhrātaramabravīt* l *prājñalim prahvamāsīnam abhivīkśya smayanniva* ll” (after hearing his mother speak like this, śrī rāma looked at his brother lakśmaṇa, who was standing with his palms cupped together \[in *anjali* posture\], and said the following with a smiling face). Just as lakśmaṇa stood obediently with his palms cupped, due to the pride of carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān, śrī pāncajanyam too stood in a curved way, says āḻvār.

**sangam idattān** – since āḻvār saw the conch on the left hand of emperumān, he says *sangam idattān* (conch on the divine left hand). Just as nammāḻvār had mercifully said, after seeing the divine disc on the right hand, as in tiruvāīmoḻi 6-4-9 “*valakkai āḻi idakkaiccangam*” (divine discus on the right hand and divine conch on the left hand) and periyāḻvār had mercifully said after seeing pirātti on the divine chest, as in tiruppallāṇḍu 2 “*nin valamārbinil vāḻginṟa mangai*” (divine pirātti who lives on the right chest), kulaśēkhara āḻvār too says *sangam idattān* after directly seeing śrī pāṇcajanyam.

**than vēngadattu** – since this noble abode is the apt place for accepting service, emperumān considers tiruvēngadam as ‘mine’. Such a hill, as desired by emperumān.

**kōnēri vāḻum kurugāyp piṟappēnē** – just as he desires to see *amānava puruśan* (divine entity who is unlike human), on the shores of virajai river in paramapadam, guiding those who have attained *mōkśam* (śrī vaikuṇṭam), if one were to be involved with arcāvatāram, āḻvār desires to be born as a crane which lives in tirukkōnēri, also called as svāmy puśkariṇi (the divine tank near the temple at tiruvēngadam).

**vāḻum** – just as he desires to live in the huge temple in śrī rangam, āḻvār desires to live in the kōnēri in tirumalai. Instead of using terms such as *irukkum* (remaining) etc, since he has said *vāḻum* (living), āḻvār considers that living there itself will be prosperous.

**kurugāyp piṟappēnē** – āḻvār says that he does not desire human birth, which is capable of distinguishing between body and soul, especially to be born as a king who has the responsibility of protecting his citizens. This is so because it (being born as a human, especially as a king) will be the cause for ego, and hence, will not allow the distinction to be made between body and soul. Instead, he desires to be born as a bird, even if it results because of carrying out sins in previous births, so long as it \[being born as a bird\] is within the boundaries of tirumalai. Since āḻvār, who said “*udal piṟavi yān vēndēn*” (I will not desire human birth) says “*kurugāp piṟappēnē*”(I will desire to be born as a crane), it is clear that he considers being born in a divyadēśam (divine abode) as not a \[worldly\] birth at all.

We will take the 2nd pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 4.2. Anada selvattu

*ānāda selvattu arambaiyargaḷ taṟcūḻavānāḷum selvamum maṇṇarasum yān vēṇḍēntenar pūnjōlait tiruvēngadaccunaiyilmīnāyp piṟakkum vidi udaiyēn āvēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ānāda selvattu – having indestructible wealth (of youth); arambaiyargaḷ – celestial ladies such as rambā et al; than sūḻa – as they surround a person; vān Alum selvamum – the wealth of ruling over svargam (heaven); maṇ arasum – ruling this earth too; yān vēṇḍēn – I will not desire; tēn Ar pū sōlai – in gardens having flowers dripping with honey; tiruvēngadam sunaiyil – in the reservoir present in tiruvēngadam; mīn āy piṟakkum – born, at the least, as a fish; vidi udiyēn āvēnē – let me have the fortune

**Перевод:**

**I will not desire the wealth of ruling svargam which has indestructible (youthful) wealth of celestial ladies such as rambā et al who would surround a person; I will not desire ruling earth too. Instead, let me have the fortune of being born as a fish in the reservoir in tiruvēngadam, which has gardens having flowers dripping with honey.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the first pāsuram, āḻvār said that he does not desire the enjoyments in this world. When asked “The pleasures in svargam (heaven, the land of celestial entities) last for a longer time than these (our earthly) pleasures and are more in quantum than these limited pleasures. What will you do if you get them?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram saying “If I were to get the pleasures in svargam as well as the pleasures here, I will not desire them”*

**ānāda selvattu arambaiyargaḷ** – the term *ānāda selvam* refers to the indestructible wealth of youthfulness. Ladies such as rambā, mēnakā et al, who have such indestructible wealth of youth.

**taṟcūḻa** – surrounding the person; even if the person desires to escape, they will fall head over heels over him and hold him back.

**vānāḷum selvamum maṇṇarasum yān vēṇḍēn** – apart from enjoying the wealth in svargam, even if I am offered the wealth of kingdom which I had rejected in the first pāsuram, I will not accept these. āḻvār is not rejecting these because of any shortcoming in the wealth, but because these do not have the connection with bhāgavatas (followers of emperumān) which is the benefit that he desires.

When queried “What wealth did you desire?” he says

**tēnar puṇjōlatit tiruvēngadac cunaiyil** – the term *tēn* refers both to the honey in flowers and to beetles. Hence, *tēnar pūnjōlai* could be construed as gardens with flowers having lot of honey or gardens with flowers with lot of beetles. tiruvēngadam is surrounded by such gardens.

**mīnāyp piṟakkum vidiyudaiyēn āvēnē** – if as in the first pāsuram, had he been born as a crane, once it grows up a bit and gets to develop its wings, it is capable of trying to fly out of tiruvēngadam. Instead of going out of tiruvēngadam like that, if he were to be born as a fish, it has to live and perish in that same place. Hence, he desires to be born as a fish.

**piṟakkum vidi udaiyēn āvēnē** – it is not necessary to be born immediately as a fish; due to the result of the virtuous deeds done by him (*puṇyam*), if he gets an affirmation that in the next birth, he would be born as a fish, that is sufficient, he says. The term *vidi* refers to the fortune that would surely result from virtuous deeds.

In the next article, we will take up the 3rd pāsuram in this decad.

## 4.3. Pinnitta sadaiyanum

*pinnitta sadaiyānum piramanum indiranumtunnittup pugalariya vaigunda nīḷvāsalminvattac cudarāḻi vēngadakkōn tān umiḻumpon vattil pidittu udanē pugap peṟuvēn āvēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pinnitta sadaiyānum – Sivan who has matted hair; piramanum – and brahmā; indiranum – along with dēvēndiran; tunnittu – pushing each other; pugal ariya – impossible to enter; vaigundam nīḷ vāsal – at the courtyard of tirumalai which is called as bhūlōka (earthly) vaikuṇṭam; min vatttam sudar āḻi – tiruvāḻiyāḻvān (divine disc, sudarśana) who is splendorous like a curved lightning; vēngadakkōn tān – tiruvēngadamudaiyān; umiḻum – to spit saliva; pon vattil pidittu – carrying a golden cup in the hand; udanē puga peṟuvēn āvēnē – let me have the fortune of entering along with those who carry out loyal service to emperumān

**Перевод:**

**While entities such as brahmā, rudran, indran et al cannot even think of entering śrī vaikuṇṭam, they will find it almost next to impossible, even if they keep pushing against each other, while entering bhūlōka vaikuṇṭam, viz. tiruvēngadam. It is such a difficult temple to enter, unless one is a loyal attendant to emperumān there. āḻvār desires to be one such loyal attendant, carrying the golden cup for emperumān to spit out his saliva.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that if he is able to carry out service in line with his paratantra svarūpam (nature of being subservient to emperumān), he would accept the human birth too, which he had rejected earlier.*

**pinnitta sadaiyānum** – one who has his matted hair braided; alternatively, one who has the matted hair hanging behind. Since son is supposed to stand behind his father, it could be construed that Sivan is standing behind his father, brahmā, with matted hair.

**piramanum** – brahmā, who sired the earlier mentioned Sivan

**indiranum** – taittirīya upaniśad nārayaṇavalli 11 says “*tasyā śikhāyā madyē paramātmā vyavastita*: l  
*sa brahmā sa Siva sēndra*: *sōkśara*: *paramasvarāt* ll” (In the middle of that auspicious form, called as *vanhiśikhai*, paramātmā (supreme entity) is present in a splendorous way. He is the antaryāmi (indvelling soul) of brahmā; he is the antaryāmi of Sivan too; he is the antaryāmi of indra too. He is the antaryāmi of the one who is superior (to brahmā et al and those who desire kaivaiya mōkśam \[where ātmā enjoys itself\], one who is unaffected by karma (deeds, both virtuous and sinful) and who is indestructible, the muktātmā (one who has attained śrī vaikuṇṭam after getting liberated from samsāram)). indra is mentioned along with brahmā and Sivan as he is next in the line when brahmā, Siva et al are referred to as emperumān’s possessions.

**tunnittup pugal ariya vaigunda nīḷ vāsal** – in the long courtyard of tirumalai temple, which is referred to as bhūlōka vaikuṇṭam (śrī vaikuṇṭam on earth) where they cannot easily enter, even if they push against each other. In any case they do not qualify to enter paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam). Since tiruvēngadamudaiyān’s sannidi (place of residence) is mentioned next, this meaning is apt.

**min vattac cudar āḻi** – one who has cakkarattāḻvān (sudarśana, the divine disc held by emperumān), who is effulgent like a curved lighting streak and who revolves constantly.

**vēngadakkōn tān umiḻum pon vattil pidittu udanē pugap peṟuvēn āvēnē** – when brahmā and Sivan are pushing one another to enter the temple, emperumān will promulgate his order “Let this person alone, who is carrying out the service of holding the spittoon for me to spit out my saliva, enter”. Since this is emperumān’s order, āḻvār desires to be that person who is one of the loyal attendants for emperumān so that he could enter the temple along with other loyal attendants.

We will take up the 4th pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 4.4. On pavalam velai

*oṇpavaḷam vēlaiyulavu taṇ pāṟkadaluḷkaṇ tuyilum māyōn kaḻaliṇaigaḷ kāṇbadaṟkupaṇ pagarum vaṇḍinangaḷ paṇpādum vēngadattuccenbagamāy niṟkum tiruvudaiyēnāvēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

oṇ pavaḷam vēlai ulavu – bringing splendorous coral to the shore and taking a stroll (with its waves as legs); taṇ pāṟkadaluḷ – in the cool, tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); kaṇ tuyilum – closing his eyes \[in sleep\]; māyōn – emperumān who has amazing activities, his; kaḻal iṇaigaḷ kāṇbadaṟku – to prostrate before his two divine feet; paṇ pagarum vaṇḍu inangaḷ – swarms of beetles, which are singing; paṇ pādum vēngadattu – in tirumalai, which is apt for singing; seṇbagamāy niṟkum – to remain as a seṇbaga (campaka) tree; tiru udaiyēn āvēnē – let me have the fortune

**Перевод:**

**Swarms of beetles are singing joyously in tirumalai hill, which is apt for singing. They are in tirumalai in order to prostrate before the two divine feet of emperumān, who reclines \[usually\] in the milky ocean where the waves will bring shining coral gemstones to the shore and take a walk there. I will consider it to be my fortune if I could \[take birth and\] remain as a campaka tree on that hill.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “If I could carry out servitude to emperumān, even human birth is acceptable”. In this pāsuram he says “Since human form is common to a king also, I do not desire that. If I could be of some use to tiruvēngadamudaiyān, it is acceptable if I could be born as a seṇbaga (campaka) tree”.*

**oṇ pavaḷam vēlai ulavu taṇ pāṟkadaluḷ**– the waves in the milky ocean are bringing to the shore effulgent coral gemstones and taking a stroll in such a way that this will remove the ennui of those who reach that place. Since the term *vēlai* denotes wave as well as shore, we can construe this to mean – milky ocean which flourishes by bringing the splendorous coral gems to the shore

**kaṇ tuyilum māyōn** – one who has amazing activity of displaying the beauty of his reclining posture, in the divine milky ocean, just as it has been mentioned in tirumalai 23 “*kidandadōr kidakkai*” (the unparalleled posture of reclining beautifully).

**kaḻal iṇaigaḷ kāṇbadaṟku** – we cannot go to tiruppāṟkadal to worship his divine feet. In order to overcome this deficiency, \[he has come to tirumalai so that\] we can go to tirumalai. For āḻvār, since tirumalai is more desirable than tiruppāṟkadal, we should consider this interpretation alone.

**paṇ pagarum** – Unlike human beings who have to learn the words of the song and with the help of the one who teaches, learn to sing the words with a tune, the sound raised by beetles becomes music by itself.

**vaṇḍinangaḷ paṇ pādum vēngadattu** – the music generated in tirumalai which syncronises with the music from the humming of beetles. Since āḻvār has used the term *paṇ pādudal* (singing songs) twice as in *paṇ pagarum vaṇḍinangaḷ paṇ pādum*, the vyākyātā (commentator) periyavāccān piḷḷai has said that “āḻvār is saying that in syncronisation with the songs sung by āḻvārs like them, the beetles are singing in tirumalai”.

**seṇbagamāy niṟkum tiruvudaiyan āvēnē** – let the wealth of remaining in tirumalai as a *seṇbaga* (campaka) tree come to me. The implied meaning here is: what I desire is to be with emperumān constantly. Should I get it, it is acceptable to me to remain like a *seṇbaga* tree. I do not desire to remain as a more intelligent human being.

We will next take up the 5th pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.5. Kamba mada yanai

*kamba mada yānaik kaḻuttagattin mēlirunduinbamarum selvamum ivvarasum yān vēṇḍēnemperumān īsan eḻil vēngadamalai mēḷtambagamāy niṟkum tavam uadiyan āvēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kambam – (those who see it) to tremble; madam yānai – an elephant which is in exult; kaḻuttu agattin mēḷ irundu – sitting (with pride) on its neck; inbu amarum selvamum – having wealth with which to enjoy all pleasures; ivvarasum – this royal rule (which is a means for that); yān vēṇḍēn – I will not desire; em perumān – being my svāmy (lord); īsan – and being sarvēśvaran, the lord for all; eḻil vēngadamail mēl – on top of tirumalai; tambagam āy niṟkum – to remain as a pillar tree (without being useful to anyone); tavam udaiyēn āvēnē – let me have the fortune

**Перевод:**

**I will not desire to sit on an exulting elephant which makes others to tremble in fear. I will not desire the royal rule which makes one to sit like this on an elephant and which gives wealth to enjoy all the pleasures in this world. Let me have the fortune of standing as a pillar, on top of tirumalai hills, where my lord and the lord of all stands.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that he would desire to be a seṇbaga tree in tirumalai. There is a possibility that the temple kainkaryaparars (those carrying out service in the temple) would take the flowers from the tree to the temple \[and decorate emperumān with the flowers\]. People may think that he had desired to be a seṇbaga tree with that benefit in mind. Hence, in this pāsuram, he says that he would remain like a pillar, without expecting any benefit.*

**kamba mada yānaik kaḻuttu agattin mēl irundu** – the term *kambam* refers to trembling. Hence, one could construe it as an elephant which has the eminence of making all those who look at it to tremble. ōr, since it is in exult, it has been tied to a pillar \[kambam also refers to a pillar\] so that it cannot escape. In the first option, the term *madam* refers to eminence while in the second, it refers to the elephant being in exult.

**kaḻuttagattin mēlirundu** – such an elephant \[in exult\], while not allowing others to climb atop it, would allow a king to sit on top. The implied meaning is that the king would sit on top of such an elephant which creates fear in others and conduct the elephant the way he likes to.

**inbamarum selvamum ivvarasum yān vēndēn** – I do not desire the wealth, brought about by the pride of sitting on top of such an elephant, to enjoy all the pleasures of royalty and I do not desire ruling the kingdom which it brings.

When asked as to what is it that he needs, he says

**emperumān īsan eḻil vēngadamalai mēl** – Just as rāmānujar had said in his śaraṇāgati gadyam “*akhila jagat svāmin*! *asmat svāmin* !” (Oh, one who is the lord of the entire world! Oh, one who is my lord!), while remaining as the lord for the entire world, emperumān has showered distinguished mercy on me. tiruvēngadamalai where such emperumān is mercifully residing.

**tambagamāy niṟkum tavam udaiyēn āvēnē** – being of no use to anyone, even if I were to sprout on that tiruvēngadamalai hill \[as a plant\] and dry up into a pillar \[over a long period of time\], that is acceptable to me. The opinion is that it is sufficient if one remains in tirumalai; there is no need to be of any benefit to anyone.

**tavam udaiyēn āvēnē** – īsn’t āḻvār thinking that even remaining as a pillar atop tirumalai without being of any use, is the result of virtuous deeds over many births!

We will take up the 6th pāsuram of this decad, next.
