## 5.1. Taru tuyaram tadayel

*taru tuyaram tadāyēl un caraṇallāl śaraṇ illaivirai kuḻuvum malarppoḻil sūḻ vittuvakkōttammānēarisinattāl īnṟa tāy agaṟṟidinum maṟṟu avaḷtanaruḷ ninaindē aḻum kuḻavi adu pōnṟu irundēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

virai kuḻuvum – being very fragrant; malar – having flowers; poḻil sūḻ – surrounded by gardens; vittuvakkōdu ammānē – Oh sarvēśvara (lord of all) who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu \[divyadēśam\]; taru tuyaram – this sorrow, called as samsāram, which you give yourself; tadāyēl – if (you yourself) do not remove; un caraṇ allāl śaraṇ illai – there is no other means, than your divine feet; īnṟa tāy – the mother who gave birth to; arisinattāl – with lot of anger; agaṟṟidinum – even if she pushes away (the child); maṟṟu – again; avaḷ than aruḷ ninaindu – thinking of her affection alone; aḻum kuḻavi aduvē – like that baby only, which is crying; pōnṟu irundēn – was in a similar situation

**Перевод:**

**Oh the lord of all, who has taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu, which is surrounded by gardens having very fragrant flowers! You have to remove my sorrow, of samsāram, which you had given me yourself. I do not have any means other than your divine feet. My situation is much similar to that of a crying child which holds on to the feet of its mother, even if the mother, out of anger, pushes the child away.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that he was in the situation of a weeping child which will hold on to the feet of its mother even when the mother pushes the child away in anger.*

**taru tuyaram tadāyēl** – if you do not remove the sorrow which you had given yourself. While śāstras (religious texts) say that the reason for all the sorrows, which are hurdles, that a person faces, is the karma (past deeds) of the person and that the person has to remove these sorrows through his/her own efforts, how apt is it that āḻvār says that it is emperumān who gave all the sorrows? Since āḻvār knows the true nature of *jīvasvarūpam* (basic nature of jīvātmā), *karmasvarūpam* (basic nature of karma) and *sādanam* (means to overcome sorrows), he says this way. In other words, emperumān may put the blame on us and push us away saying “the jīvātmā himself carried out virtuous and sinful deeds. Hence, he has to carry out deeds through appropriate means to overcome the sorrows”. āḻvār says “I am a possession of emperumān, being a *paratantran* (dependent on emperumān). The result of karma which I carried out is an entity which emperumān can remove, if he wishes to; hence that \[the karma\] is also a *paratantram*. The result for the activity which I carry out through any appropriate means is also granted by emperumān. Thus, it is also a *paratantram*. Hence, it is emperumān who has to remove the sorrow which he gave in the first place”.

In the vyākhyānam mercifully composed by periyavāccān piḷḷai, there is a reference to an incident which happened in śrī rangam – all our preceptors had assembled on the eastern side of the great *tirumaṇamaṇḍapam* (place in front of the sanctum sanctorum) in the śrī rangam temple, where periya perumāḷ has mercifully taken residence. They would discuss about *rahasyārtams* (meanings of the three esoteric concepts in our sampradāyam, namely *tirumantram*, *dvayam* and *carama ślōkam*). There was a discussion one day about “What is the reason for jīvātmā who has been in samsāram for a very long time, to develop an interest in *bhagavat viśayam* (matters related to emperumān)?” Some of them said “It may be a virtuous deed carried out per chance, unknown to himself, by the jīvan”. A preceptor, kidāmbi āccān, said “We hold on to emperumān for everything. The way you people are speaking, it appears that we must have held on to a deity called as virtue!” (The implied meaning is that virtue is, in truth, the feeling of happiness which sprouts in emperumān’s divine mind; there is nothing really called as virtue). To emphasise this, piḷḷai tirunaṟaiyūr araiyar, another preceptor, asked “What is your opinion about virtue? Is it a material different from emperumān?” The summary of the discussion is as given below: when a reason for an event is analysed, it could be said that a cause can be attributed to something when no cause can be explored beyond that. Whichever entity gives a benefit can be said to be the means for the root cause. This cannot be anything (or anyone) other than emperumān. One cannot attribute qualities such as *advēśam* (not hating emperumān), *ānukūlyam* (carrying out deeds which are pleasing to the favourable one) as reasons for developing an interest in bhagavat viśayam. Since emperumān is the ultimate cause for people developing those qualities too, it is only emperumān who is the root cause for developing an interest in bhagavat viśayam. Thus, there is no shortcoming in considering that it is only emperumān who gives the sorrows to jīvātmā.

**tadāyēl** – if you do not remove the sorrow which you created for me. emperumān had said in śrī” bhagavat gītā 7-14 “*daivī hyēśā guṇamayī mama māyā duratyayā* l *māmēva yē prabadyantē māyām ētām tantitē* ll”  
(This māyā (here, the term *māyā* refers to prakruti or primordial matter), which was created by me, the lord, and which has three qualities \[of tamas, rajas and sātvik\] is difficult to cross-over. Those who surrender to me will cross-over this māyā). Since he had said that no one can sever the connection with prakruti which has been created by him and that it can be overcome only through surrendering to him, is it not clear that it is he who has given the sorrow? When someone gives a sorrow, how can someone else remove it? Only the entity who created the sorrow can remove it. It is pertinent to recall here the words of our preceptor piḷḷai tirunaṟaiyūr nambi “Nobody can untie the nest built by a weaver bird. When such is the case, how can this human being, who is as weak as a rat, undo the bond built with karma by the omnipotent sarvēśvaran (supreme entity)? It is only through surrendering to his divine feet that one can get rid of samsāram”.

**un caraṇallāl śaraṇillai** – It is not possible for me, who had created this \[bondage with samsāram\] for a very long time, to remove it. Even though there are some protectors in this word who have been protecting from minor problems, they are not capable of removing this great danger of connection with samsāram which has been there since time immemorial. I cannot consider them as protectors. The actions which I undertake on my own, to remove this connection, are similar to a person who is sinking in the ocean, tying a huge stone around his neck. They \[my actions\] will only make me sink deeper into samsāram. Hence, apart from your divine feet, there is no other means for removing the connection with samsāram.

**virai kuḻuvu malarp poḻil sūḻ vittuvakkōttammānē** – Oh sarvēśvaran, who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu which is surrounded by fragrant gardens!

**virai kuḻuvu malarp poḻil sūḻ** – cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 says “*sarva ganda:* *sarva rasa*:” (One (paramātmā) who has all the smells and who has all the tastes). The supreme being, who is to be attained in paramapadam, has come down to this world and has mercifully taken residence in a place.

**vittuvakkōttammānē** – when emperumān becomes the means for uplifting all the cētanas (ones with knowledge), should he not become a simple arcāvatāra vigraham (idol form in divine places) so that all could attain him?

**ammānē** – just as a mother lies down under a cradle in which her child is sleeping, in order to protect it, emperumān has come to this world, where his children, the jīvātmās, are living. Since āḻvār is totally subservient to emperumān, just like an acētana entity (one which does not have ability to think), being totally under his control, he had said that he does not have the ability to protect himself. Here, by saying *ammānē* he says that only emperumān is qualified to protect him, since emperumān is the owner and he is the owned. When emperumān becomes the means for jīvātmās to get uplifted, is it not necessary that he becomes the owner, and they, the owned? When the infant gets a disease, the mother takes the medicines. Since he is going to make a mother as an allegory in the latter part of the pāsuram, he is referring to emperumān as *ammānē* here, to reflect the relationship between him and emperumān.

In the second part of the pāsuram, he is taking a weeping child, which is holding on to its mother who had pushed it away in anger, as an illustration for himself.

**arisinattāl ….. irundēnē** – in the first part of the pāsuram, he had affirmed that it is only emperumān who protects him. Is it appropriate here to say “*īnṟa tāy agaṟṟidinum*” conveying the meaning that emperumān has a shortcoming? Since he is firm that it is only emperumān who protects him and since there was a delay in getting the result of kainkaryam, and since he knows the relationship between him and emperumān, being a *cētana* (one who could think), he has the right to point out the shortcoming in emperumān. nammāḻvār, who had said in tiruvāīmoḻi 2-9-4 “*tanakkēyāga enaikkoḷḷumīdē*” (emperumān should accept him as an *acētana* and use him in any way that emperumān likes), said immediately “*enakkē kaṇṇanai yān koḷ siṟappu*” (the great benefit that I desire from emperumān appropriate to my nature) indicating that he wants kainkaryam from emperumān which will please emperumān. Even though both acit, which does not have knowledge, and cit, which has knowledge, are subservient to emperumān, it is only cit which has the necessity to attain benefit which will please emperumān.

**arisinattāl** – having the anger to cut the child, looking at the hyperactive child.

**īnṟa tāy** – instead of saying *vaḷartta tāy* (foster mother), since āḻvār says *īnṟa tāy* (biological mother), he brings out the relationship between him and emperumān.

**agaṟṟidinum** – since he says even if she pushes away, āḻvār opines that she, being the biological mother, will not push aside the child. But even if she pushes aside…. Usage of the term *īnṟa tāy* denotes the difficulties which the mother had undergone for the sake of the child: various rituals such as fasting etc which the mother would have undertaken before conceiving the child, carrying the child in her womb for ten months, undergoing pains during labour etc.

**agaṟṟidinum** – since she had undergone difficulties even before the child was born, the mother will only think that the child should become very distinguished and eminent in later years and not push the child away. This term indicates the situation which arises because the mother gets angry and tries to push the child away. Through this illustration, āḻvār shows that since emperumān had been like a mother to him, even if he gives up āḻvār \[due to anger\] he does not have any refuge other than emperumān.

**maṟṟavaḷ than aruḷ ninaindē aḻum kuḻavi** – even if the mother pushes away the child, will the child go to another person, seeking mercy? The child holds on to the legs of the mother alone, who had nursed it from infancy stage. In common parlance it is said that where there is anger, good characteristic will also be present. The mother has unbounded love towards the child; when the child becomes adamant, she shows her anger towards the child. Other women will not show such unbounded love towards that child; hence, they will not show anger too, towards the child.

In the vyākyanam written mercifully by periyavāccān piḷḷai, an incident has been quoted, aligned to this logic. mudaliyāṇḍān, one of the primary disciples of bhagavat rāmānujar, had a disciple, nambi tiruvaḻudi vaḷanādu dāsar. One day, mudaliāṇḍān kicked him in anger and asked him to move out of his place. dāsar sat on the *tiṇṇai* (a low pedestal on the front courtyard of houses in a village, where people sit) for a whole day, without eating anything. When mudaliyāṇḍān was about to take food on the next day, he remembered his disciple, dāsar. He asked other disciples about him. They said that he was sitting on the *tiṇṇai*, without eating anything. He called his disciple and asked him “Why are you staying put on the tiṇṇai and not going anywhere?” dāsar responded, saying “If a person feeds a dog few morsels of food on one day, even if he drives it away, it will not go anywhere else and keep roaming near that house itself. What other refuge do I have?”

**aḻum kuḻavi aduvē pōnṟu irundēnē** – when the mother pushes a child away, no one else can comfort the child and make it stop from crying. Unless the mother, who had nursed it from infancy days, and who pushed the child away, comforts it, the child will not stop crying.

āḷavandār, one of our preceptors, had mercifully explained this pāsuram in his stōtra ratnam 28<sup>th</sup> ślōkam “*nirāsakasyāpi’ na tāvadutsahē mahēśa*! *hātum tava pāda pankajam* l *ruśā nirastō’pi śiśu*: *stanandayō na jātu mātuś caraṇau jihāsati* ll” (Oh sarvēśvara! Even if you push me away, I will not have the temerity to let go of your divine lotus-like feet. Will an infant, which is being nursed by its mother, leave the feet of its mother, even if she pushes it away?) This āḻvār himself, in his perumāḷ tirumoḻi 7-4 had mercifully said “*aḷavil piḷḷaimai inbattai iḻanda pāviyēn*” (I had lost the unbounded ecstasy of enjoying infancy) bringing out the strong bond between the infant and its mother through the wailing of dēvaki \[who had lost the ecstasy of bringing up kriṣṇa\]. An infant will not know its father and others related through blood, but will know only its mother. Hence, the term *aḻum kuḻavi* here, refers only to an infant still in nursing stage. The summary of this pāsuram, thus, is that “jīvātmā is like an infant towards emperumān. It is emperumān alone who is the means for the benefits that accrue to this jīvātmā”.

We will take up the 2nd pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 5.6. Sendalale vandu

*sendaḻalē vandu aḻalaic ceydidinum sengamalamandaram sēr vengadirōṟkkallāl alarāvālvenduyar vīttāvidinum vittuvakkōttammā! unandamil sīrkkallāl agam kuḻaiya māttēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sem taḻalē vandu – reddish fire approaching; aḻalaic ceydidinum – even if it creates warmth; sem kamalam – reddish lotus flower; andaram sēr vem kadirōrkku allāl – will blossom only for the warm rays coming from sun, which is shining brightly in the sky; alarā – \[it\] will not blossom (for that reddish fire); vittuvakkōttammā – Oh my lord, who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu!; vīttāvidinum – even if you do not remove; un andam il sīrkku allāl – apart from your auspicious qualities which are without any boundary; agam kuḻaiya māttēn – I will not melt (for anything else)

**Перевод:**

**Lotus flower will blossom only if the warm rays from the sun, which is shining brightly in the sky, fall on it. It will not blossom if reddish fire is stoked near it. Oh my lord, who has taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu! My heart will not melt for anything other than your auspicious qualities, even if you do not remove my cruel sins.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that just like lotus flower will not blossom other than with sun, his heart will not blossom other than with emperumān’s auspicious qualities.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sendaḻalē vandu aḻalaic ceydidinum** – if some people think that lotus is blossoming only because of the heat emitted by the rays of sun, and keep red hot fire near the lotus and stoke that fire ….

**sengamalam andaram sēr vengadirōṟkallāl alarāvāl** – lotus will blossom only due to the warm rays coming from sun which is at a long distance away and will not blossom due to the hot fire kept near it.

**venduyar vīttāvidinum vittuvakkōttammā** – the cruel, immeasurable sins, which I have accumulated, have to be overcome only through experiencing the results of these sins. Even if you, who have come to destroy them and make my heart to blossom, disregard me

**un andam il sīrkku allāl** – apart from the unbounded, auspicious qualities of yours

**agam kuḻiya māttēnē** – my heart, which is like a lotus, will not blossom.

We will consider the 7th pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 5.7. Ettanaiyum van maranda

*ettanaiyum vān maṟanda kālattum paingūḻgaḷmaitteḻunda māmugilē pārttirukkum maṟṟavai pōlmeyttuyar vīttāvidinum vittuvakkōttammā! ensittam un pālē vaippan adiyēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vittuvakkōttammā – Oh my lord, who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu; vān – clouds; ettanaiyum maṟanda kālattum – even if it had not rained for a long time; paim kūḻgaḷ – crops, which are green; maittu eḻunda māmugilē pārttu irukkum – will look for huge clouds which are dark; avai pōl – just like those crops; mey tuyar vīttāvidinum – even if you do not remove my sorrows which have to be necessarily experienced in order to get rid of; adiyēn – I, your servitor; en sittam un pālē miga vaippan – will engage my mind only towards you

**Перевод:**

**Even if clouds had not rained for a long time, crops will look up to the dark clouds only, for their nourishment. On my lord, who has taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu! In the same way, my mind will engage only with you, even if you do not remove my sorrows which have to be got rid of only through experiencing them.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that just like crops will look up only to the rain bearing clouds, even if there is severe drought without any rains, my heart will look up to you only, even if you do not remove my sorrows.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ettanaiyum vān maṟanda kālattum paim kūḻgaḷ** – even during the times when the rain bearing clouds refused to rain, the crops which are green…..

**maitteḻunda māmugilē pārttirukkum maṟṟavai pōl** – they will only look up to the dark clouds in the sky and will not know to go in search of ponds, puddles etc.

**meyttuyar vīttāvidinum vittuvakkōttammā** – brahmaivaivartam prakruti kāṇḍam 26-70 says “*avaśyam anubhōktavyam krutam karma śubhāśubham* l *nābhuktam kśīyatē karma kalpakōti śatairapi* ll” (The results of puṇya and pāpa karma (good and bad deeds) carried out by a person have to be necessarily experienced. Even if a hundred crore kalpam (extraordinarily long period of time; a *kalpam* is one morning time of brahmā) passes, karma will not disappear without experiencing). In line with this ślōkam, *meyttuyar* is the bundle of sins whose results have to be necessarily experienced in order to get rid of them. emperumān has mercifully arrived at divine abodes in order to sever the connection with samsāram for those who attain him. Even if you do not sever such a connection…..

**en sittam un pālē miga vaippan adiyēnē** – every time when it appears that you, who are supposed to protect me, do not protect me, my heart engages with you more and more.

Next, we will take up the 8th pāsuram of this decad.

## 5.8. Tokkilangu arellam

*tokku ilangu āṟellām parandōdit todu kadalēpukkanṟip puṟam niṟka māttāda maṟṟavai pōlmikkilangu mugil tiṟattāy! vittuvakkōttammā! unpukkilangu sīrallāl pukkilan kāṇ puṇṇiyanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tokku ilangu āṟellām – all the rivers which flow with abundance of water; parandu ōdi – (at all places) running expansively; todu kadalē pukku anṟi – other than reaching the deep ocean; puṟam niṟka māttāda – will not reach any other place; mikku ilangu – shining radiantly; mugil niṟattāy – Oh one who is like a dark cloud!; vittuvakkōttammā – Oh my lord who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu!; puṇṇiyanē – Oh the controller of the earlier carried out virtuous deeds too!; avai pōl – like those rivers; pukku ilangu – entering (my mind) and shining radiantly; un sīr allāl – other than your auspicious qualities; pukkilan – I will not immerse myself

**Перевод:**

**Rivers which flow expansively through various places with abundance of water, will not rest until they reach an ocean. Oh one who is shining radiantly like a dark cloud! Oh one who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu! Oh one who is the controller of virtuous deeds \[done by everyone\]! Just like those rivers, my mind too will not immerse in anything other than the ocean of your auspicious qualities which shine radiantly, after entering my mind.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that just like rivers which run expansively all over the place, finally reach an ocean, his mind, which is a river, engages with the ocean of emperumān’s auspicious qualities.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tokku ilangu āṟellām parandu ōdi** – rain water, gathered from heavy rains falling over various places, run expansively in various places.

**todu kadalē pukkanṟip puṟam niṟka māttāda maṟṟavai pōl** – other than reaching deep ocean, they will not stop elsewhere. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam ślōkam 1-16 “*sarvadā’bhigata*: *sadbhi*: *samudra iva sindubhi*: ” (Just like rivers attain an ocean, eminent people attained śrī rāmapirān), similar to rivers uniting with ocean, eminent people also united with śrī rāma. There was no completeness for śrī rāma because those eminent people attained him, neither was there any deficiency for him if they did not attain him. In a similar vein, the ocean did not become full because the rivers reached it, neither would it become deficient if they did not reach it. Don’t the rivers attain the ocean since they do not stop anywhere else!

Through the second half of the pāsuram, āḻvār says that he too attains emperumān since he cannot sustain himself anywhere else.

**mikkilangu mugil niṟattāy** – Oh one who has the complexion of a radiant dark cloud!

**vittuvakkōttammā** – the divine abode of tiruvittuvakkōdu is in itself the mountain where that dark cloud has settled.

**un pukkilangu sīr allāl pukkilan kāṇ** – I will only experience your auspicious qualities which will shine brighter and brighter as they are experienced; I will not engage with the qualities of others which are worthless.

What is the reason for this?

**puṇṇiyanē** – As per etymology, emperumān is called as *puṇyan* – *pumāmsam nayati iti puṇya*: (emperumān is called as *puṇyan* because he carries the jīvātmā with him). Since the first virtuous deed is emperumān himself with emperumān carrying āḻvār towards him, the opinion here is that āḻvār will engage only with the auspicious qualities of emperumān and not with anyone else’s qualities.

We will next consider the 9th pāsuram of this decad.

## 5.9. Ninnaiye tan vendi

*ninnaiyē tān vēṇḍi neeḷ selvam vēṇḍādāntannaiyē tān vēṇḍum selvam pōl māyattālminnaiyē sēr tigiri vittuvakkōttammā!ninnaiyē tān vēṇḍi niṟpan adiyēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

minnaiyē sēr – matching the radiance of lightning; tigiri – having cakrāyudam (divine weapon, disc); vittuvakkōttammā – Oh one who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu!; ninnaiyē tān vēṇḍi – desiring only you; neeḷ selvam vēṇḍādāṇ tannaiyē – one who is not desirous of opulence, which is permanent; tān vēṇḍum – attaining him on its own; selvam pōl – like wealth; māyattāl – due to your knowledge (even if you disregard me); adiyēn – this servitor \[I\]; ninnaiyē tāṇ vēṇḍi niṟpan – I will remain, desirous of attaining you

**Перевод:**

**Oh the lord, who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu! Oh one who has the divine weapon, disc, whose radiance is similar to that of lightning! Just as wealth will attain, on its own, a person who does not have any desire for it, I will remain desirous of attaining you, even if you disregard me, due to your knowledge.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram āḻvār says that just as wealth seeks out and attains the person who did not want it, I will attain you on my own even if you push me away due to hatred.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ninnaiyē tān vēṇḍi neeḷ nelvam vēṇḍādān tannaiyē tān vēṇḍum selvam pōl** – this verse is an allegory for the meaning which has been mentioned in the second part of the pāsuram. The meaning for this verse is – opulence will seek out and attain *prapannas* (those who have surrendered to emperumān) who will desire only you and will despise wealth, even if it is as huge as that owned by kubēran (the lord of wealth). The implied meaning is that if such a prapannan has the firmness in mind that he will not spend it in bad ways and go astray, *periya pirātti* (the main lord for wealth) will bestow on that person the most eminent wealth. It is pertinent to note here that since kulaśēkhara āḻvār was one such, he attained the position of a king himself. Alternatively, instead of considering it as material wealth, we could also say that the most eminent wealth of mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) itself will seek such an eminent person and attain him.

**minnaiyē sēr tigiri vittuvakkōttammā** – Oh the lord of tiruvittuvakkōdu, who has the most splendorous sudarśanāḻvān, who is effulgent like lightning on his hand, in order to bring me to the correct path and protect me, just as it has been said by nammāḻvār in periya tiruvandādi pāsuram 87 “*eppōdum kai kaḻalā nēmiyān nam mēl vinai kadivān*” (one who always holds the divine discuss in his hand in order to rid us of our sins and hurdles)!

**māyattāl** – In the allegory, it has been said *neeḷ selvam vēṇḍādān*. Aligned to this, we have to consider the meaning for this term to be – even if you reject me due to your amazing *sankalpam* (vow or thought) in order to correct me. The opinion is that since emperumān has the divine weapon of discus on his hand in order to protect me, even if you reject me, that action is also only towards correcting me. In line with the glossary *māyā vayunam jñānam*, the term *māyā* here refers to the knowledge of emperumān which is in the form of his *sankalpam*.

**ninnaiyē tān vēṇḍi niṟpan adiyēnē** – just as wealth seeks out and attains the prapannan who had despised and rejected it, āḻvār says that as emperumān rejects him more and more, he will hold on to emperumān more and more firmly.

In the *arumpadavurai* (explanatory notes) for this vyākhyānam, it has been shown that for the verse *neeḷ selvam vēṇḍādān tannaiyē tān vēṇḍum selvam pōl* the wealth of mōkśam \[śrī vaikuṇṭam\] will seek out and attain such eminent entities as hanuman who did not want the wealth of mōkśam but only the divine feet of śrī rāmapirān and toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār, who did not want mōkśam but wanted only śrī ranganātan.

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 5.10. Vittuvakkottamma ni

*vittuvakkōttammā ! nī vēṇḍāyē āyidinummaṟṟārum paṟṟilēn enṟu avanait tāḷ nayandakoṟṟavēl tānaik kulasēkaran sonnanaṟṟamiḻ pattum vallār naṇṇār naragamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vittuvakkōttammā – Oh sarvēśvaran (lord of all) who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvittuvakkōdu!; nī vēṇḍāyē āyidinum – even when you did not like (me); maṟṟu ārum paṟṟilēn enṟu – \[I\] will not take refuge under anyone else, saying this; avanait tāḷ nayanda – having desire towards the divine feet of that sarvēśvaran; koṟṟam vēl tānai – one who has the victorious spear and army; kulasēkaran sonna – mercifully composed by kulaśēkhara āḻvār; nal tamiḻ pattum – the ten pāsurams which have eminence of meanings; vallār – those who have learnt; naragam naṇṇār – will not take birth in this samsāram (even if they have committed cruel sins)

**Перевод:**

**sarvēśvaran has mercifully taken residence in the divine abode of tiruvittuvakkōdu. kulaśēkhara āḻvār, who has victorious spear and army, mercifully composed ten pāsurams on that emperumān, saying that he will not take refuge under anyone else, even if emperumān despises and rejects him. Those who learn these ten pāsurams will not have birth again, in this samsāram.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār brings the decad to an end by mentioning the benefits which will accrue to those who learn this decad in which he had said that even if emperumān despises and rejects him, there is no other refuge for him and that he will hold on to the divine feet of emperumān.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vittuvakkōttammā nī vēṇḍāyēyādinum** – there is no possibility for you, who have mercifully taken residence in vittuvakkōdu only to protect me, to reject me. Even if you reject me in order to correct me ….

**maṟṟārum paṟṟilēn enṟu avanait tāḷ nayanda** – desirous of his divine feet, saying “There is no other refuge; I have nowhere else to go”

**koṟṟavēl tānai kulasēkaran sonna** – this padigam (collection of ten pāsurams) has been composed by kulaśēkharap perumāḷ who has the spear and the army which will defeat his enemy along with his supporting army and bring him victory. The implied meaning here is that just as spear and soldiers are his assistants in defeating his enemies, his quality of *ananyagatitvam* (having nowhere else to go for taking refuge) is his assistant in attaining emperumān.

**naṟṟamiḻ pattum vallār** – even though the ocean is deep, pearls and other treasures which it holds are clearly visible at depths. In the same way, this padigam has simple words in tamiḻ which hold eminent meanings. Those who learn such a padigam well ….

**naṇṇār naragamē** – even if they have carried out sins which are responsible for toiling repeatedly in samsāram, they will not be born again here. The term *naragam* refers to samsāram which creates deeper sorrow than hell.

This completes the 5th decad. We will take up the pravēśam (Introduction) to 6th decad, in the next article.

## 6.1. Er malarp pungulal

*ēr malarp pūnguḻal āyar mādar enaippalaruḷḷa ivvūril unṟanmārvu taḻuvdaṟ(ku) āsaiyinmai aṟīndaṟindē unṟan poyyaik kēttukūrmaḻaipōl panikkūdal eydik kūsi nadungi yamunaiyāṟṟilvārmaṇaṟkunṟil pularaninṟēn vāsudēvā! un varavu pārttē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāsudēvā – Oh the son of vasudēvar!; ēr malar pū kuḻal āyar mādar – covherd women with beautiful flowers and fragrant hair; enai palar uḷḷa – many such persons living; i ūril – in this tiruvāyppādi; un than mārvu taḻuvudaṟku – to hug your divine chest; āsai inmai aṟīndu aṟindē – knowing fully well the lack of such desire; un than poyyaik kēttu – listening to your false words; kūr maḻai pōl pani kūdal eydi – getting caught in the cool dew which is pouring like heavy rain; kūsi – feeling shy (about being seen by others); nadungi – trembling (with fear and cold); yamunai āṟṟil – in yamunā river; vār maṇal kunṟil – on a huge mound; un varavu pārttu – expecting your arrival; pulara ninṟēn – I was standing until dawn

**Перевод:**

**Oh the son of vasudēva! There are many women in this āyarppādi with beautiful flowers and fragrant hair. Knowing fully well that I do not have the desire to hug your divine chest, listening to your false words, I came to this huge mound in the middle of river yamunā, waiting for your arrival in the dew which is pouring like heavy rain, feeling shy, trembling with fear and shyness, until the early hours of the morning.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) kaṇṇan had told a gōpikā (covherd girl) to wait for him on the mound in the river yamunā. Despite waiting for him through the night, he had not come there. When he came in the early hours of the morning, she got praṇaya rōśam (anger arising out of affection). This pāsuram describes her love-quarrel towards him.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ērmalarp pūnguḻal āyar mādar** – covherd girls, with beautiful flowers, with their hair giving fragrance to the flowers

**enaip palar uḷḷa ivvūril** – in this tiruvāyppādi, where many such people have gathered

**undan mārvu taḻuvudaṟku āsaiyinmai aṟindu aṟīndē** – Knowing fully well that “This town has many women. You are a person common to all the women. Hence, it is not proper on my part to desire that only I should hug you”

**un than poyyaik kēttu** – one false word which you said made me not to understand your svarūpam (basic nature) that you are common to everyone. What is that false word? “Do I have a refuge other than you? Do I know a girl other than you?” Hearing your words which you said as if you were obedient to me…

**kūr maḻai pōl panik kūdal eydi** – getting caught in the cillness of dew which was pouring like heavy rains

**kūsi** – feeling ashamed, thinking “Will anyone see me waiting like this, for you?”

**nadungi** – trembling, out of fear and the cillness

**yamunaiyāṟṟil** – did I stand in a secluded place which no one knows? Did I not stand in the yamunā river, which everyone frequents?

**vār maṇal kunṟil pulara ninṟēn** – it is accepted that I had to stand. Did I stand in a narrow street where I could stand alone? Did I not stand on a huge mound on the shore of river yamunā where people gather together, play and make merry? Since it is a place frequented by many, instead of people thinking that I have also come here to play, did I not make it obvious to them that I have come here to unite with kaṇṇan and be happy?

**vāsudēvā** – Oh the son of vasudēva! I did not stand here with faith in you, a habitual liar, when someone would ask me “Why are you standing here?” Since you are the son of vasudēvar, who does not know lies, and who always speaks truth, I believed you would also speak only the truth.

**un varavu pārttē** – I stood like this, with the desire of seeing your beauty.

We will next take up the 2nd pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.2. Kendai on kan

*keṇḍai oṇ kaṇmadavāḷ orutti kīḻaiyagattut tayir kadaiyakkaṇ(du) ollai nānum kadaivan enṟu kaḷḷa viḻiyai viḻittup pukku                    vaṇḍamar pūnguḻal tāḻndulāva vāṇmugam vērppac cevvāy tudippattaṇḍayir nī kadainditta vaṇṇam dāmōdarā! meyyaṟivan nānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

dāmōdarā – Oh dāmōdarā!; kīḻai agattu – on the house, to the east (of my house); keṇḍai oṇ kaṇ – one who has eyes similar to a shining kayal (carp) fish; madavāḷ orutti – a beautiful girl; tayir kadaiya kaṇḍu – seeing her, churning curd (alone); nānum – I too (along with you); ollai kadaivan enṟu – will churn immediately, saying so; kaḷḷam viḻiyai viḻittu – with deceptive look; pukku – approaching and entering her place; vaṇḍu amar pū kuḻal – the hair, with flowers, on which beetles are sitting; tāḻndu ulāva – swaying, in a lowered position; vāḷ mugam vērppa – with the radiant face perspiring; sem vāy tudippa – with the red lips quivering; taṇ tayir – cold curd; nī kadainditta vaṇṇam – the way you churned; nān mey aṟivan – I know the truth, fully

**Перевод:**

**Oh dāmōdarā! There is a beautiful girl, with eyes matching shining carp fish, who lives to the east of my house. You saw her churning curd, all alone. Approaching and entering her house, with a deceptive look, you told her that you will help her immediately, to churn the curd. I really know \[what you are up to\] from the way you churned, with your radiant face perspiring, your reddish lips quivering and your hair, with beetles sitting on the flowers, swaying and lowering.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār speaks in the voice of another gōpi (covherd girl).*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**keṇḍai oṇ kaṇ madavāḷ orutti** – a covherd girl having beautiful eyes, similar to carp, and having an ignorance which will make her believe whatever her beloved told her

**kīḻaiyagattu** – āḻvār speaks imagining himself to be a covherd girl, thinking that he has a house in āyppādi, and narrating an incident happening in a house located to the east of his (her) house. He says “Did you not shamelessly loiter near my house!” He describes now that event.

**tayir kadaiyak kaṇḍu** – in order to carry out a mischievous act, would not kaṇṇan seek out girls who are churning curd, all alone? Locating such a girl, he ran towards that house, as if he had attained a most unattainable benefit.

**ollai nānum kadaivan enṟu** – He entered saying “If you churn the curd alone, butter will not gather. To get the butter faster, I will also churn, along with you”. Just as nammāḻvār had mercifully said in tiruvāimoḻi 7-1-7 “*anṟu dēvar asurar vānga alai kadal aravamaḷāvi*” (once upon a time, with the celestial and demon entities churning, in the ocean with rising waves, using vāsuki, the snake, as the rope for churning), on that day, since it was someone else’s interest, emperumān had allowed the celestial and demonic entities to churn the ocean until they became tired; then, he stood on both sides of the churning staff and churned the ocean himself. Here, since he was churning in order to enjoy the beauty of the covherd girl, he churned the curd with the girl standing on one side, and himself, on the other side.

**kaḷḷa viḻiyai viḻittup pukku** – since she is an innocent girl, her vision, activity and thought will be focussed on churning the curd. Since he is one with deceptive eyes, his vision, activity and thought will be on enjoying her beauty, with churning of curd, as an excuse. Since she is an innocent girl, looking at his deceptive eyes and vision, she believed that he had really come to help her to churn the curd.

**vaṇḍamar pūnguḻal tāḻndulāva vān mugam vērppac ceyya vāy tudippa** – while churning the curd, in order to attract her attention, he carried out activities due to which his hair, on which he had donned flowers, got lowered and swayed in the air; his radiant face started perspiring; his reddish lips quivered. If all his activities were to be described, they will be as huge as a mahābhārata collection of verses.

**vaṇḍamar pūnguḻal tāḻndulāva** – what suffering did the beetles undergo, when they came believing that your hair will be a safe place for them?

**vāḷ mugam vērppa** – just as how pearls will appear on a lotus petal, drops of perspiration settled on his lotus-like face.

**cevvāy tudippa** – his lips were quivering. āren’t all these, symbolic of what happens when a beloved is enjoying his lady! In tamiḻ literature, such an exchange of emotions between a man and woman is termed as ‘*sunaiyādal*’. The implied meaning is that such an exchange took place here.

**taṇ tayrl nī kadainditta vaṇṇam** – all those activities which you indulged in desirously, when you entered to churn curd. When the covherd girl \[āḻvār\] said this, kaṇṇan said “You are unnecessarily blaming me! I did not do anything like this”

**dāmōdarā! meyyaṟivan nānē** – she says that if he could hide the scars on his stomach caused by the rope when his mother tied him just as it has been mentioned in the famous pāsuram *kaṇṇinuṇ siṟuttāmbināl* (with short ropes tied together), he could hide these activities too. *dāmōdaran* – one tied with rope on his stomach.

We will take up pāsuram 6,3, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 6.3. Karumalark kundal

*karumalark kūndal orutti tannaik kadaikkaṇitt(u) āngē orutti tanpālmaruvi manam vaittu maṟṟottikk(u) uraittoru pēdaikkup poy kuṟīttupuri kuḻal mangai orutti tannaip puṇardi avaḷukkum meyyanallaimarudiṟūttāy! un vaḷarttiyūdē vaḷarginṟadāl unṟan māyaidānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

malar – being with flowers; karu kūndal – one having dark locks; orutti tannai – a girl; kadaik kaṇittu – glancing at her; āngē – when such is the situation; orutti than pāl – to another girl; manam maruvi vaittu – keeping the mind on her firmly; maṟṟoruttikku – to another girl; oru pēdaikku – to another girl who is ignorant; poy kuṟittu – indicating a false place (to await him there); puri kuḻal mangai orutti tannai – with a young girl having beautiful hair; puṇardi – you united; avaḷukkum – to that girl too; meyyan allai – you said only lies; un valarttiyūdē – along with your growing years; un than māyai – your deceptiveness too; vaḷarginṟadu Al – Alas! It is growing

**Перевод:**

**You were glancing at a girl with dark locks and flowers. You kept your mind firmly on another girl. To an ignorant girl you said “I am your servitor”. To a fourth girl, you indicated a place, asking her to wait for you there. You united with a young girl having beautiful hair, but to her too, you spoke only lies. Oh one who pulled down two maruda trees while being very young! Along with your growing years, your deceptiveness is growing too.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Words of another covherd girl are mentioned in this pāsuram.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**karumalarkkūndal orutti tannai kadaik kaṇittu** – glancing at a girl who had dark, smooth hair, decked up with flowers, making it clear that he had been won over by her hair. Had he looked at her directly, it would have been a normal event. But glancing at her brings in a sense of deception.

**āngē orutti than pāl maruvi manam vaittu** – even as he was glancing at this girl, he was thinking firmly of another girl, making it appear that he does not know of anyone other than her. His action and mind were not in syncronisation.

**maṟṟoruttikku uraittu** – of the three faculties of mind, activity and word, the third one, word, was not aligned to either of the first two girls, but it was towards a third girl, telling her “I am your servitor”.

**oru pēdaikkup poy kuṟittu** – to another girl who had already known him and believed his words, he said “You go to this place and wait for me there”, indicating a place where he would meet her.

**puri kuḻal mangai orutti tannai puṇardi** – you were embracing another girl who had diśevelled hair, since she did not bother much about her hair, but was appropriate for enjoyment.

**avaḷukkum meyyan allai** – even embracing her was an act of falsehood.

**marūdiṟuttāy** – aren’t you the one who, even as a little boy, touched two maruda trees and snapped them? The implied meaning here is that it was his wont to harass those who he had touched, right from his childhood days.

**un vaḷarttiyūdē vaḷarginṟadāl undan māyaidānē** – along with your growing years, aren’t your deceptive activities too growing? It is important to note here that emperumān is supreme and common to everyone. All the others are in the status of his consort and like to be touched by him. pāsurams such as these would superficially appear to be blasphemy but in truth, they are words of praise.

We will take up the 4th pāsuram of the 6th decad, next.

## 6.4. Tay mulaip palil

*tāy mulaip pālil amudu irukkat tavaḻndu taḷarnadai ittuc cenṟupēy mulai vāy vaittu nanjai uṇḍu pittan enṟē piṟar ēsa ninṟāyāymigu kādalō(du) yān iruppa yān ida vanda en tūdiyōdēnī migu bōgattai nangu ugandāy aduvum un kōrambukku ēṟkumanṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tāy – the mother, yaśōdaip pirātti’s; mulaiyil – in (her) bosom; pāl amudu irukka – even while nectar like milk was available (not drinking that); tavaḻndu – crawling; taḷar nadai ittuc cenṟu – walking unsteadily; pēy mulai vāy vaittu – nursing from the bosom of demon pūtanā; nanjai uṇḍu – drinking milk mixed with poison; pittan enṟē – called as mad; piṟar ēsa ninṟāy – oh one who stood to be reviled by the people of the town; yān – I; āy migu kādalōdu iruppa – remaining with lot of affection; nī – you; yān vida vanda – one who came as my (messenger); en tūdiyōdē – with my servant; migu bōgattai – lot of pleasure; nangu ugandāy – experienced well; aduvum – even that; un kōrambukku – for your mischief

**Перевод:**

**While your mother had nectar like milk in her bosom, (not drinking it) you went crawling and with unsteady walk, to the demon pūtanā to drink her milk which was mixed with poison, to be reviled by the people of the town as being mad. While I was waiting with deep affection for you, you enjoyed with my servant who I had sent to you as my messenger. īsn’t that too appropriate, for your mischievous nature?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This pāsuram is yet another covherd girl’s words.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tāy mulaippālil amudu irukka** – your mother observed various rituals in order to conceive you, carried you in her womb for ten months, sustained herself when you were nursing and felt sorrowful and was unable to sustain when you veren’t nursing. When she was having nectar like milk in her bosom in order to nurture you . . . .

**tavaḻndu taḷar nadai ittuc cenṟu** – initially you crawled and later, when that became difficult, you walked on unsteady feet to \[where pūtanā was\]

**pēy mulai vāy vaittu nanjai uṇḍu** – kept your mouth on the bosom of the demon pūtanā who had no affection for you and drank her milk which was capable of killing you

**pittan enṟē piṟar ēsa ninṟāy** – you stood to be reviled by the common people who said that you are incapable of distinguishing between good, which is to be accepted, and bad, which is to be rejected.

Those things were done when I was a child. Now, I am grown up. What is the foolish act which I indulged in now, if you ask …

**āy migu kādalodu yān iruppa yān ida vanda en tūdiyōdē nī migu bōgattai nangu ugandāy** – I was waiting for you with lot of love. I had sent my servant as my messenger to bring you to me. Did you not enjoy pleasure with her? The word *āy* refers to *āyam* which means gold. *āy migu kādalodu* thus means a person who is waiting with desire to attain you, having love for you which will change the colour \[of the person\] like gold.

**yān iruppa nangu ugandāy** – while I was waiting for you, with love, with my colour drained out \[due to separation from you\] you desired her, sent by me to you as my messenger, since she was my messenger. Even though you were thinking of enjoying her, she did not have any thought of embracing you. Hence, she would have become unsteady in her thoughts. Despite that, you wanted to hug her. What should I think of you!

**aduvum un kōrambukku ēṟkumanṟē** – the word *kōrambu* refers to mischief. The meaning here is that you will get a bad name of doing mischief; there is no benefit for you.

We will next take up pāsuram 6.5

## 6.5. Minnotta nun idaiyal

*minnotta nuṇṇidaiyāḷaik koṇḍu vīngiruḷvāy enṟan vīdiyōdēponnotta ādai kukkūdalittu pōginṟa pōdu nān kaṇḍu ninṟēnkaṇṇuṟṟavaḷai nī kaṇṇālittuk kaiviḷikkinṟadum kaṇḍē ninṟēnennukk(u) avaḷai vittingu vandāy innam angē nada nambi! nīyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

minnotta – like lightning; nuṇṇidaiyāḷai – a girl with slender waist; koṇḍu – hugging \[her\]; vīngu iruḷ vāy – in pitch darkness; pon otta ādai – with golden coloured dress; kukkūdal ittu – covering the head; en than vīdi ūdē – through my street; pōginṟa pōdu – when you were going (with her); nān kaṇḍu ninṟēn – I was looking at you; kaṇṇuṟṟavaḷai – (further) another girl whom you saw; kaṇṇāl ittu – when you were glancing (at her, to be your servitor); kai viḷikkinṟadum – gesticulating with your hand (to tell her to come to a place); kaṇḍē ninṟēn – I was looking at you only; ingu – to my place; ennukku vandāy – why did you come?; nambi – Oh one, who is complete in all qualities!; innam – further; nī angē nada – you go back to her

**Перевод:**

**I was looking at you when you were going through my street, hugging a girl who had a slender waist, like lightning, covering your head with gold coloured dress, in pitch darkness. Even as you were going with her, you saw another girl there and signalled to her to meet you in a particular place. Oh the one, with completeness in all qualities! Why did you leave that innocent girl and come here? You may go back to her.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction)B Another covherd girl pushes away kaṇṇan, due to love-quarrel, and tells him “Do not come here” in this pāsuram.*

**vyākyānamn (Commentary)**

**min otta nuṇ idaiyāḷaik koṇḍu** – going with a girl with a very slender waist, as thin as lightning, and hugging her waist without hurting it.

**vīngiruḷ vāy** – darkness was helping you such that you could not be spotted taking steps in it.

**endan vīdiyūdē** – while going with her, couldn’t you have gone through some other street? Whose street did you think it was? Without any fear, did you not go through my street?

**pon otta ādai kukkūdalittup pōginṟa pōdu** – even as you were going, hiding yourself, did you not go with a golden dress which would have given brightness even to that darkness? Did you not go without any fear of “We are going with such and such girl. We are going through a street where a girl, who has deep affection for me, is living. We are going in pitch darkness”?

**pōginṟa 0pōdu nān kaṇḍu ninṟēn** – Unlike you, I am not one who thinks of others. Since I know “Where you are going, what you are doing” etc, I was watching as to what all you were doing.

She says further that he was not true to that girl too…

**kaṇṇuṟṟavaḷai** – Even as you were going, hugging this girl, you spotted another girl going that way.

**nī kaṇṇāl ittu** – you glanced at her and signalled to her to be your servitor.

**kai viḷikkinṟadum kaṇḍē ninṟēn** – hugging this girl, you gesticulated to that other girl with your hands “Come to that place” I saw all these.

**ennukku avaḷai vittu ingu vandāy** – not knowing your nature, that innocent girl believed that you are a truthful person. Why did you leave her, making her hate you, and come here?  As soon as he heard this, kaṇṇan told her “Are there other people for me? Am I not one who does not have anyone other than you?” . . . .

**innam angē nada nambi nīyē** – She tells him “Are you not one who is complete with all good qualities? Could you speak such words as if you were one with some shortcoming? You may go back to that place itself”

We will consider pāsuram 6.6, next.

## 6.6. Marporu toludai

*maṟporu tōḷudai vāsudēvā! valvinaiyēn tuyil koṇḍavāṟēiṟṟai iravidai ēmatt(u) ennai innaṇai mēlittu aganṟu nī pōyaṟṟai iravum ōr piṟṟai nāḷum arivaiyarōdum aṇaindu vandāyeṟṟukku nī en marungil vandāy? emperumān! nī eḻundaruḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mal poru – one who fought with wrestlers; tōḷ udai vāsudēvā – Oh one who has the shoulders and who is the son of vasudēvar!; valvinaiyēn – I, who had carried out lot of sinful deeds; tuyil koṇḍavāṟē – as soon as I slept off; iṟṟai iravu idai ēmattu – during the middle jāmam of that night \[a jāmam is a period of 4 hours\]; in aṇai mēl – on a mattress appropriate for union; ennai ittu – making me to lie down; nī aganṟu pōy – you left me; aṟṟai iravum – that night; ōr piṟṟai nāḷum – and the subsequent day; arivaiyarōdum­ – with (all the five lakh) girls; aṇaindu vandāy – you came after uniting with them; nī – you, who are like this; en marungil – near me; eṟṟukku vandāy – why did you come?; emperumān – On my svāmy (lord)!; nī eḻundaruḷ – you may move out

**Перевод:**

**Oh the son of vasudēvar, and who has the shoulders with which you fought against the wrestlers! I am a covherd girl who has carried out plenty of sinful deeds. You had put me down on an appropriate bed for union, and after I slept off, you left me. You spent that night and the subsequent day with the (five lakh covherd) girls. Why did you come near me, after uniting with them? Oh my lord! You may move out \[from here\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This pāsuram reflects the angry words of a girl directed at kriṣṇan who returns to her after leaving her and embracing a few other girls.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maṟporu tōḷudai vāsudēvā** – since you are the son of vasudēvar, even if you behave in an unrestrained way, you cannot be stopped. The implied meaning is that since he is an uncontrollable person, his activities do not cause any surprise.

**valvinaiyēn tuyil koṇḍavāṟē** – when you were near me, I did not tie you down. Alas! I had committed such sins that even my sleep became my enemy since I came to know of your activities only after you had left.

**iṟṟai iravidai ēmattu ennai** – did you not leave me and go in the middle of the night when I should have hugged you?

**innaṇai mēlittu nī pōy** – Since it was a comfortable mattress, you would have thought “She would sleep when I am embracing her. Once she sleeps, I could go”. You had planned well and left me.

**aṟṟai iravum ōr piṟṟai nāḷum** – that night as well as the next morning. You could not have managed to do there, what you did to me, an ignoramus. Hence, you had to stay the rest of the night as well as the whole of the next day.

**arivaiyarōdum aṇaindu vandāy** – you came, after hugging all the girls in āyppādi (śrī gōkulam). Once she said this, he thought “The best way to pacify her is to embrace her” and went near her to embrace her.

**eṟṟukku nī en marungil vandāy** – I do not know as to which girl, who was not fit to be embraced, you had hugged. Don’t touch me. Stay at a distance.

**emperumān! nī eḻundaruḷē** – since you had behaved in an intimate manner earlier, due to that habit, I had told you “You have come close to me”. I should not have said that. You must forgive me. Are you not a great person? I should have told you “Mercifully, you should leave”

We will consdier pāsuram 6.7, next.

## 6.7. Pai aravinanai

*paiyaravinaṇaip paḷḷiyināy! paṇḍaiyōm allōm nām nī ugakkummaiyari oṇ kaṇṇinārumallōm vaigi em cērī varavoḻi nīseyya udaiyum tirumugamum sengani vāyum kuḻalum kaṇḍupoyyoru nāḷ pattadē amaiyum puḷḷuvam pēsadē pōgu nambī*

**Пословный перевод:**

pai aravin aṇaip paḷḷiyināy – Oh one who sleeps on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who has spread out hoods!; nambī – Oh, one who is complete!; nām – we; paṇḍaiyōm allōm – are not the earlier type of people (to be caught in your deceptive talk); nī ugakkum – being desired by you; mai – applying the black pigment \[to decorate the eyes\]; ari oṇ – radiant like a black coloured beetle; kaṇṇinārum allōm – we are not the girls with such eyes; vaigi – at unreasonable time; em cēri – to our residential place; varavu oḻi nī – you do not come; seyya udaiyum – with beautiful dress; tiru mugamum – with divine face; sem kani vāyum – with reddish lips resembling kōvai fruit (a type of fruit from common creeper of the hedges); kuḻalum – with beautiful locks; kaṇḍu – seeing \[these\]; poy – believing your false words as true; oru nāḷ pattadē amaiyum – what we had suffered on one day is more than enough; puḷḷuvam – deceptive words; pēsādē pōgu – leave this place, without saying (again)

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who sleeps on the mattress of tiruvantāḻvān! Oh one who is complete in all qualities! We are not the girls of earlier times, to be caught in the web of your deceptive words. We are not the ones desired by you, applying black decorative pigment to our radiant eyes which look like black coloured beetles. You do not come at unreasonable times to our neighbourhood. We have suffered enough believing your false words as true, looking at your beautiful dress, divine face, reddish lips, beautiful locks etc. Leave this place, without saying those deceptive words, again.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) These are the words of another covherd girl spoken in the mood of love-quarrel.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pai aravin aṇaip paḷḷiyināy** – kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa), looking at her mood of love-quarrel, tells her “Even if you do not like me, I like you. Am I not the one who is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān, thinking about protecting the entire worlds?” Referring to this, she calls him as one who is reclining on the mattress of ādiśēśan.

**paṇḍaiyōm allōm nām** – Since we now know the activities which you indulge in, to subjugate us to you, do not think of us as the earlier ones. She says that they have gone away from their old thought of “*nāgaṇai misai nambirān caraṇē śaraṇ namakku*” as said in tiruvāīmoḻi 5-10-11 (The divine feet of the one who is reclining on tirvnantāḻvān are the refuge for us). The fact that she, who has been believing that the reclining position of emperumān on ādiśēśan is her refuge, is now doubting him because he has delayed in coming to her, shows her involvement with kaṇṇan.

When he said “Even though you are not the earlier ones, I am always of the old type only, enjoying you always”, she says . . . .

**nī ugakkum mai ari oṇ kaṇṇinārum allōm** – she says “We are not the old type that you enjoy”. Since the description is about the eyes, the implied meaning is that “We are not the type to make you bewildered with our beautiful eyes and act in an unrestrained manner”.  The meaning for the term *mai ari oṇ kaṇ* could be considered as either ‘beautiful eyes which will win over the black pigment applied to it’ or as ‘beautiful eyes which are like the black decorative pigment and like the black beetle’. The word *ari* refers to both defeating an enemy and beetles. When he asks “What do you want me to do?” she says ….

**em cēri varavoḻi nī** – it is enough if you do not come to our neighbourhood where our orders alone will be carried out. He says “It is a common place for everyone. Why do you say that only I should not come?” She responds saying “I am only saying not to come when I am alone lest others think ‘Why is he coming when there is no one else? Possibly there is some connection between them’. I am not telling you not to come when others are also going around this place”. He started using certain weapons during the times when she tells him not to come. Since he becomes submissive when she speaks, he starts carrying out certain activities such that she cannot speak. Pointing out to these, she says further …

**seyya udaiyum tirumugamum sengani vāyum kuḻalum kaṇḍu** – he indulged in activities such as adjusting his dress, showing his face by going in front of her, smiling at her, decorating his hair etc. These are the weapons available to him to subjugate her to his wishes. As soon as he did all these, she denied. In other words, she closed her eyes. When he asks “Why are closing you closing your eyes, foregoing the benefit of having your eyes?” she says ….

**poyyoru nāḷ pattadē amaiyum** – She says “Is my suffering not enough when I had believed all that you had said in the past?” When he tried to speak a few words implying whether he would say such words, she says …

**puḷḷuvam pēsādē pōgu** – We can hear you only if we see you. When we do not want to even see you, where is the question of hearing your words? We have suffered enough, hearing your words. There is no purpose in your trying to speak, when we do not even wish to see you. The word *puḷḷuvam* refers to deception. She says “Do not use again your deceptive tactics with us when we already know about those.” kaṇṇan now says “You are asking me to go. Is there any refuge for me other than you?” She says …

**nambī** – when you are complete with all your qualities, should you talk like someone who is deficient in anything? We have understood that you are complete in all respects, not expecting anything from anyone, and that there is no congruence between what you speak and how you act. There is no purpose in speaking a few words as if you are deficient in something which you expect to set right through us. Leave this place.

We will take up pāsuram 6.8, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 6.8. Ennai varuga ena

*ennai varuga enakkuṟittittu inamalar mullaiyin pandar nīḻalmanniyavaḷaip puṇarap pukku maṟṟennaik kaṇḍu uḻaṟā negiḻndāyponniṟa ādaiyaik kaiyil tāngip poyyaccam kātti nī pōdiyēḷuminnam en kaiyagattu īngorunāḷ varudiyēl en sinam tīrvan nānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ennai varuga ena – calling me (to a particular place); kuṟittittu – notifying the place; inam malar mullaiyin pandar nīḻal – in the shadow of the jasmine shed, having plenty of flowers; manniyavaḷai – one who had taken residence there, awaiting him; puṇarappukku – to unite with her; maṟṟu ennaik kaṇḍu – later, looking at me; uḻaṟā negiḻndāy – moved away, distressed; pon niṟa ādaiyai – the divine dress; kaiyil tāngi – holding in your hand; poy – falseness; accam kātti – acting as if you were scared of me; nī pōdiyēlum – even if you escaped (from me); innam – hereafter; īngu – here; en kai agattu – to my place; oru nāḷ varudiyēl – if you come on a day; nān en sinam tīrvan – I will get rid of my anger

**Перевод:**

**You had told me to come to a particular place. \[When I reached that place\] I found that you were waiting to unite with a girl who had taken residence under the jasmine shed, which had plenty of flowers. After looking at me, you went away from the place, in distress, holding your divine dress in your hand. Even though you acted as if you were scared of me and escaped from me, if you come to my place one day, in future, I will take my revenge on you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This pāsuram is the words spoken by another covherd girl, in the pathos of love-quarrel.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ennai varuga enak kuṟittittu** – you had told me, your servitor, to come to a particular place.

**ina malar mullaiyin pandar nīḻal manniyavaḷai** – you were hugging a girl, who you had earlier told to come to the place which was in the shadow of a jasmine shed, which had abundance of blossomed flowers. Through the term *manniyavaḷai* it is clear that he had specified that place to that other girl a long time back and that she had taken residence under the jasmine shed, awaiting him.

**manniyavaḷaip puṇarappukku** – he started hugging that girl, who had taken residence there.

**maṟṟu ennaik kaṇḍu uḻaṟā negiḻndāy** – you had planned in your mind to hug her for a long time and during the time that you were with her, after seeing me, you went away from the place, in a distressed manner. Since he had asked both the girls to come to that place, it is clear that he had planned to make both of them to long for him.

**ponniṟa ādaiyaik kaiyil tāngi** – holding in the hand, the golden coloured dress worn on the upper part of the body

**poy accam kātti nī pōdiyēlum** – even if you go away from me, putting on an act of being fearful of me. That he feigned that he was scared after looking at her and left the place is clear from his smiling at her from his hiding place. He feigned fear only to scare these two girls. If he had really been fearful, would he have told both of them to come to the same place? Only to prove that he was really not truthful to either of them, he had feigned fear.

**pōdiyēlum** – his desire at that point of time was just to escape from her. He did that exactly.

**innam en kaiyagattu īngu oru nāḷ varudiyēl** – since you do not have an integral nature, it is certain that one day you are going to get caught by me. If you get caught like that ….

**en sinam tīrvan nānē** – I am going to take revenge for the anger which you have created now in me.

periyavāccān piḷḷai, the commentator for this prabandam, has narrated an incident which occurred in śrī rangam, during the time of bhagavad rāmānujar. An araiyar (araiyars are kainkaryaparars (servitors) in the temple, who are experts in describing the meanings of pāsurams through dance movements), uyndapiḷḷai, was giving explanation to this pāsuram through dance movements. At that time, the divine brother of emperumānār (rāmānujar), and his disciple, embār, was mercifully present in that gathering. When the araiyar was showing the movements for this specific verse “*en sinam tīrvan nānē*” (I will take revenge on you), he made a movement with his leg, as if he was kicking kaṇṇan. embār, the colossal aficionado, to make it evident that this is what kaṇṇan expected since that would tantamount to “īsn’t it his luck that she is kicking him? īsn’t he expecting contact with her physical form? Hence kicking him would not be appropriate for taking revenge”, showed the araiyar the apt movement by covering his face with his fingers and turning the face away. In other words, the real way to show her despise towards him, is to turn the face away from kaṇṇan in hatred. The araiyar understood this and showed this movement in the next turn.

We will take up pāsuram 6.9, next.

## 6.9. 6.9 Mangala nal vana

*mangala nanvanamālai mārvil ilanga mayil taḻai peelipongiḷa ādai araiyil sāttip pūngottuk kādil puṇarappeydukongu naṟunguḻalārgaḷōdu kuḻaindu kuḻal inidu ūdi vandāyengaḷukkē orunāḷ vandu ūda un kuḻalin isai pōdarādē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mangalam – being auspicious; nal – being beautiful; vanamālai – the vanamālai (a divine garland on emperumān’s divine chest); mārvil – on the divine chest; ilanga – as it shines; mayil taḻai peeli – lush feathers of peacock; sūdi – donning (on the divine crown); pongu iḷa ādai – very light dress; araiyil sātti – wearing it on the divine waist; pū kottu – cluster of flowers; kādil – in the ear; kongu naṟum kuḻalārgaḷōdu – with girls who have honey and fragrance in their locks; kuḻaindu – mixing well (as an outlet for the resulting happiness); kuḻal – flute; inidu ūdi vandāy – you came, playing on it beautifully; oru nāḷ – just for a day; engaḷukkē – only for us; vandu ūda – to come and play \[the flute\] for us; un kuḻalin isai – the music from your flute; pōdarādē – will not come

**Перевод:**

**The auspicious and beautiful divine garland is shining on your divine chest. You have donned the lush feathers of peacock on your divine crown. You have donned a light dress on your divine waist. You have decorated your ears with a cluster of flowers, fittingly. You mingled well with girls, whose hair was full of honey and fragrance. As an outlet for your happiness, you played on your flute, beautifully. We, the covherd girls, desire that you should come only for us, one day, and play the flute for us. But, the music from your flute will not come.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Though the covherd girls were engaging in love-quarrel with kaṇṇan, they all admit their defeat and say it explicitly, in this pāsuram.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**mangala nal vanamālai mārvil ilanga** – vanamālai, the divine garland has, within it, all that is auspicious in the world, and it is beautiful, too. It shines brightly on your divine chest, just as pēyāḻvār had said in mūnṟām tiruvandādi 59 “*maipōl neduvaraivāy tāḻum aruvipōl tār kidappa sūḻum tirumāmaṇivaṇṇan*” (Just as streams flow down from both sides of a dark mountain, garlands come down from the divine shoulders towards the feet, surrounding his divine chest. pirātti is surrounding emperumān, being unable to let go of this beautiful form of emperumān)

**mayil taḻaip peeli sūdi** – donning the rich feathers of peacock on the divine crown.

**pongiḷa ādai araiyil sātti** – wearing a very light dress on the divine waist

**pūngottuk kādil puṇarappeydu kongu naṟum kuḻalārgaḷōdu kuḻaindu** – wearing a cluster of flowers appropriately on the ears and mingling with covherd girls who have locks which are smelling sweet and fragrant, like honey.

**kuḻal inidu ūdi vandāy** – mingling with all of us, the covherd girls, who were engaging in love-quarrel with you and who were thinking of separating from you. As an outlet for the ecstasy which resulted from such mingling, you came playing sweetly on your flute.

**engalukkē orunāḷ vandu ūda un kuḻal innisai pōdarādē** – you are common to all. Your music from the flute is meant for all to hear and enjoy. When such is the case, we desire that you should come to us in every place that we go to and play the flute only for us. Even if you wish to play the flute, the music from the flute will not come out. Such is the character of your flute. The meaning of this is – the reference to music from flute is only being together with kaṇṇan. The meaning for the phrase “music from your flute will not come out” is – mingling with all of us is not proper for you. You are greater than all of us. This being the case, being together with us, who are all ignorant, is not appropriate for you. The covherd girls admit their defeat, in this pāsuram.

We will next take up the last pāsuram, 6.10, of this decad.

## 6.10. Alli malart tirumangai

*alli malart tirumangai kēḷvan tannai nayand(du) iḷa āyccimārgaḷellippoḻudinil ēmattūdi eḷgiyuraitta uraiyadanaikkolli nagarkkiṟai kūdaṟkōmān kulasēkaran innisaiyin mēviccolliya indamiḻ mālai pattum sollavallārkku illai tunbandānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

iḷa āyccimārgaḷ – youthful covherd girls; alli malar tirumangai kēḷvan tanni nayandu – desiring the consort of periyapirātti, who was born in a lotus flower; elli ēmap poḻudinilē – in the middle of the night; ūdi – having love-quarrel; eḷgi – (due to that) being involved; uraitta – spoken; urai adanai – the pāsurams; kolli nagarkku iṟai – the king of kolli nagar; kūdal kōmān – the chief of madurai; kulasēkaran – kulaśēkhara āḻvār; in isaiyil mēvi – to fit well with sweet music; in tamiḻ – in sweet tamiḻ language; mālai – like a garland; pattum – these ten pāsurams; solla vallārrku – those who are capable of learning; tunbandān illai – (as a hurdle to bhagavad anubhavam (experiencing matters relating to emperumān)) no sorrow will entail

**Перевод:**

**Youthful covherd girls, in the middle of the night, had a love-quarrel with kaṇṇan, the consort of periya pirātti, who was born in a lotus flower. Due to their involvement with kaṇṇan, they spoke some words. kulaśēkhara āḻvaār, the king of cēra and pāṇḍiya kingdoms, composed those words as a garland, set to music in sweet tamiḻ language. Those who are capable of learning these ten pāsurams will not experience any sorrow which could be a hurdle to their bhagavad anubhavam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) By giving the benefits to those who learn this decad, āḻvār brings the decad to an end.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**allimalart tirumangai kēḷvan tannai nayandu** – desiring kaṇṇan, who is the consort of periya pirāttiyar, who was born in a lotus flower. Once he has been identified as “*alli malart tirumangai kēḷvan*” it is clear that he has huge affection towards covherd girls who show affection towards him.

**iḷa āyccimārgaḷ** – unlike kaṇṇan who has matured well, they are so youthful that they will not be able to sustain themselves should they separate from him.

**ellip popḻudinil ēmattu**– in the middle of the night when there is no one to separate them should they unite.

**ūdi eḷgi uraitta urai adanai** – having love quarrel initially, subsequently engaging with each other due to that, and that engagement manifesting as words; such a pāsuram.

**kolli nagarkku iṟai kūdal kōmān** – *kolli* refers to tiruvanjikkaḷam, the capital city of cēra kingdom. *kūdal* refers to madurai, the capital city of pāṇḍiya kingdom. Earlier (in the pāsuram 2.10) he had referred to himself as *kōḻikkōn*. *kōḻi* refers to uṟaiyūr, the capital city of cōḻa kingdom. Thus, this refers to kulaśēkharap perumāḷ being the king of cēra, cōḻa and pāṇḍiya kingdoms. It appears that just as the covherd girls left the esteem of their femininity, āḻvār left the esteem of being the head of the three kingdoms.

**innisaiyil mēvic conna intamiḻ mālai pattum** – śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 4.8 says “*pātyē gēyē ca maduram pramāṇais tribhiranvitam* l *jātibhis saptabhir baddam tantreelayasamanvitam* \|\|” ((kuśa and lava sang) śrī rāma kāviyam (śrī rāma’s narrative) which is sweet to read or to sing, having three meters (namely *drutam, madyam* and *viḷambitam*), having seven notes (namely śatjam, niśādam, daivatam, pancamam, madyamam, ghāndāram, riśabham), in syncronisation with the strings of vīṇā and rhythm). Similar to this, the sweet tamiḻ garland of ten pāsurams which were mercifully composed sweetly, fitting well with musical notes.

**solla vallārkku illai tubam tane** – even if one does not have the pathos of āḻvār’s nāyaki (consort of emperumān) mental make-up, will there be any hurdle for anyone who learns these ten pāsurams in experiencing emperumān? Those who learn the ten pāsurams will always engage with matters relating to emperumān, without having such doubts. Unlike the love-quarrel which happened \[to the covherd girls, as a hurdle\] while experiencing emperumān, they will have *bhagavad anubhavam* as their routine.

We will, next, take up the 7th decad of this prabandam.

## 7.1. Alai neel karumbu

*ālaineeḷ karumbu annavantālō ambuyat tadam kaṇṇinan tālōvēlai nīr niṟattu annavantālō vēḻap pōdagam annavan tālōēla vārkuḻal en magan tālō enṟenṟu unnai en vāyidai niṟaiyattālolittidum tiruvinai illāt tāyaril kadaiyāyina tāyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ālai neeḷ karumbu annavan – Oh one who is like a long sugar cane fit to be squeezed in a sugarcane mill!; tālō – lullaby for you; ambuyam – like a lotus flower; tadam kaṇṇinan – Oh one who has expansive eyes!; vēlai nīr niṟattu annavan – Oh one who has the complexion of ocean!; tālō – lullaby for you; vēḻam pōdagam annavan – Oh one who has the activities like an elephant calf!; tālō – lullaby for you; Elam vār kuḻal – one who has long hair which is fragrant; en magan – my son; tālō – lullaby for you; enṟu enṟu – saying like this many times; en vāyidai niṟaiya – for my mouth to feel happy; unnai – you; tāl olittidum tiruvinai illā – one who does not have the wealth of singing lullaby; kadiyāyina tāy – I am the lowliest mother

**Перевод:**

**I am the lowliest among all mothers since I do not have the wealth of singing lullaby for you while putting you to sleep. I did not sing lullaby with which my mouth would have felt happy, by saying several times lullabies such as – Oh one who is like a long sugar cane fit enough to be squeezed in a sugarcane mill! Oh one who has wide eyes which look like lotus! Oh one who has the complexion of ocean! Oh one who has the activities of a baby elephant! Oh one who has long and fragrant hair! Oh my son!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) When you were an infant, I did not have the fortune to sing lullaby to put you to sleep, says dēvaki, in this pāsuram.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ālai nīr karumbu annavan tālō** – Oh one who is sweet to the tongue like the long sugarcane which is appropriate to be fed into a sugar mill!

**ambuyattadam kaṇṇinan tālō** – Oh one whose eyes are like the lotus flower which has just blossomed, and has freshness, coolness and softness, and with which you shower me with your grace! Through this, āḻvār says that kaṇṇan is sweet to the sense of vision.

**vēlai nīr niṟattu annavantālō** – Should one describe the sweetness of each of the limbs? With the divine form which has all those limbs, don’t you remove the ennui of the beholder, like the ocean!

**vēḻappōdagam annavan tālō** – Oh one who does activities like an elephant calf such that one will keep on looking at, forever!

**ēla vār kuḻal en magan tālō** – Since dēvaki feels that even with many more such allegories, she cannot describe fully his activities, she calls him out saying “Oh one who has long locks which are fragrant”!

**enṟu enṟu unnai en vāyidai niṟaiyat tāl olittidum tiruvinai illā** – one who has lost the fortune of singing lullaby for you like this

**tāyaril kadai āyina tāyē** – she laments that among all the mothers who observe various rituals to beget a child, and enjoy that child after birth, she has become the lowliest.

We will next take up the 2nd pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.2. Vadikkol anjanam

*vadikkoḷ anjanam eḻudu semmalarkkaṇ maruvi mēl inidu onṟinai nōkkimudakkic cēvadi malarcciṟu karundāḷ poliyum nīr mugil kuḻaviyē pōlaadakki ārac cenjiruviral anaittum angaiyōdaṇaind(u) ānaiyiṟkidandakidakkaik kaṇidilap peṟṟilēn andō kēsavā! keduvēn keduvēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vadikkoḷ – being sharp; anjanam eḻudu – applied with black pigment; sem malark kaṇ – with divine eyes matching a reddish lotus; mēl onṟinai – a play-item which is hanging from above \[in the cradle\]; inidu – sweetly; maruvi nōkki – looking at, constantly; karu tāḷ – dark outer foot; malarc ciṟu sē adi – divine feet which have lotus like reddish small sole; mudakki – folding them; nīr poliyum – shining like a cloud which has taken water till its neck; mugil kuḻavi pōla – like a baby cloud; sem siṟu viral anaittum – all the beautiful, small toes; am kaiyōdu – inside the palm; adakki Ara – to be held well; aṇaindu – holding them; ānaiyil kidanda kidakkai – the way you were reclining like an elephant calf; kaṇḍitap peṟṟilēn – I was not fortunate to see and enjoy; andō – alas!; kēsavā – Oh one with beautiful locks!; keduvēn keduvēnē – how I became such a great sinner that I could not enjoy anything

**Перевод:**

**Oh the one with beautiful locks! I became such a great sinner that I could not  enjoy any of your activities as a child such as – looking constantly at dolls hanging from the top of the cradle, with your divine, lotus like and sharp eyes which have been applied with black pigment; lying like an elephant calf, folding your outer black coloured feet and the  little inner feet with reddish soles; folding all your little toes such that they are held within the palm; looking like a little cloud which has drunk water up to its neck.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that he had sinned so much that he could not see emperumān as an infant \[in the pathos of dēvaki\]*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vadikkoḷ anjanam eḻudu semmalarkkaṇ** – divine, reddish lotus-like eyes which are sharp and which have been applied with decorating black coloured pigment. *vadi* – sharpness. Alternatively, we could construe this verse to mean divine eyes which have been applied with black coloured pigment so that they do not cause any burning sensation. *semmalarkkaṇ* – reddish lotus-like eyes.

**maruvi mēl inidu onṟinai nōkki** – when keeping infants in a cradle, its mother would tie a few dolls so that the infant would keep looking at it sweetly, with a smile on its face. āḻvār is referring to this as *maruvi mēl inidu onṟinai nōkki*.

**mudakkic cēvadi malarc ciṟu karundāḷ** – the pāsuram now elaborates on the beauty of the infant kaṇṇan. Folding the leg where the inner part (sole) is reddish in colour and the outer, black in colour.

**poliyum nīr mugil kuḻaviyē pōla** – just like a baby cloud (small cloud) which has imbibed water fully.

**adakki ārac cenjiṟu viral anaittum angaiyōdu aṇaittu** – clasping the beautiful toes, which are together, inside the beautiful palm

**ānaiyil kidanda kidakkai kaṇḍidap peṟṟilēn andō** – dēvaki expresses her sorrow of not enjoying the way he was lying in the cradle, just like an elephant which stretches its limbs and lies with total independence, without any control.

**kēsavā keduvēn keduvēnē** – since the term *kēsavan* refers to the beauty of the divine locks, it implies that she had not enjoyed the beauty of the divine locks which kaṇṇan had, as a child. Since she says *keduvēn keduvēnē*, repeating the term *keduvēn*, she says that she had lost twice, first time before kaṇṇan was born by losing out on the enjoyment of earlier born children at the time of their birth itself \[since kamsan killed them as soon as they were born\] and second time, after kaṇṇan was born, by sending him off to āyppādi (śrī gōkulam) soon after he was born. She says that on both occasions, she had lost out \[on not having enjoyed the childhood activities of her children\] due to the heinous sins committed by her.

We will next take up the 3rd pāsuram of this decad.
