## 7.3. Mundai nanmurai

*mundai nanmuṟai anbudai magaḷir muṟai muṟai tandam kuṟangidai iruttiendaiyē!endan kulapperunjudarē!eḻil mugil gaṇattu eḻil kavarēṟē!undaiyāvan enṟuraippa nin sengēḻ viralinum kadaikkaṇṇinum kāttanandan peṟṟanan nalvinai illā nangaḷ kōn vasudēvan peṟṟīlanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

anbudai – being affectionate; mundai nal muṟai magaḷir – ladies who have been hereditarily having good relationships; muṟai muṟai – in turns; irutti – keeping; “endaiyē – Oh my svāmy!; endan kulapperum sudarē – Oh one who is like a splendorous light in our clan!; eḻu mugil gaṇattu – seven different types of clouds’; eḻil kavar – attracting the beauty; ēṟē – Oh one who is like a lion! “(praising like this); undai yāvan enṟuuraippa – as soon as being asked “Who is your father?”; nin – your; sem kēḻ viralinum – with the reddish coloured fingers; kaṇṇinum – with the corners of the eye; kātta – as you showed (that fortune); nandan peṟṟanan – nandagōpar had; nalvinai illā nangaḷ kōn – my husband, who is without that fortune; vasudēvan – vasudēvar; peṟṟilan – did not have

**Перевод:**

**Women, who are affectionate and who have good relationship through their clans, kept you on their laps, in turns. They praised you in different ways saying “Oh one who is like our lord! Oh one who is like a splendorous light to our clan! Oh one who is a like a lion, having taken in the beauty from seven different types of clouds!” When they asked you “Who is your father?” you showed them with the reddish coloured fingers and through the corners of your eyes. nandagōpar (who was indicated by you as your father) was very fortunate. My husband, vasudēvar, did not have that fortune.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) dēvaki says in this pāsuram that it is not only that she had lost, but even vasudēvar lost because he married her.*

**mundai nanmuṟai anbudai magaḷir** – being affectionate, women who have been related such as aunts, their mothers, their grandmothers et al

**muṟai muṟai tam tam kuṟangidai kidatti** – keeping (you) in turns, in their laps

**endaiyē endan kulap perum sudarē eḻu mugil gaṇattu eḻil kavar ēṟē**– Oh my svāmy! Oh one who became a radiant light to our clan! Oh one who is like a lion which has won over the beauty of seven different types of cloud!

**undai yāvan enṟu uraippa** – praising like this \[as mentioned above\], when queried “Who is your father?”

**nin sengēḻ viralinum kadaik kaṇṇinum kātta nandan peṟṟanan** – nandagōpar attained the pride of being shown \[as your father\] through your reddish fingers and the corners of your eye

**nalvinai illā nangaḷ kōn vasudēvan peṟṟilan** – śrī vasudēvar, because he married me \[who is without any fortune\], lost that fortune\[of being identified by you as your father\]

We will take up the 4th pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 7.4. Kali nila elil

*kaḷi nilā eḻil madi purai mugamum kaṇṇanē! tiṇ kai mārvum tiṇ tōḷumtaḷir malark karunguḻal piṟai aduvum tadangoḷ tāmaraik kaṇgaḷum polindaiḷamai inbattai inṟ(u)endan kaṇṇāl paruguvēṟku ivaḷ tāy ena ninaindaaḷavil piḷḷaimai inbattai iḻanda pāviyēn enadāvi nillādē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaḷi nilā – having moon which creates happiness; eḻil madi purai – matching a full moon; mugamum – divine face; tiṇ kai – divine hands which have strength; mārbum – divine chest; tiṇ tōḷum – divine shoulders which have strength; taḷir malar karu kuḻal – shining brightly below the dark, divine locks, which have been decorated with tender leaves and flowers; piṟai aduvum – the divine forehead which is like the crescent of the moon on the third day \[after new moon day\]; tadangoḷ – being broad; tāmarai kaṇgaḷum – divine eyes which are like lotus flower (all these); polinda iḷamai inbattai – the beauty of youthfulness which is shining beautifully; inṟu – now; endan kaṇṇāl – with my eyes; paruguvēṟku – to me who is imbibing; ivaḷ tāy ena ninainda – knowing only ‘She is my mother’; aḷavu il piḷḷaimai – happening in infancy; inbattai – the happiness; iḻanda – one who lost (without enjoying); pāviyēn enadu – the sinner, my; āvi – life; nillādu – will not sustain

**Перевод:**

**Oh kaṇṇapirān! I am now imbibing the beauty of youthfulness which is shining beautifully, with various limbs of your divine form, such as the divine face which looks like a full moon which gives happiness to the beholders, the strong arms, the divine chest and the strong shoulders, the divine forehead which appears like the crescent of the moon, below the dark and radiant hair which is decked with tender leaves and flowers, the broad, divine eyes which are like lotus flower. I, who had committed lot of sins, lost out on looking at your beauty when you were an infant, when you would have known only me, your mother, and no one else. My life will not sustain.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) dēāvaki says in this pāsuram that she cannot sustain her life since she is a sinner, who lost the joy of enjoying her child.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kaḷi nilā eḻil madi purai mugamum** – the divine face which is like a full moon, which is brimming with the brightness of moon enjoyed by everyone.

**kaṇṇanē tiṇ kai mārvum tiṇ tōḷum** – *kaṇṇanē* – dēvaki calls out to kriṣṇa. hanuman tells śrī rāma when they meet for the first time, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 3-15 ślōkam “*āyatāśca suvrutāsca bhāhava*: *parighōpamā*: l *sarva bhūśaṇa bhūśārhā*: *kimartam na vibhūśitā*: \|\|” (Why are the divine shoulders \[of śrī rāma\] which are long and well rounded, worthy of being decorated with ornaments, not decorated?) kaṇṇan has firm, divine hands, divine chest and divine shoulders which are great to be praised.

**taḷir malark karunguḻal piṟai aduvum** – divine forehead which appears below the dark, divine hair which has been decked up with tender leaves and flowers, making people to exclaim whether it is a crescent of moon.

**tadam koḷ tāmaraik kaṇgaḷum** – just as a huge lotus flower fills up an entire pond, kaṇṇan’s divine eyes were so wide that it made wonder whether the divine form was made of only his eyes.

**polinda iḷamai inbattai inṟu endan kaṇṇāl paruguvēṟku** – to me \[his mother\] who is imbibing through my eyes, the shining youthfulness which is brightened by the beauty of the divine limbs.

**ivaḷ tāyena ninainda aḷavil piḷḷaimai inbattai iḻanda pāviyēn** – as an infant, he would have known only me and no one else. At that time, I would have enjoyed his beauty. But I have sinned so much that even after seeing his youthful state and enjoying it, only sorrovfulness remains within me.

**enadu āvi nillādē** – I cannot sustain my life.

Next, we will take up the 5th pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.5. Maruvum nin tiru

*maruvum nin tiru neṟṟiyil cutti asai tara maṇivāyidai muttamtarudalum undan tādaiyaip pōlum vadivu koṇḍ(u) uḷḷam uḷ kuḷiraviralaic cenjiṟu vāyidaic cērttu veguḷiyāy ninṟuraikkum avvuraiyumtiruvilēn onṟum peṟṟīliēn ellām deiva nangai yasōdai peṟṟāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nin tiru neṟṟīyil – on your divine forehead; maruvum – fitting brightly; cutti – the ornament worn on forehead; asai tara – to sway; maṇi vāyidai – with your beautiful mouth; muttam tarudalum – planting kisses; undan tādaiyaip pōlum – like your father; vadivu – (your) form; kaṇḍu koṇḍu – enjoying it; uḷḷam uḷ kuḷira – to make the mind feel happy; sem siṟu vāyidai – in the reddish, divine mouth; viralaic cērttu – putting the divine fingers inside; veguḷiyāy ninṟu – standing with anger; urailkkum avvuraiyum – the gibberish that you utter, all these; onṟaiyum – even one; tiru iḷen – one without fortune, I; peṟṟīlēn – have not enjoyed; deiva nangai yaśōdai – yaśōdāp pirātti who could be considered as a celestial person; ellām peṟṟāḷē – (all those childish activities) she enjoyed completely

**Перевод:**

**I have not had the fortune to enjoy any of your childhood activities such as planting kisses with your mouth even as the ornament cutti worn on your divine forehead; having a beautiful form similar to your father’s which would make my mind feel happy; putting your divine fingers inside your reddish mouth and speaking gibberish, while you stand with anger. yaśōdāp pirātti, who could be considered as a celestial lady, had the fortune to enjoy all these, completely.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) She says in this pāsuram that she did not have the fortune that the most fortunate yaśōdā had.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maruvu nin tiru neṟṟiyil cutti asai tara** – the ornament *cuttii* worn on the forehead was fitting so well with the forehead that it made one to wonder “Was it born along with the divine forehead?” As it swayed . . .

**maṇi vāyidai muttam tarudalum** – planting kisses with your beautiful mouth

**un tādaiyaip pōlum** – like your father, vasudēvar

**vadivu kaṇḍu koṇḍu uḷḷam uḷ kuḷira** – looking at your \[beautiful\] form, my mind felt completely happy.

**viralaic cenjiṟu vāyidaic cērttu veguḷiyāy ninṟu uraikkum avvuraiyum** – putting your divine fingers inside your reddish, little mouth, standing with anger and speaking gibberish; the implied meaning is that the words spoken by kaṇṇan even in anger have a beauty.

**tiruvilēn onṟum peṟṟilēn** – I am such an unfortunate person that I even lost the experience of enjoying the childhood activities \[of my son\].

**ellām deiva nangai yaśōdai peṟṟāḷē** – just as I was born to lose everything, yaśōdā was born to enjoy everything. Such a celestial person, yaśōdā, experienced all your childhood activities, without missing even one.

We will take up the 6th pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 7.6. Tannam tamarai

*taṇṇam tāmaraik kaṇṇanē! kaṇṇā! tavaḻndu eḻundu taḷarndadōr nadaiyāl maṇṇil sembodi ādi vand(u) endan mārvil mannidap peṟṟilēn andō! vaṇṇac cenjiṟu kaiviral anaittum vāri vāykkoṇḍa adisilin miccil uṇṇap peṟṟilēn ō! koduvinaiyēn ennai enseyyap peṟṟadu emmōyē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

taṇ am tāmaraik kaṇṇanē – Oh one with divine eyes which appear like a cool, beautiful lotus flower!; kaṇṇā – Oh kaṇṇapirān! (kriṣṇa); tavanḻndu – crawling \[on all fours\] (you); eḻundu – getting up; taḷarndadōr nadaiyāl – with a tottering walk, which was unsteady; semmaṇ podiyil – in reddish soil; ādi – playing; vandu – coming (in that state); mannidap peṟṟilēn – I did not have the fortune of your hugging me!; andō – What a misfortune that I have had!; vaṇṇam sem siṟu – beautiful, red and small; kai viral anaittum – with all the divine fingers; vāykkoṇḍa – eaten; adisilin – the food’s; miccil – remnants; uṇṇap peṟṟilēn – I did not have the fortune to eat; O – Alas!; koduvinaiyēn – I have become a great sinner!; emmōy – my mother; ennai – me; en seyya – for what purpose; peṟṟadu – gave birth to?

**Перевод:**

**Oh kaṇṇapirān, with divine eyes which appear like a cool, beautiful lotus flower! I did not have the fortune when as a small child, you would crawl on all fours, get up, walk unsteadily in your tottering style, play in reddish soil and embrace me in that state. I did not have the fortune of eating the remnants of food sticking to your mouth after you had eaten the food with all your divine, beautiful, red and small fingers. I have become a great sinner. Why did my mother give birth to me?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) dēvaki is lamenting in this pāsuram as to why her mother gave birth to her.*

**taṇṇam tāmaraik kaṇṇanē kaṇṇā** – Oh kriṣṇa, who has divine eyes which look like a blossomed cool, beautiful lotus flower!

**tavaḻndu eḻundu taḷarndadōr nadaiyāl** – with the tottering walk in which you would crawl, then try to get up, begin to walk

**maṇṇil sembodi ādi vandu endan mārvil mannidap peṟṟilēn andō** – since the place had reddish soil, you would apply that soil on your body, and in that state, you would have come and hugged me. Alas! I missed that.

**vaṇṇam sem siṟu kaiviral anaittum vāri vāykkoṇḍa adisilin miccil uṇṇappeṟṟilēn O! koduvinaiyēn** – Alas! I did not have the fortune of eating the remnants of food \[which remained on your mouth\] after you had eaten the food with all your beautiful, reddish, divine fingers.

**ennai enseyyap peṟṟadu emmōyē** – Did my mother give birth to me only so that I would remain the reigning queen to the king and to not have the fortune of eating the remnants of food from her child?

Was it not said in tirukkuṟaḷ 64 “*amiḻdinum āṟṟa inidē tam makkāḷ siṟu kai aḷāviya kūḻ*” (the remnants of food in which the little hand of their children has dabbled will be far sweeter (to the parent) than ambrosia) and in tirukkuṟaḷ 65 “*makkaḷ meytīṇḍal udaṟkinbam maṟṟu avar soṟkēttal inbam sevikku*” (children’s touch gives happiness to the body and hearing their words gives happiness to the ears)?

Next, we will consider the 7th pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.11. Mallai managar

*mallai mānagarkku iṟaiyavan tannai vān selutti vandu īnganam māyattu ellaiyil piḷḷai seyvana kāṇāt teyvat tēvagi pulambiya pulambal kolli kāvalan māladi mudi mēl kōlamām kulasēkaran sonna nallisait tamiḻ mālai vallārgaḷ naṇṇuvār ollai nāraṇan ulagē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mallai – having plenty of opulence; mānagarkku – for the big town of śrī madurai (present day maturā); iṟaiyavan tannai – kamsan, the king; vān selutti – sending him off to svargam (heaven); vandu – coming to tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam); īnganam – like these; ellaiyil māyattu piḷḷai – kriṣṇa, who carried out unlimited amazing activities, his; seyvana kāṇā – one who could not see his activities; deyvat tēvagi – dēvaki who has divine qualities; pulambiya pulambal – the pāsurams which she composed with her lament; kolli kāvalan – controller of kolli nagar; māl adi mudimēl kōlamām – one who has kept the divine feet of emperumān on his crown; kulasēkaran – kulaśēkhara āḻvār; sonna – mercifully told; nallisai – having sweet music; tamiḻ mālai – tamiḻ pāsurams; vallārgaḷ – those who can recite; ollai – very soon; nāraṇan ulagu – paramapadam; naṇṇuvārgaḷ – will attain

**Перевод:**

**dēvaki did not get to see the unlimited, amazing activities of kriṣṇa, who had sent to heaven, kamsan, the king of madurai, which has plenty of opulence. She composed these pāsurams through her lament, which were told by kulaśēkhara āḻvār, the controller of kolli nagar, who has kept the divine feet of emperumān on his crown. Those who recite these tamiḻ pāsurams which are set to sweet music, will attain paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) soon.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār mercifully gives the benefits to those who recite this padigam and brings the padigam to an end.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentarya)**

**mallai mānagarkku iṟaiyavan tanni vān selutti** – by killing kamsan, the king of north madurai, which has plenty of opulence, with his own hands, emperumān sent him to svargam.

**vandu īnganam māyattu ellaiyil piḷḷai seyvana kāṇā** – not having seen the unlimited activities like these which kaṇṇan performed as a child, after killing kamsan and coming to tiruvāyppādi.

**deyvat tēvagi pulambiya pulambal** – the words spoken by dēvaki who has divine qualities. Though she did not see the amazing activities of kaṇṇan, since she gave birth to him, it could be said that she had divine qualities. In the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad, āḻvār had referred to yaśōdāp pirātti as *deyva nangai* (divine lady) since she had seen and enjoyed the activities of kaṇṇan as a child; Since dēvaki had the fortune of giving birth to him, he is calling her as *deyvat tēvagi.* In pāsuram 25 of tiruppāvai, did not āṇḍāḷ too call these two ladies as nonpareil “*orutti maganāyp piṟandu ōriravil orutti maganāy oḷittu vaḷara*” (born to one divine lady as her son and within a night, brought up by another divine lady, through hiding).

**kolli kāvalan** – one who ruled from kolli, the capital city of cēra nādu.

**māladi mudimēl kōlamām kulasēkaran** – since he is greater than everyone, emperumān is called as *māl*. kulaśēkharap perumāḷ had the divine feet of emperumān on his crown as a garland.

**sonna nal isait tamiḻ mālai vallārgaḷ** – those who learn this decad which has been composed as tamiḻ pāsuram by kulaśēkharap perumāḷ with sweet music

**naṇṇuvār ollai nāraṇan ulagē** – they will very soon attain paramapadam, the divine abode of śrīman nārāyaṇan, the lord of both vibhūtis, instead of lamenting like me, kulaśēkharan, who desired to enjoy the avatāram of kaṇṇan in this samsāram, but couldn’t.

Next, we will take up the 8th decad of this prabandam.

## 8.1. Mannu pugalk kausalai

*mannu pugaḻk kausalai tan maṇi vayiṟu vāyttavanē!tennilangaik kōn mudigaḷ sinduvittāy! sem pon sērkanni nan māmadiḷ pudai sūḻ kaṇapuratten karumaṇiyē!ennudaiya innamudē! irāgavanē! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

mannu pugaḻ – having stable greatness; kausalai than – kausalai’s; maṇi vayiṟu – in beautiful stomach; vāyttavanē – one who divinefully incarnated as the son!; then ilangaik kōn – rāvaṇa, the lord of lankā on the southern side, his; mudigaḷ – ten heads; sinduvittāy – (with arrows) one who scattered!; sem pon sēr – made with pure gold; kanni – indestructible; nal – being beautiful; mā – being huge; madiḷ – made with compound walls; pudai sūḻ­ – surrounded on all sides; kaṇapurattu – mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram; en karumaṇiyē – being like the black pupil of my eye!; ennudaiya innamudē – one who is like sweet ambrosia for me!; tālēlō – lullaby, to you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who incarnated as the divine son of kausalyā, who has firm greatness, in her beautiful stomach! Oh one who scattered the ten heads of rāvaṇa, the lord of lankā on the southern side, with your arrows! Oh one who is like the pupil of my eye, who has mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram, which is surrounded by huge compound walls, which are indestructible, beautiful and made with pure gold! Oh one who is like the sweet ambrosia for me! Oh śrī rāma! \[My\] lullaby to you \[for you to sleep well\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mannu pugaḻ kausalai than maṇi vayiṟu vāyttavanē** – kausalai had the firm greatness of having emperumān, who is the controller of all the worlds, as her son. Oh one who was born in the beautiful stomach of such kausalai!

**then ilangaikkōn mudigaḷ sinduvittāy** – Oh one who scattered the ten heads of rāvaṇa, who was the lord of lankā, with arrows! Since the main purpose of incarnating as śrī rāma was to annihilate rāvaṇa, āḻvār mentions destruction of rāvaṇa soon after mentioning śrī rāma’s incarnation.

**sem pon sēr kanni nan māmadiḷ pudai sūḻ kaṇapurattu en karu maṇiyē** – being made of most eminent gold, being indestructible, being beautiful, the huge compound walls surround tirukkaṇṇapuram, the divyadēśam where śrī rāma has taken residence mercifully. Oh one who is the pupil of my eye and who conducts me! *kanni* refers to the quality of being indestructible.

**ennudaiya innamudē** – Oh one who is like ambrosia for my eyes and subsistence, instead of the nectar for celestial entities which was just the essence of salty water!

**irāgavanē tālēlō** – āḻvār says that rāghavan \[śrī rāma\], who incarnated in the great clan of raghu, is his ambrosia, unlike the nectar of celestial entities which was without any essence.

We will take up the 2nd pāsuram of tisdecad, next.

## 8.2. Pundariga malar

*puṇḍariga malar adanmēl buvaniyellām padaittavanē!tiṇḍiṟalāḷ tādagai tan uram uruvac cilai vaḷaittāy!kaṇḍavar tam manam vaḻangum kaṇapuratten karumaṇiyē!eṇḍisaiyum āḷudaiyāy! irāgavanē! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

puṇḍarigam malar adan mēl – (appeared on the divine navel) atop lotus flower (creating brahmā and through him); buvani ellām padaittavanē – Oh one who created all the worlds!; tiṇ tiṟalāḷ tādagai than – tātakā, (the demon woman) who had firm strength; uram uruva – to pierce her chest; silai vaḷaittāy – Oh one who shot the arrow by drawing the bow!; kaṇḍavar – those who vorśipped; tam manam vaḻangum – being appropriate for them to voluntarily give their minds; kaṇapurattu en karu maṇiyē – Oh my bluish gem who has mercifully come to tirukkaṇṇapuram!; eṇ disaiyum – those who are in all eight directions; āḷ udaiyāy – Oh one who has made them as your servitors!; irāghavanē – Oh śrī rāma!; tālēlō – lullaby to you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who created all the worlds through your lotus-like, divine navel! Oh one who drew the bow and shot the arrow to pierce the chest of the strong demon, tātakā! Oh one who is my bluish gemstone and who has come mercifully to tiukkaṇṇapuram where those who you, willingly give up their minds to you! Oh one who has made the people in the eight directions to be your servitors! Oh śrī rāma! My lullaby to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār sings lullaby in this pāsuram for emperumān who created all the worlds in his divine lotus-like navel and who incarnated as śrī rāma and annihilated demon tātakā.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**puṇḍariga malar adan mēl buvani ellām padaittavanē** – Oh one who created brahmā in your lotus-like, divine navel and remaining within him, one who created all the worlds!

**tiṇ tiṟalāḷ tādagai than uram uruvac cilai vaḷaittāy** – āḻvār talks about how emperumān, instead of merely creating the world and leaving at that, removed demons such as tātakā et al, just as a farmer, besides cultivating the crop, removes the weeds too. Reference to tātakā holds equally for other demons such as subhāhu et al. Oh one who drew the bow and shot an arrow to pierce the firm chest of tātakā!

**kaṇḍavar tam manam vaḻangum kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē** – Oh one who stands in tirukkaṇṇapuram like a bluish gemstone such that those who come to worship you, give up voluntarily, their minds to you!

**eṇḍhisaiyum āḷudaiyāy irāgavanē tālēlō** – Oh one who stands such that even those who came after this rāmāvatāram could come from all eight directions and worship! Lullaby to you.

We will consider the 3rd pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 8.3. Kongumali karungulalal

*kongumali karunguḻalāḷ kausalai tan kula madalāy!tangu perum pugaḻc canagan tirumarugā! dāsaradī!gangaiyilum tīrtta mali kaṇapuratten karumaṇiyē!engaḷ kulattu innamudē! irāgavanē! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

kongu mali – with abundant fragrance; karu kuḻalāḷ – one who has black locks; kausalai than – kausalyā dēvi’s; kulam madalāy – Oh one who uplifted the clan!; tangu perum pugaḻ – having great, lasting fame; sanakan – to janaka maharājan; tirumarugā – Oh one who is his son-in-law!; gangaiyilum – more than river gangai; mali tīrttam – having pious waters; kaṇapurattu – one who has mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram; en karumaṇiyē – Oh one who is like a bluish gem to me!; engaḷ kulam – to all our royal clan; irāgavanē – Oh śrī rāma!; tālēlō – lullaby to you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who uplifted the clan of kausalai, who has abundantly fragrant black locks! Oh one who is the son-in-law of janaka maharājar who had great, lasting fame! Oh one who is the divine son of daśarata cakravarti! Oh one who has taken residence mercifully in tirukkaṇṇapuram which has more pious waters than river gangai! Oh one who is like a bluish coloured gemstone to me! Oh one who is the most enjoyable ambrosia in our royal clan! Oh śri rāma! My lullaby to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār sings lullaby to the one who was born as kausalai’s son and who became the son-in-law of janaka cakravarti.*

**kongu mali karum kuḻalāḷ kausalai than kula madalāy** – Oh one who uplifted the clan of kausalai dēvi, who had fragrant and dark locks!

**tangu perum pugaḻc canagan tirumarugā** – Oh one who is the son-in-law of king janaka who is the repository of all that has fame!

**dāsaradī** – Oh one who was born as the son of king daśarata, appropriate to become the son-in-law of king janaka.

**gangaiyilum tīrttamali kaṇapuratten karumaṇiyē** – Oh one who has taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram, which has more pious waters than the river gangai, and who is the epitome of simplicity! When emperumān took trivikrama avatāram (incarnation), his divine foot reached satyalōkam (the residence of brahmā). When brahmā washed his divine feet, the water which flowed from his divine feet came down as gangai. Hence, its piety can be known. Since the water bodies in tirukkaṇṇapuram such as nitya puśkariṇi etc have constant connection with emperumān, don’t these water bodies have more piety than gangai?

**engal kulattu innamudē! irāgavanē! tālēlō** – Oh one who incarnated in the clan of raghu and who is sweet, like ambrosia, for all of us, who were born in the regal clan!

**tālēlō** – lullaby to you.

We will take up the 4th pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 8.4. Tamarai mel

*tāmaraimēl ayanavanaip padaittavanē! dayaradan tanmāmadalāy! mytili tan maṇavāḷā! vaṇḍinangaḷkāmarangaḷ isai pādum kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē!ēmaruvenjilai valavā! irāgavanē! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

tāmarai mēl – on the divine navel, which is like a lotus; ayan avanai – brahmā; padaittavanē – Oh one who created!; dayaradan than – daśarata’s; mā madalāy – Oh the eldest son!; mytili than maṇavāḷā – Oh the consort of sītāp pirātti!; vaṇḍinangaḷ – swarms of beetles; kāmarangaḷ isai pādum – such that they hum in a rāgam called as kāmaram; kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē – Oh one who is like a bluish gemstone, residing mercifully in tirukkaṇṇapuram!; E maru(vu) – one which will incite (anyone who holds it) to shoot; vem silai valavā – Oh one who controls the dangerous bow called as śrī śārngam!; tālēlō – my lullaby to you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who created brahmā on the divine lotus-like navel! Oh one who is the eldest son of daśarata! Oh one who is the consort of sītāp pirātti! Oh one who is like a blue coloured gemstone who has taken residence mercifully in tirukkaṇṇapuram, where swarms of beetles are humming the kāmara tune! Oh one who controls the dangerous bow called as śrī śārngam which will incite (anyone who holds it) to shoot arrows! Oh śrī rāma! My lullaby to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār sings lullaby to emperumān who created brahmā from his lotus-like navel, who was born as the eldest son of daśarata and who was the consort of mytili \[sītāp pirātti\].*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tāmarai mēl ayan avanaip padaittavanē** – when queried if the role of creation \[of the worlds\] is brahmā’s as mentioned in the second pāsuram of this decad, āḻvār says that emperumān created even that brahmā on his divine lotus-like navel.

**dayaradan than māmadalāy** – Oh one who was born as the son of daśarata cakravarti, in order to remove the deficiency of daśarata who was without an heir for sixty thousand years!

**mytili than maṇavāḷā** – more than the pride of having sired brahmā and greater than the eminence of being the son of daśarata cakravarti, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 37-18 ślōkam “*apramēyam hi tattējō yasya sā janakātmajā* l *na tvam samarsttas tām hartum rāmacāpāśrayam vanē* ll” (\[mārīca tells rāvaṇa when the latter seeks the former’s help in abducting sītāp pirātti from daṇḍakāraṇya forest\] that daughter of janaka \[sītāp pirātti\] belongs to that resplendence of śrī rāma which is inestimable. You are not capable of stealing that pirātti, who has taken refuge under śrī rāma’s bow in the forest), oh one who has the pride of having sītāp pirātti, who is like the decorative ornament for the clan of women, to be born exclusively for you!

**vaṇḍinangaḷ kāmarangaḷ isai pādum kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē** – Oh one who has taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram where swarms of beetles hum the notes of *kāmaram* tune, and who is simplicity personified for me!

**E maru vem silai valavā irāgavanē tālēlō** – Oh śri rāghava, who is controlling the bow śrī śārngam, which will incite anyone who holds it to shoot arrows! My lullaby to you.

We will next take up the 5th pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.5. Paralum padar selvam

*pārāḷum padar selvam barada nambikkē aruḷiārā anbu iḷaiyavanōdu arungāna(m) adaindavanē!sīrāḷum varai mārbā! tirukkaṇṇapurattarasē!tārāḷum nīṇmudi en dāsaradī! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

pār āḷlum padar selvam – huge wealth of kingdom through which the entire earth could be ruled; baradan nambikkē – to bharatāḻvān who is complete in all auspicious qualities; aruḷi – mercifully giving to him; ārā anbu iḷaiyavanōdu – along with iḷaiya perumāḷ \[lakśmaṇan\] who has affection without any shortcoming; aru kānam adaindavanē – Oh one who mercifully went to forest which could not be entered by anyone!; sīr āḷum varai mārbā – Oh one who has the divine chest which is the residence of veeralakśmi (wealth of courage)!; tirukkaṇṇaurattu arasē – Oh one who is the ruler of tirukkaṇṇapuram!; tār āḷum nīṇ mudi – Oh (one who has) the long crown with garland; en dāsaradī – my śrī rāma!; tālēlō – lullaby to you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who mercifully gave the huge wealth of kingdom, through which the entire earth could be ruled, to bharatāḻvān, who is complete in all auspicious qualities! Oh one who mercifully went, along with lakśmaṇa who has affection for you without any deficiency, to the forest which is impossible to enter for anyone! Oh one who has the divine chest where the deity for courage is residing! Oh one who is the ruler of tirukkaṇṇapuram! Oh śrī rāma, who has the long crown with garland! My lullaby to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār sings lullaby to śrī rāma who handed over the reins of the kingdom to bharata and who went to the forest, along with lakśmaṇa.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pārāḷum padar selvam barada nambikkē aruḷi** – emperors rule the entire earth through the wealth of their kingdom. Mercifully handing over the reins of such a kingdom to bharatan, who is full of auspicious qualities including *pāratantryam* by which he existed solely for emperumān obeying him fully.

**ārā anbu iḷaiyavanōdu arum gānam adaindavanē** – śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 31-24 says “*kuruśva mām anucaram vaidarmyam nēha vidyatē* l *krutārtō’ham bhaviśyāmi tava cārta prakalpatē* ll” (Make me as your servitor. There is nothing which is unrighteous in this. (Through this) I will be grateful. Your objective will also be accomplished). As mentioned in this, oh one who went along with iḷaiya perumāḷ, who would always carry out acts of servility, to forest where no one goes, and which is full of wild animals!

**sīrāḷum varai mārbā** – Oh one who has chest which is like a mountain and in which the deity of courage resides!

**tirukkaṇṇapurattu arasē** – Oh one who is the chief of tirukkaṇṇapuram!

**tārāḷum nīṇmudi en dāsadī tālēlō** – Oh one who wears the garland, which indicates that you are the head of entire world, on your divine crown, and who is the son of emperor daśaratan! My lullaby to you.

We will next take up the 6th pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.6. Surramellam pin todara

*suṟṟamellām pintodara tol kānam adaindavanē!aṟṟavargat(ku) arumarundē! ayōddi nagarkk(u) adipadiyē!kaṟṟavargaḷ tām vāḻum kaṇapuratt(u) en karumaṇiyē!siṟṟavai tan soṟ koṇḍa sīrāmā! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

suṟṟam ellām pin todara – followed by all relatives; tol gānam adaindavanē – Oh one who went to the ancient forest!; aṟṟavargatku – for your devotees who exist only for you; aru marundē – Oh one who is like an eminent medicine!; ayōddi nagarkku adipadiyē – Oh who is the chief of divine ayōdyā!; kaṟṟavargaḷ tām vāḻum – inhabited by learned sagacious people; kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē – Oh one who is like a sweet, bluish gemstone for me, in tirukkaṇṇapuram!; siṟṟavai than – kaikēyi, your aunt, her; sol koṇḍa sīrāmā – Oh śrī rāmapirān who followed her words obediently!; tālēlō – lullaby to you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who went to the old forest, followed by all your relatives! Oh one who is like a special medicine for your devotees who exist only for you! Oh one, who is the chief of divine ayōdyā! Oh one, who is like an endearing bluish gemstone for me, in tirukkaṇṇapuram which is inhabited by learned sagacious people! Oh one, who listened to the words of your aunt, kaikēyi! My lullaby to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār sings lullaby in this pāsuram, to the head of ayōdyā \[śrī rāma\] who went to the forest followed by all his relatives, and who showered his mercy on the sages dwelling in the forest.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**suṟṟamellām pin todara tol gānam adaindavanē** – Oh one who went to the forest, followed by all the relatives! In the commentary for this pāsuram, vyākyātā (one who mercifully wrote the commentary) periyavāccān piḷḷai, quotes a narrative which happened during the time of śrī rāmānujar. When the vyākhyānam for this pāsuram was being told by rāmānujar, those in the assembly queried him “All the relatives did not go to the forest. After going for some distance, they returned” Is this correct? rāmānujar quoted śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam ślōkam 31-25 “*bhavāmstu saha vaidēhyā girisānuśu ramsyatē* l *aham sarvam kariśyāmi jāgratas svapataśca tē* ll” (You, along with sītāp pirātti, would be enjoying on the mountain ridges. I will do all the kainkaryams (services) to you, both when you are awake and when you are sleeping). Just as it has been mentioned in this ślōkam, since iḷaiya perumāḷ went along with śrī rāma, there is no harm in saying that all the relatives followed śrī rāma, to the forest.

**aṟṟavargatku aru marundē** – sages in the forest told śrī rāma as in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 1-21 ślōkam “*nyastadaṇḍā vayam rājan*! *jitakrōdā jitēndriyā*: l *rakśitavyāstvayā śaśvat garbhabhūtās tapōdanā*: ll” (Oh king! We have conquered our anger and our senses (in matters related to demons). Our tapas (penance) is our wealth. We are to be protected by you always, like the child inside the (mother’s) womb). śrī rāma, at a later point in time, told sītāp pirātti, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam āranya kāṇḍam 10-19 ślōkam “*apyaham jīvitam jahyām tvām vā sītē sa lakśmaṇām* l *na tu pratijñām samśrutya brāhmaṇēbhyō viśēśata*: ll” (Oh sītā! I may give up my life. I may give up lakśmaṇa and even you. But I will never give up on my word, especially one made to brāhmaṇas). Just as it has been said in these ślōkams, oh one who is like a rare medicine, protecting life!

**ayōddi nagarkku adipadiyē** – vēdas refer to mōkśalōkam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) as viṣṇu’s paramapadam and as ayōdyā (that which cannot be defeated in war). Oh one who rules from the place whose capital is ayōdyā!

**kaṟṟavargaḷ tām vāḻum kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē** – Oh one who is dear like a bluish gemstone, residing in tirukkaṇṇapuram which is inhabited by wise people who have learnt and understood! Those who attempt to attain emperumān through *upāsanā* (bhaktiyōgam, or the path of devotion) have to perform devotion in one place, at one time, reach paramapadam and then enjoy him. Unlike that, oh one who is remaining in tirukkaṇṇapuram, where we can go and worship you in tirukkaṇṇapuram, which is on the southern side!

**siṟṟavai than sol kōnda sīrāmā tālēlō** – even while śrī kausalaiyār (kausalyā dēvi) followed śrī rāma \[as he was preparing to go to the forest\] saying “I, who gave birth to you, will not sustain myself, if separated from you”, taking upon the words of your aunt, kaikēyi, oh one who went to the forest! My lullaby to you.

We will next take up the 7th pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.7. Alin ilaip palaganay

*ālin ilaip pālaganāy anṟu ulagam uṇḍavanē!vāliyaik kon(ṟu) arasu iḷaiya vānarattuk(ku) aḷittaanē!kālin maṇi karai alaikkum kaṇapurat(tu) en karumaṇiyē!āli nagark(ku) adipadiyē! ayōddimanē! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – on that day when deluge took place; ālin ilai – on a tender banyan leaf; bālaganāy – in the form of a child; ulagam – all the worlds; uṇḍavanē – Oh one who protected by keeping inside \[your\] stomach!; vāliyaik konṟu – killing vāli; iḷaiya vānarattukku – to his younger brother, sugrīva; arasu aḷittavanē – Oh one who handed over the kingdom!; kālin – wind’s; maṇi karai alaikkum – bringing the gemstones (from the ocean); kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē – Oh my bluish gemstone who has mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram!; Ali nagarkku – for the town of tiruvāli; adipadiyē – Oh one who is the controller!; ayōddimanē – Oh the king of ayōddi!; tālēlō – my lullaby to you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who lay on a tender banyan leaf after protecting all the worlds, during the time of deluge, by keeping them in your stomach! Oh one who handed over the kingdom to sugrīva, the younger brother of vāli, after killing vāli! Oh my bluish gemstone, who has mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram where wind brings the gemstones (from the ocean) to its shore! Oh one who is the controller of the divyadēśam tiruvāli! Oh one who is the king of ayōddi! My lullaby to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār sings lullaby to emperumān who lay on a banyan leaf, swallowing all the worlds, and handing over the kingdom to sugrīva, after killing vāli \[sugrīva’s elder brother\].*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ālin ilaip pālaganāy anṟu ulagam uṇḍavanē** – when *pralayam* (deluge) occurred, you had swallowed all the seven worlds in your divine stomach and protected them, lying on a tender banyan leaf. Even after that, just as it has been mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 11-5-3 “*ēḻulagamuṇḍum idam udaittu*” (even after eating the seven worlds, there was space inside (the stomach)), all the worlds were contained in a small portion inside his stomach. It could be said that the expansiveness of the protector was so huge that the area occupied by the protected was insignificant. Oh one who has the ability to carry out *agaditaghatanā* (carry out impossible) tasks!

**vāliyaik konṟu arasu iḷaiya vānarattukku aḷittavanē** – vāli had tied down, to his tail, without taking support from anyone, the demon rāvaṇa, who you had defeated. Since he had not protected his younger brother, sugrīva, even after he had surrendered to vāli, you killed vāli and handed over his kingdom to sugrīva, who surrendered to you!

**kālin maṇi karai alaikkum kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē** – this could be construed as: tirukkaṇṇapuram where wind will bring all the gemstones from nearby ocean and strew them on the shore. Here, the term *kāl* refers to wind. Alternatively, *kāl* could be considered as canal, the backwater from ocean, from within which the gemstones will be strewn across the shore. Such tirukkaṇṇapuram!

**Ali nagarkku adipadiyē** – Oh the head of tiruvāli! In the vyākyāṇam, periyavāccān piḷḷai, very delightfully says that he took the support of Ali after killing vāli!

We will next take up the 8th pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.8. Malai adanal anai

*malai adanāl aṇai katti madiḷ ilangai aḻittavanē!alai kadalaik kadain(du) amararkku amudam aruḷic ceydavanē!kalai valavar tām vāḻum kaṇapurat(tu) en karumaṇiyē!silai valavā! sēvaganē! sīrāmā! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

malai adanāl aṇai katti – constructing a sētubandanam (bridge) with mountains; madiḷ ilangai aḻittavanē – Oh one who destroyed lankāpuri which had fortified walls!; alai kadalai kadaindu – churning the ocean which was throwing up waves; amararkku – for celestial entities; amudu aruḷic ceydavanē – Oh one who mercifully bestowed nectar!; kalai valavar tām vāḻum – inhabited by great people have learnt all the śāstras (traditional texts); kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē – Oh my bluish gemstone, who has mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram!; silai valavā – Oh one who is an expert in drawing śrī śārngam (the divine bow)!; sēvaganē – Oh warrior!; sirāmā – Oh śrī rāma!; tālēlō – lullaby to you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who constructed a bridge with mountains and destroyed lankā, which had fortified walls! Oh one who mercifully bestowed nectar to celestial entities by churning the milky ocean! Oh my bluish gemstone who has mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram which is inhabited by great people who are experts in śāstras! Oh one who is an expert in drawing śrī śārngam! Oh warrior! Oh śrī rāma! My lullaby to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār sings lullaby, in this pāsuram, to emperumān who destroyed lankā during rāmāvatāram and who offered nectar to celestial entities when he churned the milky ocean.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**malai adanāl aṇai katti madiḷ ilangai aḻittavanē** – Oh one who destroyed lankā, by building a bridge across the ocean, with mountains which have the property of sinking in water, and which was a protective fort for lankā, while lankā was further protected by its fortified walls!

**alai kadalaik kadaindu amararkku amudu aruḷic ceydavanē** – Oh one who churned the deep ocean to take out nectar and offered it to celestial entities so that they will not face death at the hands of demons!

**kalai valavar tām vāḻum kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē** – there are fourteen *vidyāstānams* (knowledge in arts) in śāstras, namely four vēdas, six auxiliaries, the prologue and epilogue in *mīmāmsai* (philosophy), nyāya śāstram (treatise on justice), itihāsa purāṇam and dharma śāstram (treatise on righteousness). tirukkaṇṇapuram is inhabited by experts on these śāstrams and who enjoy you in that place. Oh one who is my bluish gemstone, who has taken residence mercifully in such tirukkaṇṇapuram and who has the simplicity of ruling that place, by controlling such scholars!

**silai valavā** – Oh one who rules over śrī śārngam, the divine bow!

**sēvaganē tālēlō** – Oh one who is such a great warrior that he doesn’t need even that bow! My lullabies to you.

We shall take up the 9th pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 8.9. Talai avilum naru

*taḷai aviḻum naṟungunjit tayaradan tan kula madalāy!vaḷaiya oru silai adanāl madiḷ ilangai aḻittavanē!kaḷaikaḻunīr marungalarum kaṇapurat(tu) en karumaṇiyē!iḷaiyavargat(ku) aruḷudaiyāy! irāgavanē! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

taḷai aviḻum naṟu kunji – Oh one whose divine hair is fragrant and is so dense that it will get loosened from its well tied state!; dayaradan than – daśarata cakravarti’s (emperor’s); kulam madalāy – Oh one who uplifted the clan!; oru silai vaḷaiya – with a unique bow drawn; adanāl – through that bow; madiḷ ilangai aḻittavanē – Oh one who destroyed lankā which had fortified walls!; kaḷai kaḻu nīr – sengaḻunīr (red water lily) flowers which were plucked as a bunch; marungu alarum – to blossom all around; kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē – Oh my bluish gemstone who has mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram!; iḷaiyavargatku – in matters related to (your) younger brothers; aruḷ udaiyāy – Oh one who has lot of affection!; irāgavanē – Oh śrī rāma!; tālēlō – lullaby to you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one whose divine hair is fragrant and which gets loosened despite being tied well, and who uplifted the clan of daśarata cakravarti! Oh one who destroyed lankā with a unique bow which was drawn well! Oh my bluish gemstone who has mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram where bunches of plucked red water-lilies are blossoming all around! Oh one who has deep affection towards your younger brothers! Oh śrī rāma! My lullaby to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār sings lullaby to śrī rāma, who was the head of the clan of daśarata and who was the archer, who destroyed lankā.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**taḷai aviḻum narum kunjit tayaradan than kula madalāy** – oh one who is having divine hair which is fragrant and which is such dense that it will get loosened and fall down despite being tied well, and being the one who uplifted the clan of daśarata cakravarti!

**vaḷaiya oru silai adanāl madiḷ ilangai aḻittavanē** – oh one who destroyed lankā, which was so strong that it will make even eminent weapons such as *brahmāstra* (a powerful weapon given by brahmā) to be ineffective, with a unique bow handled by people!

**kaḷai kaḻu nīr marungu alarum kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē** – red water lily plant is a weed for paddy cultivation. When these plants are plucked out of the fields in tirukkaṇṇapuram by farmers and thrown on the sides, the flowers in them will blossom as if the plants were in their original plantation. Oh one who is mercifully residing in such tirukkaṇṇapuram and being the epitome of simplicity for me! The term *kaḷai kaḻu nīr marungu alarum* refers to the fertile nature of the soil.

**iḷaiyavargatku aruḷ udaiyāy** – Oh one who showered his affection on younger brothers and those such as guhan, sugrīvan and vibhīśaṇan who were like your brothers, saying “If they are not useful to my brothers, I do not need even my sustaining airs”

**irāgavanē tālēlō** – Oh śrī rāma! My lullabies to you.

We shall take up next, the 10th pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.10. Devaraiyum asuraraiyum

*dēvaraiyum asuraraiyum disaigaḷaiyum padaittavanē!yāvarum van(du) adi vaṇanga aranganagart tuyinṟavanē!kāviri nal nadi pāyum kaṇapurat(tu) en karumaṇiyē!ēvari venjilai valavā! irāgavanē! tālēlō*

**Пословный перевод:**

dēvaraiyum – celestial entities; asuraraiyum – (those who were born as their enemies) demonic entities; disaigaḷaiyum padaittavanē – oh one who created the worlds in all the directions!; yāvarum vandu adi vaṇanga – to enable all the living creatures to come and worship at the divine feet; aranga nagar – in śrī rangam; tuyinṟavanē – oh one who reclined!; kāviri nal nadi pāyum – such that river kāviri, which gives benefits, flows; kaṇapurattu en karumaṇiyē – oh my bluish gemstone who has mercifully taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram!; ēvari vem silai valavā – oh one who controls the bow which implores one to shoot \[arrows\], which is beautiful and which creates fear (for enemies); irāgavanē – oh śrī rāma!; tālēlō – lullaby to you

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār sings lullaby to emperumān who, apart from creating all the worlds with all jīvātmās such as dēvas (celestial entities) and asuras (demonic entities), is reclining at śrī rangam such that all cētanas (those with knowledge) could come and worship his divine feet.*

**dēvaraiyum asuraraiyum disaigaḷaiyum padaittavanē** – Oh one who created the natural enemies, celestial entities and demonic entities and who created spaces for them, to inhabit, in all the ten directions!

**yāvarum vandu adivaṇanga aranga nagart tuyinṟavanē** – vasn’t the purpose of creating all of them, only for them to come and take refuge under him? Oh one who came to tiruvarangam as arcāvataāram \[incarnation as idol form\], for that purpose, and reclined!

**kāviri nal nadi pāyum kaṇapurattu en karu maṇiyē** – Oh one who has come as the epitome of simplicity for all and taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram where the river kāviri, instead of having to flow through a dam where water is stored, comes on its own in full force and flows into the agricultural fields!

**ēvari vem silai valavā irāgavanē** – Oh one who controls, as per your wish, the bow śrī śārngam in your hands, a bow which will enable anyone, who holds it in his hands, to be an archer! It is a bow which is beautiful and which will destroy enemies by the mere stance in which it is held!

**irāagavanē tālēlō** – my lullabies to you.

We shall take up the last pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 8.11. Kanni nan mamadil

*kanni nanmāmadiḷ pudai sūḻ kaṇapuratt(tu) en kāguttantannadi mēl tālēlō enṟuraitta tamiḻ mālaikolnavilum vēl valavan kudaik kulasēkaran sonnapanniya nūl pattum vallār pāngāya pattargaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

en kāguttan tannadi mēḷ – with regard to my cakravarti’s divine son; tālēlō enṟu uraitta – pāsurams sung (by śrī kausalyai) as lullabies; kol navilum vēḷ valavan – one who is holding the spear which is engaged in killing; kudai kulasēkaran – kulaśēkhara āḻvār who has the regal white umbrella; kanni nan māmadiḷ pudai sūḻ kaṇapurattu – in tirukkaṇṇapuram which is surrounded on all four sides by the indestructible, great, beautiful, huge fortified walls; sonna – mercifully composed; panniya – being widespread; nūl – being distinguished; tamiḻ mālai – hymns which are in tamiḻ; pattum – all ten; vallār – those who learn; pāngu āya pattargaḷ – are devotees who are qualified to enjoy emperumān

**Перевод:**

**kulaśēkhara āḻvār, who has the regal, white coloured umbrella and who holds the spear which is engaged in killing \[enemies\], mercifully composed ten pāsurams in tamiḻ which are lullabies sung by śrī kausalyai on the divine son of cakravarti (daśaratan), śrī rāma. śrī rāma is residing in tirukkaṇṇapuram which is fortified with walls which are surrounding on all four sides, which are indestructible, great, beautiful and huge. Those who learn these ten pāsurams will be devotees of that śrī rāma, and are qualified to enjoy him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār brings the decad to an end by enunciating the benefits to those who learn this decad.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kanni nal māmadiḷ sūḻ kaṇapurattu en kāguttan** – emperumān who is mercifully in the standing posture in tirukkaṇṇapuram which is surrounded on all the sides by indestructible, great, beautiful and huge fortified walls. Even though the perumāḷ, who has mercifully come and taken residence in tirukkaṇṇapuram, is kaṇṇan only, āḻvār recited these pāsurams on emperumān assuming him to be śrī rāman only and not as kaṇṇan.

**tannadi mēl tālēlō enṟu uraitta tamiḻ mālai** – the lullabies sung by śrī kausalyā dēvi on her son, śrī rāman, in his infant days, which āḻvār composed as tamiḻ songs on tirukkaṇṇapuram emperumān.

**kol navilum vēl valavan kudai kulasēkaran sonna** – the manner in which āḻvār held the spear will make people exclaim “How cruel is the spear in destroying his enemies!”, along with the regal white umbrella which indicates that he is the king of all kings. Songs composed by such kulaśēkhara āḻvār.

**panniya nūl pattum vallār** – those who are experts in learning these pāsurams which have spread far and wide in tamiḻ speaking world, and which do not have any deficiency.

**pāngāya pattargaḷē** – they will get to enjoy emperumān in the same manner as kausalyā dēvi did, as a mother and kulaśēkhara āḻvār did, as a servitor.

Next, we shall take up the pravēśam of the 9th decad.

## 9.1. Van talin inai

*van tāḷin iṇai vaṇangi vaḷa nagaram toḻudētta mannanāvānninṟāyai ariyaṇai mēl irundāyai nedungānam padarappōguenṟāḷ em irāmāvō! unaip payanda kaikēsi tan soṟkēttunanṟāga nānilattai āḷvittēn nanmaganē! unnai nānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

em irāmāvō – Oh our śrī rāma!; val tāḷin iṇai vaṇangi – (after you) vorśipped the two strong, divine feet of nārāyaṇan (who always protects, without giving up, those who surrendered to him); vaḷam nagaram – people in the fertile city of ayōdyā; toḻudu ētta – to remain after performing anjali (cupping the hands together); mannan āvān ninṟāyai – one who was to be crowned as the king; ari aṇai mēḷ irundāyai – you, who were ready to get on to the regal throne; nedum kānam padara pōgu enṟāḷ – (kaikēyi) told (you) to go to the forest, which is difficult to enter; nal maganē – Oh my son, who always listens to my commands!; nān – I (daśarata cakravarti); unai payanda kaikēsi than sol kēttu – listening to the words of kaikēsi, who begot you; nanṟāga unnai nānilattai āḷvittēn – made you to rule this world, very beautifully

**Перевод:**

**Oh śrī rāma! After you had vorśipped the divine feet of śrīman nārāyaṇan, the people of the fertile city of ayōdyā saluted you as you were ready to be crowned as the king and were about to sit on the regal throne. Listening to the words of kaikēyi, who begot you, I made you to rule this world beautifully, by asking you to go to the forest, which is difficult to enter.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) \[āḻvār, in the pathos of daśaratan\] laments in this pāsuram that at the time of being crowned as the king, kaikēyi has connived to make you \[śrī rāma\] to go to the forest.*

**vyākhyānam(Commentary)**

**van tāḷin iṇai vaṇangi** – just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 6-1 “*saha patnyā viśālākśyā nārāyaṇam upāgamat*” (along with his wife, sītāp pirātti, who has wide eyes, śrī rāma vorśipped śrīman nārāyaṇa), as part of his ascending the throne \[of ayōdyā\], śrī rāma had vorśipped śrī ranganāta \[śrī ranganāta was in ayōdyā prior to coming to śrī rangam\]. Though daśarata cakravarti was ruling the entire world under his regal umbrella, apart from prostrating before him, his father, śrī rāma vorśipped the divine feet of periya perumāḷ, who had affirmed that he would never give up on his devotee. nammāḻvār, in his tiruvāimoḻi 1-2-10 had said “*vaṇ pugaḻ nāraṇan tiṇ kaḻal*” (śrīman nārāyaṇan has auspicious qualities; his divine feet will not give up \[his devotes who attain them\]). This verse is in line with nammāḻvār’s verse.

**vaḷa nagaram toḻudētta** – the city of ayōdyā was well decorated in anticipation of the ascension event. Such that the people of the city would worship and praise . . . .

**mannanāvān ninṟāyai** – having completed all the activities which were to be done as part of the ascension to the throne, you, who had tied the auspicious thread on your wrist . . .

**ariyaṇai mēl irundāyai** – you, who had almost had one foot on the throne, having completed all the preparatory activities towards that

**nedungānam padarap pōgu enṟāḷ** – just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 1-30 “*tē vanēna vanam gatvā nadīstīrtvā bahūdakā*:” (they (sītāp pirātti, śrī rāma and lakśmaṇa) travelled from one forest to another, crossing many waters), kaikēyi told you to go to from one forest to another, which was deeper than the earlier one. And which, leave alone kings, even common people will not enter.

**em irāmāvō** – in line with the etymology “*ramayati itirāma*:” (rāma is one who makes everyone to be happy), your name refers to the quality of removing all hurdles to those who think of you or who recite your name or who see you. She \[kaikēyi\] asked such a person to go to the forest.

**unaip payanda kaikēsi than sol kēttu** – śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam ślōkam 7-35 says “*rāmē vā bharatē vāham viśēśam nōpalakśayē* l *tasmāt tuśtāsmi yadrājā rāmam rājyē’bhiśēkśyati* ll” (I do not see any difference between śrī rāma and bharata. Hence, I am happy about śrī rāma being anointed as the king). kaikēyi was more affectionate towards śrī rāma than his mother, kōsalai. Due to this, I \[daśarata\], desirous of seeing her happiness, told her about my decision of anointing you for the crown, even ahead of kōsalai. Hearing the words of such kaikēyi, who was more than your biological mother . . . .

**nanṟāga nānilattai āḷvittēn** – she \[kaikēyi\], who was good, became deceptive, after hearing the words of kūni \[mantara, kaikēyi’s servitor\]. Not knowing this, thinking that she is your mother, I told her about the ascension to the throne. But I got caught in the quagmire of her boons. The way I made you to rule the entire world was indeed beautiful \[with sarcasm\].

**nan maganē unnai nānē** – though I had done like this \[to you\], you stood in the righteous path of listening to and holding true the words of parents. I had given you my word that I will make you to ascend the throne; but, due to the infatuation which I had towards kaikēyi, I could not deny her the boons which she had sought and as a result, I went back on my word to you.

We will next take up the second pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.2. Vevvayen vevvurai

*vevvāyēn vevvurai kēt(tu) irunilattai vēṇḍādē viraindu venṟimaivāya kaḷiṟoḻindu tēroḻindu māvoḻindu vanamē mēvineyvāya vēl nedungaṇ nēriḻaiyum iḷangōvum pinbu pōgaevvāṟu nadandanai? emmirāmāvō! emperumān! en seygēnē?*

**Пословный перевод:**

em irāmāvō – Oh our śrī rāma!; vevvāyēn vevvurai kēttu – after hearing harsh words from my cruel mouth; irunilattai vēṇḍādē – not desiring to rule the expansive earth; viraindu – very quickly; venṟi maivāya kaḷiṟu oḻindu – leaving aside the elephant, which brings victory, which is like a black mountain and which has a huge form; tēr oḻindu – leaving aside the chariot too; mā oḻindu – leaving aside the horse too; vanamē mēvi – reaching the forest; neyvāya vēl nedum kaṇ nēr iḻaiyum – (sītāp) pirātti, having wide eyes which are sharp like a well-oiled spear and appropriately decorated with ornaments; iḷangōvum – and iḷaya perumāḷ \[lakśmaṇa\]; pinbu pōga – following; evvāṟu nadandanai – (not being used to walking long distances) how did you walk?; emperumān – Oh my svāmy! (lord); en seygēn – what will I do?

**Перевод:**

**Oh our śrī rāma! After hearing the harsh words from my cruel mouth \[that you have to go to forest\], not desirous of ruling this expansive earth, leaving the divisions of elephants, which are huge, victorious and which look like black mountains, of chariots and of horses too, you had quickly set out on your journey to the forest. pirātti, who has wide eyes which are like a well-oiled spear and who had appropriate ornaments, and iḷaiya perumāḷ, followed you to the forest. Oh my lord! How did you walk \[to the forest\]? What will I do?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, he laments about how śrī rāma, giving up all his wealth, with sītāp pirātti and lakśmaṇa following him, walked in the cruel forest.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vevvāyēn vevvurai kēttu** – I have a mouth which spits fire. With that mouth, I had told you “You have to proceed to the forest; you have to give up kingdom”. Listening to these words …

**iru nilattai vēṇḍādē** – deceiving all the people \[of ayōdyā\] who followed you, when you went to the forest, saying “We cannot sustain ourselves without you” and giving them up …

**viraindu** – going very quickly, lest kaikēyi thought that you were desirous of ascending the throne

**venṟi maivāya kaḷiṟoḻindu tēroḻindu māvoḻindu** – leaving aside the victorious divisions of the army such as elephants which were huge, looking like black mountains, the chariots and the horses

**vanamē mēvi** – though you had given up all these divisions, had you gone to another country such as kōsala nādu (the place of kausalyā dēvi) kaikēyi would have doubted whether you were intending to wage a war from that country. Hence, you went directly to the forest.

**neyvāya vēl nedungaṇ nēriḻaiyum iḷangōvum pinbu pōga** – while it was bad enough that you had to go, did you not take sītāp pirātti, who is not deserving to go to the forest, who had the beauty of eyes and ornaments, as well as lakśmaṇa, your younger brother, along with you?

**evvāṟu nadandanai emmirāmā O emperumān en seygēnē** – how did you, who are a prince and not used to walking, go with these two to the cruel forest, walking? Oh my lord, what will I do?

We will next take up the 3rd pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.3. Kollanai vel

*kollaṇai mēl vari nedungaṇ kausalai tan kula madalāy! kuni villēndummallaṇainda varaittōḷā! valvinaiyēn manamurukkum vagaiyē kaṟṟāymellaṇai mēl mun tuyinṟāy inṟu inippōy viyan kāna marattin nīḻalkallaṇaimēl kaṇ tuyilak kaṟṟaṇaiyō? kāguttā! kariya kōvē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

kol aṇai – engaged in killing; vēl – like a spear; vari nedum kaṇ – having eyes with reddish and blackish lines as well as being expansive; kausalai than – śrī kausalyā’s; kula madalāy – Oh one who came to uplift the clan!; kuni vil ēndum – one who has the curved bow; mal aṇainda varai tōḷā – Oh one who has divine shoulders which are like strong mountains!; val vinaiyēn – this cruel sinner’s; manam urukkum vagaiyē kaṟṟāy – Oh one who melted the heart (with the beauty of your weapons and your shoulders)!; kariya kōvē – Oh one who is my lord, with the complexion of a bluish cloud!; mel aṇai mēl mun tuyinṟāy – you, who had earlier slept on soft mattresses; inṟu ini pōy – from now onvards, going into the forests; viyan kānam marattin nīḻal – under the shadow of a (leafless) tree, in the cruel forest; kal ānai mēḷ – having stones as mattress; kaṇ tuyila kaṟṟanaiyō – did you learn to sleep?

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who came to uplift the clan of śrī kausalyā, who has expansive eyes with reddish and blackish lines, and which are like a spear engaged in killing! Oh one who has the curved bow! Oh one who has divine shoulders which are like strong mountains! Oh one who melted this cruel sinner’s heart! Oh one who incarnated in the clan of king kakutsta! Oh one who is my lord and who has the complexion of a bluish cloud! You had earlier slept \[inside the palace\] on soft mattresses. From now onvards, going into the forest, under a tree’s shadow, how did you learn to sleep on hard rocks?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) He laments in this pāsuram “Did you, who are used to sleeping on soft mattresses, learn to sleep on hard rocks in the forests?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kol aṇai mēl vari nedungaṇ kausalai than kula madalāy** – Oh one who came to uplift the clan of śrī kausalyai who has wide eyes which have reddish and blackish lines and which are radiant like a spear which is engaged in killing!

**kuni vil ēndum mal aṇainda varait tōḷā** – the bows of archers will be ever ready with the cord drawn and arrows ready to be shot; as a result, it would be curved always. This is referred to in the verse *kuni vil ēndum*. Through the verse *mal aṇainda varait tōḷā*, āḻvār says that even that bow is not required since his shoulders have the strength of a mountain.

**val vinaiyēn manam urukkum vagaiyē kaṟṟāy** – Oh one who has as his nature, the art of melting my heart with the beauty of your weapon and your shoulders!

**mel aṇai mēl mun tuyinṟāy** – earlier, in ayōdyā, you used to sleep on beautiful mattress.

**inṟu inippōy** – you, who used to sleep on beautiful mattresses in distinguished places such as residential quarters of mothers, from now onvards, in the times to come

**viyan kāna marattin nīḻal** – under the shadow of tress which do not have leaves, inside the cruel forests. Through the term *viyan kāna maram* it is indicated that living in forest is loathsome even for those who are habitual dwellers of forest

**kallaṇai mēl kaṇ tuyilak kaṟṟanaiyō** – have you learnt to sleep on rocks as mattresses?

**kāguttā kariya kōvē** – these activities are not appropriate for your lineage; these are not appropriate for the beauty of your divine form too.

We will next take up the 4th pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.4. Va pogu va

*vā pōgu vā innam vand(u) oru kāl kaṇḍu pō malarāḷ kūndalvēy pōlum eḻil tōḷi tan poruttā vidaiyōn tan villaic ceṟṟāy!mā pōgu nedungānam valvinaiyēn manamurukkum maganē! inṟunī pōga en nenjam iru piḷavāyp pōgādē niṟkumāṟē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

vā – come here, for a short while; pōgu – now, you can go; vā – come again; innam vandu oru kāl kaṇḍu pō – (when you keep coming and going) come again, look at me and then go; malarāḷ kūndal – one who always has flowers on her locks; vēy pōlum – like a bamboo; eḻil tōḷi than poruttu A – for the sake of sītāp pirātti who has beautiful shoulders; vidaiyōn than villai – Sivan’s bow; seṟṟāy – Oh one who broke!; val vinaiyēn manam urukkum maganē – Oh \[my\] child who is melting this great sinner’s mind!; inṟu – now; nī – you; mā pōgum nedum kānam pōga – to go the forest where elephants are roaming; en nenjam – my mind; iru piḷavāy pōgādē niṟkum āṟē – is remaining without being split into two!

**Перевод:**

**As you keep coming and going, look at me and then go. Oh one who broke Sivan’s bow for the sake of sītāp pirātti who always has flower on her locks and who has beautiful shoulders like a bamboo! Oh my child who is melting this sinner’s mind! Even as you are now proceeding to the forest where elephants are roaming, my mind is remaining intact, without having been split into two!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār \[as daśarata\] laments in this pāsuram that śrī rāma, who had protected Siva’s bow and married sītāp pirātti, has gone to the forest where wild animals roam and that despite this, his \[daśarata’s\] heart has not broken.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vā pōgu vā**– this shows the attachment that daśarata had towards śrī rāma. If he had not seen śrī rāma for a short while, daśarata will ask sumantran \[his minister\] to bring śrī rāma so that he could see his beauty from the front. Once he has seen the frontal beauty, in order to see the way he was holding the bow and the quiver, daśarata will ask śrī rāma to go. Again, he will beckon śrī rāma to come.

**innam vandu oru kāl kaṇḍu pō** – once śrī rāma returns, daśarata will ask him to look him up and then go. That he kept doing this with śrī rāma shows the deep affection which he had towards śrī rāma, is the opinion here.

**malarāḷ kūndal** – being constantly decorated with flowers, without a break, on her locks

**vēy pōlum tōḷi than poruttā** – for the sake of sītāp pirātti who has beautiful and bamboo like shoulders. Bamboo has been shown here as an allegory to highlight the freshness and roundness of sītāp pirātti’s shoulders.

**vidaiyōn than villaic ceṟṟāy** – Oh one who broke the bow of Sivan, who is strong and who has bull as his vehicle.

**mā pōgu nedungānam** – forest where wild animals such as lion, tiger, elephant etc roam around. The term *mā* refers to elephants and other wild animals.

**valvinaiyēn manam urukkum maganē** – you had made me happy by making me witness your beauty and bravery around fourteen years of age, beyond your infancy and childhood days. You made me extremely happy with the way you had subdued sage paraśurāma. You are now melting my heart since I have sinned so much that I cannot continue to see such acts of valour.

**inṟu nī pōga en nenjam iru piḷavāyp pōgādē niṟkumāṟē** – my heart, which melts when I see you, is not breaking into two when you disappear from my sight, but is remaining strong. I do not know as to how this is happening!

We will next take up the 5th pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.5. Porundar kai

*porundār kai vēl nudi pōl paral pāya melladikkaḷ kurudi sōravirumbāda kān virumbi veyiluṟaippa vembasi nōy kūra inṟuperumbāviyēn maganē! pōginṟāy kēkayar kōn magaḷāyp peṟṟaarumbāvi soṟ kētta aruvinaiyēn en seygēn? andō yānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

porundār kai vēl nudi pōl – just like the sharp end of a spear in the hands of enemies; paral pāya – with the stones hitting (the foot); mel adikkaḷ – the soft feet; kurudi sōra – starting to bleed; veyil uṟaippa – with the sun scorching; vembasi nōy kūra – with the cruel hunger becoming acute; perumbāviyēn maganē – oh the son of this great sinner!; virumbāda kān virumbi pōginṟāy – you are going willingly towards the forest (just because I had asked you to go to), which no one desires to go to; kēkayar kōn magaḷāy peṟṟa – being born as daughter to the king of kēkaya country; arumbāvi – the great sinner, kaikēyi’s; sol kētta aruvinaiyēn yān – I, the great sinner, who listened to; andō en seygēn – alas! What remedial action could I take?

**Перевод:**

**With stones which are like the sharp edge of a spear which pierces enemies’ hands, hitting the soft feet such that the feet start bleeding, with the sun scorching, with hunger becoming unbearable, oh the son of this great sinner! You are willingly going towards the forest, which none desires to go to. I, the great sinner, had listened to the words of the cruel sinner kaikēyi, the daughter of kēkaya king. Alas! How could I atone for that?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) He laments in this pāsuram that having been born as the son of this great sinner \[daśaratan, talking about himself\] who listened to the words of the cruel sinner kaikēyi, śrī rāma had to go willingly to the harsh forest, which, none wanted to go to.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**porundār kai vēl nudi pōl paral pāya** – with the stones in the forest hitting like the sharp edge of a spear in the hands of enemies

**melladikkaḷ kuridi sōra** – the feet are so soft that they cannot bear the pain of walking even on a smooth floor \[in the palace\]. However, when they have to go on the sharp stones in the forest, they will start bleeding.

**virumbāda kān virumbi** – even those with firm legs will not desire to go to a forest. Just because I had asked you to go to, you are desirously going to the forest.

**veyil uṟaippa vembasi nōy kūra** – apart from the stones piercing the foot, the sun will also be scorching from above. At appropriate times, due to non-availability of food to eat, hunger will also become acute. You are going under these circumstances.

**inṟu perum pāviyēn maganē pōginṟāy** – haven’t you been compelled to go, only because you were born as the son of this great sinner?

**kēkayar kōn magaḷāyp peṟṟa arum pāvi sol kētta** – the king of kēkaya country has given birth to a heap of sins, as his daughter. I, who had got caught in her words ….

**aruvinaiyēn en seygēn andō yānē** – this act of mine has no atonement. How will I carry out any remedial action? Alas.

We will next take up the 6th pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.6. Amma enru

*ammā enṟ(u) ugandu aḻaikkum ārvaccol kēḷādē aṇisēr mārvamen mārvattidai aḻundat taḻuvādē muḻusādē mōvādu uccikaimmāvin nadaiyanna mennadaiyum kamalam pōl mugamum kāṇā(du)emmānai enmaganai iḻanditta iḻi tagaiyēn irukkinṟēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ammā enṟu ugandu aḻaikkum – calling out happily ayyā (master or father); ārvam sol – word, with affection; kēḷādē – not hearing; aṇi sēr mārvam – chest decorated with ornaments; en mārvattidai aḻunda – to press against my chest; taḻuvādē – not hugging tightly; ucci mōvādē – not smelling the top of your crown; kaimmāvin nadai anna mel nadaiyum – the beauty of the soft gait imitating the gait of an elephant; kamalam pōl mugamum – the divine radiance in the face, like that of a lotus; kāṇādu – not having seen; emmānai en maganai iḻanditta – I, who had lost my son who charmed me with the beauty of his gait; iḻi tagaiyēn – I, who had indulged in lowly activities; irukkinṟēnē – am still alive

**Перевод:**

**I am not hearing the happy word “ayyā” called out with affection by you. I am not hugging tightly your chest, decorated with ornaments, pressing tightly against my chest and I am not immersing in that ocean of happiness. I am not smelling the top of your crown. I am not seeing the beauty of your soft gait, which is similar to that of an elephant. I am not seeing your radiant face, which is like a lotus. Yet I am still alive, though I have lost my son who charmed me with the beauty of his gait.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār, in the pathos of daśarata, laments in this pāsuram that he has lost all the pleasures such as hearing the sweet words \[of śrī rāma\], hugging him, smelling the crown of his head etc and despite losing all these, that he is still alive.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ammā enṟu ugandu aḻaikkum** – not hearing your affectionate word *ayyā* even when there is no work to be carried out and without expecting any benefit

**aṇi sēr mārvam en mārvattidai aḻunda taḻuvādē muḻusādē** – not hugging me with your divine chest, which is decorated with ornaments, and not immersing in the ocean of happiness, just like it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-18-2 “*sō’pyēnam dvajavajrābjagrutacihnēna pāṇinā* l *samspruśyākruśya ca prītyā sugādam pariśasvajē* ll” (that kriṣṇa bhagavān touched akrūra with his divine hand which has been identified with flag, diamond and lotus, pulled him and hugged him well, affectionately).

**mōvādu ucci** – after hugging like this, not smelling the crown of the head and feeling happy

**kaimmāvin nadaiyanna mennadaiyum** – the gait which is as majestic as that of an elephant, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 3-28 “*matta mādanga gāminam*” (śrī rāma who walks like an exulting elephant).

**kamalam pōl mugamum kāṇādu** – not looking at the divine face which is equated with lotus for its characteristics such as blossoming nature, beauty etc

**emmānai en maganai** – my son who made me as his servitor due to the beauty of his gait

**iḻanditta iḻi tagaiyēn irukkinṟēnē** – he laments saying “Having indulged in such a lowly act \[of sending śrī rāma to the forest\], without perishing, should I be alive?”

We will take up the 7th pāsurtam of this decad, next.

## 9.7. Pu maruvu narungunji

*pūmaruvu naṟungunji punsadaiyāp punaindu pūndugil sēr algulkāmar eḻil viḻaluduttuk kalan aṇiyā(du) angangaḷ aḻagu māṟiēmaru tōḷ en pudalvan yān inṟu selattakka vanam tān sērdaltūmaṟaiyīr! idu tagavō? sumandiranē! vasittanē! solleer nīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tūmaṟaiyīr – Oh brāhmaṇas who recite the pious vēdam!; sumandiranē – (minister) Oh sumantra!; vasittanē – (advisor) Oh vasiśta!; pū maruvum naṟu kunji – having fragrant, divine hair always with flowers; pun sadai A punaindu – making into matted hair, in a disorderly way; pū tugil sēr algul – on the divine waist where beautiful silken dresses should have been worn; kāmar eḻil viḻal uduttu – making a rope out of grass, which, those who see it would desire to have, and wearing it; kalan aṇiyādu – not wearing ornaments; angangaḷ aḻagu māṟi – without the artificial beauty which that would have created; ēmaru tōḷ en magan – my son, whose shoulders will make enemies to fear; yān inṟu selattakka – where I (having reached old age) should have gone to; vanam – to the forest; tān sērdal idu – where he, who should have enjoyed pleasures, was going; idu tagavō – is this proper!; nīrē solleer – you (the elders, who know the path of righteousness) please speak out

**Перевод:**

**(My son śrī rāma) whose fragrant, divine hair is always decorated with flowers, is now made into disorderly, matted hair. His divine waist, which will be adorned with beautiful silken dress, is now adorned with a rope of grass. His divine limbs which are not wearing ornaments now, are without the artificial beauty which they would have created \[had he been wearing those ornaments\]. Oh sumantra! Oh vasiśta! My son, whose shoulders will create fear in the minds of enemies, instead of enjoying pleasures, is now going to forest, which I should have gone to. Is this proper? You please speak out.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram he calls sumantran (his minister) and vasiśtar (his advisor) and asks them whether it is correct for his son to go to forest when he should have actually retired to the forest.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pū maruvum naṟum kunji pun sadaiyāp punaindu** – the divine hair, which was continuously decorated with flowers such that there was fragrance in it, has now been made into matted hair, which no person would desire to see.

**pūndugil sēr algul kāmar eḻil viḻal uduttu** – For sixty thousand years, I have been seeking out and collecting good, beautiful silken dresses for you to wear on your divine waist. Instead of wearing these, you are now wearing grass, which those who see it would desire, converted into a rope. The opinion here is that though this may be good to look at, it would create irritation in the waist.

**kalan aṇiyādu angangaḷ aḻagu māṟi** – apart from the natural beauty of the limbs, with decoration, further beauty would be seen. Without that additional beauty

**ēmaru tōḷ en pudalvan** – my son śrī rāma, who would destroy those who impose hurdles on useful activities, by shooting arrows.

**yān inṟu selattakka vanam tān sērdal** – having ruled for sixty thousand years and having enjoyed all the pleasures in life, I should be going to the forest \[renouncing everything\]. My son, being soft, and having to enjoy the pleasures, going to the forest . . . . .

**tūmaṟaiyīr idu tagavō** – Oh brāhmaṇas who recite *vēdavākyams* (verses from vēdas) such as taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 “*patim viśvasyātmēśvaram śāśvatam śivam acyutam*” (nārāyaṇan is the one who is the ruler of all the worlds, the controller for himself, eternal, pious and does not let go of his devotees)! You please tell this. Is this proper?

**sumandiranē vasittanē solleer nīrē** – Oh sumantra who knows the traditional *rājadarmam* (regal statutes) and who has been advising the kings! You tell if this is correct. Oh vasiśtā! Being the ācārya (spiritual preceptor) for the *sūryavamśis* (those born in the clan of sun) and who has been preaching the statutes to the kings, please tell if this is correct.

We will next take up the 8th pāsuram of this decad/

## 9.8. Pon perrar

*pon peṟṟār eḻil vēdap pudalvanaiyum tambiyaiyum pūvai pōlummin paṟṟā nuṇ marungul melliyal en marugiyaiyum vanattiṟ pōkkinin paṟṟā nin magan mēl paḻi viḷaittittu ennaiyum nīḷ vānil pōkkaen peṟṟāy? kaikēsī! irunilattil inidāga irukkinṟāyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaikēsī – Oh the daughter of kēkaya king!; pon peṟṟār eḻil vēdam pudalvanaiyum – śrī rāma who learnt all the vēda śāstrams (traditional texts) from teachers who have the wealth of education; tambiyaiyum – (his) brother lakśmaṇa; pūvai pōlum – like the bird pūvai (bush mynā); min paṟṟā nuṇ marungul – having such a slender waist that even lightning cannot match it; mel iyal en marugiyaiyum – pirātti, my daughter-in-law, who has a soft nature; nin paṟṟu Am – one who is affectionate towards you; nin magan mēl – on your son, bharata; paḻi viḷaittittu – bringing a blame; ennaiyum nīḷ vānil pōkka – (due to sadness of separating from son) making me too to go to the world above, which is far; en peṟṟāy – what benefit did you get?; iru nilattil – in this expansive earth; inidu āga – comfortably; irukkinṟāyē – you are alive

**Перевод:**

**Oh the daughter of kaikēyi! You have made śrī rāma, who has learnt all the vēda śāstrams from great, learned teachers, along with his younger brother, lakśmaṇa, and my daughter-in-law, sītāp pirātti, whose waist is thinner than even lightning, to go the forest. You have brought blame on bharata, who is affectionate towards you. You have made me too, to go the world far above. What benefit did you get (out of all these)? In this expansive earth, you are living comfortably.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) He reviles kaikēyi, asking her as to what benefit she had reaped after sending śrī rāma, sītāp pirātti and lakśmaṇa to the forest, bringing blame to bharata and sending him to the ethereal world.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pon peṟṟār eḻil vēdap pudalvanaiyum tambiyaiyum** – Teachers are considered by everyone to be wealthy people (wealth of education). śrī rāma had learnt from them all the texts which are beautiful. Both he and his younger brother, who is inseparable from him . . .

**pūvai pōlum min paṟṟā nuṇ marungul melliyal en marugiyaiyum** – looking like the bird *pūvai* (bush mynā), having a slender waist, similar to lightning, and having soft characteristics, sītāp pirātti, my daughter-in-law

**vanattil pōkki** – sending \[the three of them\] to the forest

**nin paṟṟā nin magan mēl paḻi viḷaittittu** – creating a blame on bharata, who does not consider anyone other than you as his mother, “He carried out a treacherous act on his brother”

**ennaiyum nīḷ vānil pōkka en peṟṟāy kaikēsī** – making me too to ascend the world above, what did you get as the benefit for all these acts?

**iru nilattil inidāga irukkinṟāyē** – the greatest happiness that one can get out of this samsāram (materialistic realm) is to be together with husband and children. You have sent śrī rāma, who considered you as his mother, to the forest and you have sent me, your husband, to the world above. He \[daśarata\] reviles kaikēyi, telling her “You are going to enjoy the pleasures of this world very well!”

We will next take up the 9th pāsuram of this decad.
