## 9.9. Mun oru nal

*munnoru nāḷ maḻuvāḷi silai vāngi avan tavattai muṟṟum seṟṟāyunnaiyum unnarumaiyaiyum un mōyin varuttamum onṟāgak koḷḷāduennaiyum en mey uraiyum meyyāgakkoṇḍu vanam pukka endāy!ninnaiyē maganāgap peṟap peṟuvēn ēḻ piṟappum nedundōḷ vēndē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

mun oru nāḷ – at an earlier point of time; maḻu āḷi silai vāngi – taking the bow of paraśurāma, who has axe as his weapon; avan tavattai muṟṟum seṟṟāy – destroyed the entire benefit of his penance (as your arrow’s target); unnaiyum – your eminence; un arumaiyaiyum – the difficulties which I had to endure in order to beget you as my son; un mōyin varuttamum – the sorrow brought on your mother (due to your separation); onṟu āgak koḷḷādu – not considering as material; ennaiyum – me; en mey uraiyaiyum – my truthful words; meyyāga – considering them as truth; vanam pukka endāy – Oh my lord who was ready to go to the forest; nedum tōḷ vēndē – Oh king, who has long shoulders (for the purpose of protecting)!; ēḻ piṟappum – in all the births which I will be taking, hereafter; ninnaiyē maganāgap peṟuvēn – let me have you as my son

**Перевод:**

**At an earlier point of time, you took the bow of paraśurāma, who has axe as his weapon, and destroyed the entire benefit of his penance. Not considering your eminence, the difficulties which I had endured to beget you as my son as well as the sorrow brought on your mother, you decided to go to the forest only in order to make my words as trustworthy. Oh king, who has long shoulders! Let me have you as my son in all the births which I will be taking from now onvards.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram he praises śrī rāma, who observed pitruvacana paripālanam (nurturing father’s word) saying “Oh my lord, you went to the forest in order to make my word truthful, disregarding the sorrow that your father and mother were to suffer from. I would like to beget you as my son in the next seven births too”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**munnoru nāḷ maḻu āḷi silai vāngi** – at an earlier point in time, when you were returning after marrying sītāp pirātti, you took the bow *śrīśarngam* from paraśurāman, who has paraśu, the axe, as his weapon

**avan tavattai muṟṟum seṟṟāy** – Oh one who destroyed the entire benefits of his penance, such as attaining other words such as heaven etc, through your arrow!

**unnaiyum un arumaiyaiyum** – your eminence and the difficulties which I had to endure to beget you as my son, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam ślōkam 66-3 “*mahatā tapasā rāma mahatā cāpi karmaṇā* l *rājyā daśaratēnāsi labhdō’mrutam ivāmarai* ll” (Oh Rama! You were attained by king daśarata due to great penance and great acts, just as celestial entities attained ambrosia)

**un mōyin varuttamum onṟāgak koḷḷādu** – not considering even the sorrow of your mother kausalyā who tried to follow you to the forest saying “I will not sustain myself in separation from you”

**ennaiyum en mey uraiyaiyum meyyāgak koṇḍu vanam pukkāy** – instead of knowing that only the affection which I had towards you is truthful, you thought of me as only your father. Oh my lord who went to the forest, thinking “I should not make my father, who has been speaking only truth all along, to be spoken of as having told lies after I was born”!

**ninnaiyē maganāgap peṟap peṟuvēn ēḻpiṟappum nedundōḷ vēndē** – After this, I should be born many times and in each of those births I should attain the benefit of having you as my son.

**nedundōḷ vēndē** – Oh one who has strength in your shoulders, much more than what is required in order to protect the land which is to be protected!

Next, we will take up 10th pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.10. Ten nagu mamalar

*tēn nagu māmalark kūndal kausalaiyum sumittiraiyum sindai nōvakkūnuruvin kodum toḻuttai soṟkētta kodiyavaḷ tan soṟkoṇ(du) inṟukānagamē miga virumbi nī tuṟanda vaḷanagarait tuṟandu nānumvānagamē miga virumbip pōginṟēn manu kulattār tangaḷ kōvē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

manu kulattār tangaḷ kōvē – Oh the greatest among all the kings who came in the lineage of manu!; tēn nagu – letting honey to flow; mā malar – being with eminent flowers; kūndal – having locks; kausalaiyum sumittiraiyum – kausalyā and sumitrā \[wives of king daśaratan\], these two; sindai nōva – their minds to feel sad; kūn uruvin kodum toḻuttai – kūni, the servant maid, whose mind is also crooked, just like her physical form; sol kētta kodiyavaḷ than – kaikēyi, who listened to her \[kūni’s\] words; sol koṇḍu – caught in her words; kānagamē miga virumbi nī tuṟanda – the town that you gave up, desiring the forest; vaḷam nagarai – this ayōdyā town, which has been decorated (for your coronation); nānum tuṟandu – I too am giving up; vānagamē miga virumbi pōginṟēn – desiring to go to the world above \[svarga\]

**Перевод:**

**Oh the greatest among all the kings who came in the lineage of manu! kaikēyi listened to her servant-maid kūni’s words. You were caught in that kaikēyi’s words and left for the forest, leaving this town of ayōdyā, which has been decorated for your coronation, making kausalyā and sumitrā, who have decorated their locks with flowers which secrete honey, sad. I too am leaving this town and am desirous of going to the world above.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram he laments, thinking of śrī rāma “Just as you left ayōdyā and went to the forest, being caught in the words of kaikēyi who had taken boon from me, after hearing the advice of kūni (mantarā), making kausalyā and sumitrā to be very sad, I too am leaving this town and am going to svargam”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tēnagu māmalarkkūndal kausalaiyum sumittiraiyum sindai nōva** – to make kausalyā and sumitrā, who have locks with flowers which secrete honey abundantly, feel sad.

**kūn uruvin kodum toḻuttai soṟkētta kodiyavaḷ than soṟ koṇḍu inṟu** – caught in the words of kaikēyi who listened to the words of kūni, whose mind is as crooked as her physical form.

**inṟu kānagamē miga virumbi nī tuṟanda vaḷa nagarait tuṟandu nāṇum** – taking my words “You go to the forest” as an order, you went to the forest, which none ventures into, leaving behind this town of ayōdyā. I too am leaving behind this town, which has been decorated for your coronation.

**vānagamē miga virumbi pōginṟēn** – since you have left ayōdyā, which is normally like heaven, I now look at it like hell, and am leaving for heaven.

**manu kulattār tangaḷ kōvē** – Oh the most eminent among all the kings who were born in the lineage of king manu!

Next, we will take up the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.11. Erarnda karu nedumal

*ēr ārnda karu nedumāl irāmanāy vanam pukka adanuk(ku) āṟṟātār ārnda tadavarait tōḷ tayaradan tān pulambiya ap pulambal tannaikūr ārnda vēl valavan kōḻiyar kōn kudaik kulasēkaran soṟceydasīr ārnda tamiḻ mālai ivai vallār tī neṟikkan sellār tāmē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēr ārnda – being complete with all the auspicious qualities; karu nedu māl – sarvēśvaran (lord of all) with bluish complexion; irāman āy – incarnating as śrī rāma; vanam pukka – went to forest; adanukku āṟṟā – unable to bear that activity; tār ārnda tada varai tōḷ – one who has shoulders like a mountain, decorated with victorious garland; dayaradan tān pulambiya – the lament of daśaratan; ap pulambal tannai – that lament; kūr ārnda – being sharp; vēl valavan – one who is an expert in handling spear; kudai kulasēkaran – kulaśēkhara āḻvār who has the white, regal umbrella; sol seyda – mercifully composed; sīr ārnda – having greatness; tamiḻ mālai ivai – these ten pāsurams which are like a garland of tamiḻ hymns; vallār tām – those who are experts \[in reciting\]; tī neṟikkaṇ sellār tāmē – will not go in bad ways

**Перевод:**

**Unable to bear the activity of sarvēśvaran, who is complete in all auspicious qualities and who incarnated as śrī rāma, of going to the forest, daśaratan, who has shoulders like a mountain, decorated with victorious garland, lamented. kulaśēkhara āḻvār, who is an expert in handling spear and who has the regal white coloured umbrella, mercifully composed those words of lament into ten great pāsurams in tamiḻ language. Those who are experts in reciting these ten pāsurams will not go in bad ways.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) He brings this decad to an end, by enunciating the benefits of reciting the decad.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ēr ārnda karu nedumāḷ irāmanāy vanam pukka adanukku āṟṟā** – sarvēśvaran is complete in all aspects and is greater than everyone else. Such sarvēśvaran incarnated as śrī rāma, and went to the forest, which even those who are born due to their karma, will not go to.  Unable to bear this …

**tār ārnda tadavarait tōḷ dayaradan tān pulambiya ap pulambal tannai** – the lament which cakravarti daśaratan, who has a victorious garland on his firm, mountain-like shoulders, expressed as his grief

**kūr ārnda vēl valavan …….soṟceyda** – mercifully composed by kulaśēkhara āḻvār, who has sharp spear, who is the head of kōḻi, also called as uṟaiyūr, the capital city of cōḻa kingdom, and who has the regal, white coloured umbrella as manifestation of his wealth

**sīr ārnda tamiḻ mālai ivai vallār** – śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 4.8 says “*pātyē gēyē ca maduram pramāṇais tribhiranvitam* l *jātibhis saptabhir baddam tantreelayasamanvitam* ll” ((kuśa and lava sang) śrī rāma kāviyam (śrī rāma’s narrative) which is sweet to read or to sing, having three meters (namely *drutam, madyam* and *viḷambitam*), having seven notes (namely śatjam, niśādam, daivatam, pancamam, madyamam, ghāndāram, riśabham), in syncronisation with the strings of vīṇā and rhythm). Those who learn this decad of tamiḻ pāsurams which are like garlands, complete with all the grammatical metrics of music,

**tī neṟīkkaṇ sellār tāmē** – will not go in the path of disregarding bhagavat viśayam (matters relating to emperumān) and engaging with materialistic matters such as śabhdam etc (sound,vision, smell, taste and touch, relating to the senses).

We will take up the pravēśam of the 10th decad, next.

## 10.1. Anganedu madil

*angaṇedu madiḷ pudai sūḻ ayōddi ennum aṇi nagarat(tu) ulaganaittum viḷakkum sōdivengadirōn kulattukkōr viḷakkāyt tōnṟi viṇ muḻudum uyakkoṇḍa vīran tannaiccengaṇedum karumugilai irāman tannai tillai nagart tiruccitra kūdam tannuḷ engaḷ tani mudalvanai emperumān tannai enṟu kolō! kaṇ kuḷirak kāṇu nāḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

am kaṇ – having beautiful places; nedu madil – with tall fortified walls; pudai sūḻ – surrounded on all the sides; ayōddi ennum – being famously called as ayōddi; aṇi nagarattu – in the beautiful city; ulagu anaittum – all the worlds; viḷakkkum sōdi – narāyaṇan, the great effulgence, who makes them shine; vem kadirōn kulattukku – for the clan of sūrya (sun); ōr viḷakkāyt tōnṟi – incarnating as a matchless radiance; viṇ muḻudum – for all the celestial entities; uyakkoṇḍa – (removing their sorrow) one who uplifted; vīran tannai – a great warrior; sem kaṇ – with reddish eyes; nedu karu mugilai – one who has a form like a huge black cloud; irāman tannai – one who makes everyone happy through his physical form and beauty of his eyes; tillai nagar tiruccitra kūdam tannuḷ – in tiruccitra kūtam in tillai nagar (one who has mercifully taken residence); engaḷ tani mudalvanai – one who is our unmatched causative factor for the worlds; emperumān tannai – sarvēśvaran, who is our lord; kaṇ kuḷira kāṇum nāḷ – the day when we would see comfortably with our eyes; enṟu kolō – when is it?

**Перевод:**

**śrīman nārāyaṇan incarnated as śrī rāma, in the beautiful city of ayōddi which has beautiful places within it and which is surrounded by tall, fortified walls. He came as a great effulgence in the clan of sūrya to make all the worlds shine. He incarnated with a matchless radiance and uplifted the celestial entities. He is a great warrior, with reddish eyes and with a form like huge could. He makes everyone happy with his physical form and beautiful eyes. When will I see that unmatched causative factor for the worlds, that sarvēśvaran, who has taken residence in tiruccitrakūtam in tillai nagar such that my eyes will become cool?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār describes how emperumān incarnated as śrī rāma such that celestial entities such as indra et al could get rid of their sorrows and live happily.*

**angaṇ nedu madiḷ pudai sūḻ ayōddi** – ayōddi has *bhōjñas* (materials which are to be enjoyed), *bhōgōpakaraṇas* (the implements which are required to enjoy those materials) and *bhōgastānas* (places where these materials are enjoyed). It is surrounded by fortified walls which rise up to the sky. taittirīya āraṇyakam says “*dēvānām pūrayōdyā*” (the dwelling place of nityasūris is called as ayōdyā). It also says “*purīm hiraṇmayīm brahmā* l *vivēśāparājitām* ll” (brahmām (supreme entity) pervaded that place which is golden and which is termed as *aparājitai* (that which cannot be won over). ayōddi is similar to paramapadam which is described in the vēdams as ayōdyā and which cannot be laid under siege by enemies.

**ennum** – unlike paramapadam which, very few people with knowledge, know of, this ayōddi is famously known all over the world.

**aṇi nagarattu** – this could be construed either as a beautiful place or as a place without any shortcoming in its decorations.

**ulaganaittum viḷakkum sōdi** – through its radiance it makes the entire world to be splendorous. Like the emperumān who incarnated there, and who is described in taittirīya Upanishad nārāyaṇavalli 11 as “*nārāyaṇa param brahma tatvam nārāyaṇa*: *para*: l *narāyana parōjyōti*: *ātmā nārāyaṇa*: *para*: ll” (nārāyaṇa alone is known as supreme entity; nārāyaṇa alone is the supreme truth; nārāyaṇa alone is the supreme radiance; nārāyaṇa alone is also the supreme soul), this place has supreme radiance.

**vengadirōn kulattukku ōr viḷakkāyt tōnṟi** – in the clan of sūrya who removes entire darkness in the worlds; unlike sūrya who comes alternatively as day and night, incarnating as an unmatched splendour

**viṇ muḻudum uyakkoṇḍa vīran tannai** – the unique warrior, who, through his valour, removed the sorrows of celestial entities such as indra et al.

**sengaṇ nedum karumugilai** – one who came with the divine form of a cloud which had imbibed the entire oceans, in order to protect the entire world, apart from the celestial entities, and who has lotus like eyes which manifest that he is sarvēśvaran (lord of all)

**irāman tannai** – one who attracts everyone through the beauty of his divine form, through the beauty of his divine eyes and through his auspicious qualities, without distinguishing between those who have affection and those who are inimical, making everyone to lose out to him. In the earlier verse of *vīran tannai*, the quality mentioned would be applicable here too. Has it not been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 12-29? “*satyēna lōkān jayati dīnān dānēna rāghava*: l *gurūn śuśrūśayā vīrō danuśā yudi śādravān* ll” (rāghava, the warrior, through speaking truth, wins over the world; through magnanimity, wins over the poor; through his servitorśip, wins over his teachers and through his bow, wins over his enemies in the battle)

**tillai nagar tiruccitra kūdam tannuḷ** – one who has mercifully taken residence in tiruccitra kūtam such that even those who were not present during the time of his incarnation could enjoy him now.

**engaḷ tani mudalvanai** – one who became an unmatched causative factor for us; those who could not enjoy him from those times, could enjoy him now.

**emperumān tannai** – one who is a natural lord for us.

**enṟu kolō kaṇ kuḷirak kāṇum nāḷē** – āḻvār says “Due to being caught in the burden of ruling this kingdom, my eyes were feeling sad for not having vorśipped him. When will I worship him so that they will get rid of their sorrow and I can reap the benefit of having been provided with eyes”? The meaning of the term *enṟu kolō* is this: if a date is fixed when āḻvār would be told that he could have darśan of emperumān, it would be equivalent to seeing him this day.

We will take up the second pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 10.2. Vandedirnda tadagai tan

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

## 10.6. Danam maruvu vaidehi

*danamaruvu vaidēhi piriyaluṟṟut taḷarveydic cadāyuvai vaigundattēṟṟivanamaruvu kaviyarasan kādal koṇḍu vāliyaik kon(ṟu) ilangai nagar arakkar kōmānsinamadanga mārudiyāl suduvittānait tillai nagar tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷinidamarnda ammānai irāman tannai ēttuvār iṇai adiyē ēttinēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

danam maruvu – one who is like the wealth (for emperumān); vaidēhi piriyal uṟṟu – separating from pirātti; taḷarvu eydi – (due to that) becoming fatigued mentally; sadāyuvai – (one who fought with rāvaṇa for the sake of pirātti) jatāyu; vaigundattu ēṟṟi – sending him to śrī vaikuṇṭam; vanam maruvu – (fearful of vāli) living in forest, in hiding; kavi arasan – sugrīva, the king of monkeys; kādal koṇḍu – becoming friends with; vāliyaik konṟu – destroying vāli; ilangai nagar – the town of lankā; arakkar kōmān sinam adanga – such that the anger of rāvaṇan, the leader of demons, abates; mārudiyāl suduvittānai – one who burnt, through hanuman; tillainagar tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷ – in tiruccitrakūtam, inside tillai nagar; inidu amarndu – one who mercifully came, sweetly; ammānai – sarvēśvaran (lord of all); irāman tannai – śrī rāma pirān; ēttuvār – those who praise; iṇai adiyē – the divine feet; ēttinēn – I praised

**Перевод:**

**śrī rāma became mentally fatigued after separating from sītāp pirātti, who was his wealth. He sent jatāyu, who had fought valiantly with rāvaṇa, for the sake of pirātti, to śrī vaikuṇṭam. He befriended sugrīva, who was hiding in the forest, fearful of his elder brother, vāli. śrī rāma killed vāli, burnt the town of lankā, with the help of hanuman such that the anger of rāvaṇa, the king of lankā as well as the leader of demons, abated. He came to tiruccitrakūtam, in tillainagar, mercifully. I praised the divine feet of those who praise such śrī rāma.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār speaks about śrī rāma’s separation from sītāp pirātti, granting mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) to jatāyu, making friends with sugrīva, killing vāli and destroying lankā.*

**danamaruvu vaidēhi piriyaluṟṟut taḷarvu eydi jadāyuvai vaigundattēṟṟi** – the word *danam* could be construed as *stanam*, bosom. In that case, the meaning of the verse will be: due to the beauty of her bosom and due to being the daughter of vidēha kingdom, sītāp pirātti was one whose separation made śrī rāma to be mentally fatigued. He sent jatāyu, the king of birds, who fought with rāvaṇa when he abducted sītāp pirātti and was injured, to paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam). Alternatively, *danam* could be construed as wealth. Just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-8-17 “*nityaivaiśā jaganmātā viṣṇō*: *śrīranapāyinī* l *yatā sarvagatō viṣṇus tataivēyam dvijōttama* ll” (pirātti, who is the mother of all the worlds, who will never separate from emperumān, who is the wealth of viṣṇu, is always present. Oh, the most distinguished brāhmaṇa! She is just like viṣṇu, who pervades everything and everyone), we can consider that sītāp pirātti is the wealth of perumāḷ.

**vanamaruvu kaviyarasan kādal koṇḍu vāḷiyaik konṟu** – becoming friendly with sugrīva, the king of monkeys, who was living in forest, in fear of vāli. For the sake of sugrīva, he killed vāli who had lot of strength and valour.

**ilangai nagar kōmān sinam adanga mārudiyāl suduvittānai** – rāvaṇan was immodest, being the king of lankā and being the leader of demons. In order to put down that immodesty and ego, śrī rāma made lankā to burn, with the help of hanuman, whose tail was set on fire.

**tillai nagar citrakūdam tannuḷ inidu amarnda ammānai irāman tannai** – śrī rāma who has taken permanent residence in tiruccitrakūtam now, in order to overcome the deficiency of those who had not enjoyed these activities in those times.

**ēṟṟuvār iṇai adiyē ēttinēnē** – I will praise the divine feet of those who engage with the divine beauty of his form and his auspicious qualities of simplicity etc.

Next, we will take up the 7th pāsuram of this decad.

## 10.7. Kurai kadalai

*kurai kadalai adalambāl maruga eydu kulai katti maṟu karaiyai adanālēṟieri neduvēl arakkarodum ilangai vēndan innuyir koṇḍu avan tambikku arasum īndutirumagaḷōdu inidu amarnda selvan tannait tillainagart tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷarasamarndān adi sūdum arasaiyallāl arasāga eṇṇēn maṟṟarasu tānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kurai kadalai – ocean which roars; adal ambāl – with a harsh arrow; maṟuga eydu – shooting such that it agitated \[the ocean\]; kulai katti – (on that ocean) building a bridge; adanāl – through that bridge; maṟu karaiyai ēri – reaching the other shore of the ocean; eri nedu vēl arakkarodum – demons having long spears which will burn (enemies); ilangai vēndan – and rāvaṇan, the king of lankā; in uyir koṇḍu – removing their sweet lives; avan tambikku – to vibhīśaṇa, the younger brother of rāvaṇa; arasum īndu – giving the kingdom; tirumagaḷōdu – along with sītāp pirātti, who is śrī dēvi’s incarnation; inidu amarnda – mercifully residing; tillai nagar tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷ – in tiruccitrakūtam, inside tillai nagar; arasu amarndān – one who is ruling; adi sūdum – donning the divine feet on the head; arasai allāl – apart from this kingdom; maṟṟu arasu – kingdom different from this; arasu āga eṇṇēn – I will not consider that as anything of importance

**Перевод:**

**śrī rāma built a bridge on the ocean, which roars, by shooting a harsh arrow and reached the other side of ocean, through that bridge. He killed rāvaṇa, the king of lankā, along with demons who had long spears which could burn (enemies). He offered the kingdom of lankā to vibhīśaṇa, rāvaṇa’s younger brother. Such śrī rāma is mercifully residing and ruling in tillai nagar tiruccitrakūtam, along with sītāp pirātti. I will don the divine feet of śrī rāma on my head, considering that as the kingdom to be sought and not any other kingdom.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that he does not need any kingdom other than the kingdom of donning the two divine feet of śrī rāma who mercifully came along with sītāp pirātti after completing the tasks of building sētubandanam (bridge), annihilation of rāvaṇa and coronation of vibhīśaṇa.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kurai kadalai adal ambāl maṟuga eydu** – with cruel arrows which will fly towards enemies, agitating the ocean which was roaring

**kulai katti maṟu karaiyai adanāl ēri** – the *samudra rājan* (king of ocean) was scared of śrī rāma’s arrow and begged him “Please build a bridge over me” and acceded. śrī rāma built a bridge with mountains, on that ocean, and reached the other shore, through that bridge.

**eri neduvēl arakkarodum ilangai vēndan ……. inidamarnda selvan tannai** – he killed the demons, who had long spears which were capable of burning enemies, along with their king rāvaṇa, gave the kingdom to vibhīśaṇa, rāvaṇa’s younger brother and returned with sitāp pirātti in *puśpaka vimānam* (flying vehicle), such that he could forget the intervening separation from her. One who had such wealth.

**tillai nagart tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷ …… maṟṟarasu tānē** – I will don on my head, the divine feet of śrī rāma who went to ayōddi and ruled it. I will consider that alone as the kingdom to be ruled and will not consider the kingdom where one dons a crown as symbol of egotism, as a kingdom to be sought.

Next, we will take up the 8th pāsuram of this decad.

## 10.8. Ambonedu mani mada

*ambon nedu maṇi māda ayōddi eydi araseydi agattiyan vāyt tān mun konṟāntan perum tol kadai kēttu midilaic celvi ulaguyyat tiruvayiṟu vāynda makkaḷsembavaḷat tiraḷvāyttan saridai kēttān tillainagart tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷemperumān tan saridai seviyāl kaṇṇāl paruguvōm innamudam madiyōm onṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

am pon nedu maṇi mādam – (decorated for coronation) having beautiful, distinguished mansions embellished with precious stones; ayōddi eydi – returned to ayōddi city; arasu eydi – took the burden of ruling; tān mun konṟān than – rāvaṇa. who was killed by him earlier, his; peru tol kadai – all the lengthy narratives of past history; agattiyan vāy kēttu – heard through sage agastya; midilaic celvi – sītāp pirātti, the divine daughter of śrī janakarājan; ulagu uyya – for uplifting the world; tiru vayiṟu vāytta – given birth to; sem pavaḷam tiraḷ vāy – with the mouth looking like dense reddish coral; than saridai – śrī rāmāyaṇam, his own narrative; kēttān – mercifully heard; tillainagar citrakūdam tannuḷ – in tiruccitrakūtam, which is inside tillai nagar; emperumān than – (one who has mercifully taken residence) my lord; saridai – narrative; seviyāl kaṇṇāl paruguvōm – we, who have enjoyed hearing through ears and seeing through the eyes; in amudam – even the sweet ambrosia; onṟu madiyōm – will not regard as anything of consequence

**Перевод:**

**After reaching ayōddi which had beautiful and distinguished mansions decorated with precious gemstones, and taking over the reign of his kingdom, śrī rāma heard the narrative of rāvaṇa’s clan through sage agastya. He also mercifully heard śrī rāmāyaṇam, his own narrative, through the dense, reddish coral-like mouths of kuśa and lava, the children of the divine daughter of janakarājan, sītāp pirātti. Such śrī rāma has mercifully taken residence in tiruccitra kūtam, inside tillai nagar. We, who have enjoyed hearing such śrī rāma’s narrative through our ears and seeing through our eyes, will not regard even the sweet ambrosia as anything of consequence.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that he will not regard anything other than the narrative of śrī rāma, who, after returning to ayōddi and after being coronated as the king, heard the narrative of the clan of rāvaṇa, who he had killed, through sage agastya and his own narrative from kuśa and lava, who were given birth to, by sītāp pirātti.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**am pon nedu maṇi māda ayōddi eydi** – śrī rāma entered ayōddi whose mansions were decorated for his coronation event, with distinguished, precious gemstones, so that the entire world would be happy.

**arasu eydi** – Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyṇam bālakāṇḍam 1-89 “*nandigrāmē jatām hitvā bhrātrubhis sahitō’naghā* l *rāma*: *sītām anuprāpya rājyam punar avāptavān* ll” (In nandigrāmam, shedding his matted hair, and being together with his brothers, śrī rāma, who has no faults, attained sītāp pirātti and the kingdom), he took over the reign of ayōddi so that the entire world would prosper.

**agattiyan vāyt tān mun konṟān than perundol kadai kēttu** – since he had nothing else to do, in order to spend time purposefully, through sage agastya, he heard the narrative of the entire clan of rāvaṇa, whom he had killed earlier.

**midilaic celvi ……. madiyōm onṟē** – śrī rāma mercifully heard his own narrative of śrī rāmāyaṇam from kuśa and lava, the children whom sītāp pirātti, daughter of śrī janakarāja, gave birth to, in order to uplift the entire world. That śrī rāma is today mercifully residing in tiruccitra kūtam in tillai nagar. We, who have enjoyed narrative of such śrī rāma through all our senses, just like hanuman, will not consider even the nectar enjoyed by celestial entities as anything of consequence.

We will take up the 9th pāsuram of this decad, next.

## 10.9. Seri tavac cambugan

*seṟi tavac cambugan tannaic cenṟu konṟu seḻumaṟaiyōn uyir mīttut tavattōn īndaniṟaimaṇippūṇ aṇiyum koṇḍu ilavaṇan tannait tambiyāl vānēṟṟi munivan vēṇḍatiṟal viḷangum ilakkumanaip pirindān tannait tillai nagar tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷuṟaivānai maṟavāda uḷḷam tannai udaiyōm maṟṟu uṟu tuyaram adaiyōm anṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

seṟi tavam – having carried out lot of penance; sambugan tannai – a lowly person by name jambukan; senṟu konṟu – searching (out the place where he was) and killing (him); seḻu maṟaiyōn uyir mīttu – redeeming the life of the son of a brāhmaṇa; tavattōn iṇḍha – mercifully given by agastya bhagavān (sage); niṟai maṇi pūṇ aṇiyum koṇḍu – mercifully donning an expensive chain embedded with precious gems; ilavaṇan tannai – demon lavaṇāsura; tambiyāl – through his younger brother śatrugnan; vān ēṟṟi – sending him to valorous svargam (heaven); munivan vēṇḍa – due to the curse of sage durvāsa; tiṟal viḷangum ilakkumanaip pirindān tannai – one who separated from lakśmaṇa who was splendorous with his valour; tillai nagar tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷ – in tiruccitra kūtam inside tillai nagar; uṟaivān tannai – śrī rāma who resides permanently; maṟavāda uḷḷam tannai udaiyōm – we, who have minds which will never forget; maṟṟu uṟu tuyaram adaiyōm anṟē – we will not have the sorrow that “We have not been able to enjoy emperumān”

**Перевод:**

**When a person by name jambukan, born in a lowly family, was carrying out a penance which was not ordained for him, śrī rāma sought him out and killed him; when a brāhmaṇa’s children died soon after they were born, he brought them back from paramapadam; he donned an invaluable chain embedded with precious stones, offered by sage agastya; with the help of his younger brother śatrugnan, he killed the demon lavaṇāsura and sent him to heaven; he separated from his brother lakśmaṇa, due to the curse of sage durvāsa. Such śrī rāma is residing permanently in tiruccitra kūtam in tillai nagar. We, who have our minds always meditating on such narrations of śrī rāma, have no sorrow that “We have not been able to enjoy emperumān”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that there is no sorrow for him since he has not forgotten the narrations mentioned, in śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam, of annihilation of jambuka, bringing back to life the son of a vaidika (one who follows vēdams) and the annihilation of lavaṇāsura.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**seṟi tavac cambugan tannaic cenṟū konṟu** – beheading jambukan, a person born in the fourth varṇam, and who was engaging in a penance which was not appropriate for his birth

**seḻu maṟaiyōn uyir mīttu …..tambiyāl vān ēṟṟi** – the children of a brāhmaṇa, who was conversant with the distinguished vēdam, died soon after they were born. He brought them back from paramapadam. He entered ayōddi, donning the most invaluable chain embedded with precious gems, offered by sage agastya. Through his younger brother śatrugnan he annihilated the demon lavaṇāsuran and sent him to the valorous svargam.

**munivan vēṇḍa ….. uṟaivānai** – he had to separate from younger brother lakśmaṇa, due to the curse of sage durvāsa. lakśmaṇa was his second conscience, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 4-43 ślōkam “*lakśmaṇē mām mayā sārdam praśādi tvam vasundarām* l *dvītiyam mē’ntarātmānam tvāmiyam śrīrupastitā* ll” (Oh lakśmaṇa! You rule this earth, along with me. The wealth of this kingdom has attained you, who are my second conscience). He had to order his younger brother, lakśmaṇa, who was like his life, to separate from him and go ahead of him to paramapadam. Such śrī rāma mercifully took residence in the divyadēśam tiruccitra kūtam so that even those who came after the period of his incarnation could overcome their deficiency of not having seen him.

**maṟavāda uḷḷam tannai udaiyōm maṟṟu uṟu tuyaram adaiyōm anṟē** – We, who always keep recalling such qualities of simplicity, will never have the shortcoming of thinking “We have not enjoyed śrī rāma”.

Next, we will take up the 10th pāsuram of this decad.

## 10.10. Anru sarasarangalai

*anṟu sarāsarangaḷai vaigundattēṟṟi adal aravap pagai ēṟi asurar tammaiven(ṟu) ilangu maṇi nedundōḷ nāngum tōnṟa viṇ muḻudum edir varat tan dāmam mēvisen(ṟu) inidu vīṟṟirunda ammān tannait tillainagart tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷenṟum ninṟān avan ivan enṟētti nāḷum iṟainjuminō eppoḻudum toṇḍīr nīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – that day (when śrī rāma mercifully went to his abode of paramapadam); sara asarangaḷai – (present in ayōddi) all the mobile and immobile creatures; vaigundattēṟṟi – making them to ascend paramapadam; adal aravam pagai ēṟi – mounting garuda, who is the enemy of the powerful snakes; asurar tammai venṟu – winning over the entire species of demons; ilangum aṇi nedum tōḷ nāngum tōnṟa – such that the four long, beautiful, divine shoulders were shining with the wealth of valour; viṇ muḻudum – all the entities in paramapadam; edir vara – coming out to receive him; than dāmam mēvi senṟu – mercifully going to his abode of śrī vaikuṇṭam; inidu vīṟṟīrunda – sitting, manifesting all his greatness; ammān tannai – sarvēśvaran (lord of all); tillai nagar tiruccitra kūdam tannuḷ – in tiruccitra kūtam, inside tillai nagar; enṟum ninṟān – one who resides permanently; avan ivan enṟu ētti – praising him, saying that “He is like that, he is like this etc”; toṇḍīr nīr – you people, who do not desire any other benefit; nāḷum eppoḻudum – without break, everyday; iṟainjumin – praise and uplift yourselves

**Перевод:**

**On that day \[when śrī rāma returned to paramapadam from ayōddi\], he made all the mobile and immobile creatures in ayōddi to ascend to paramapadam. All the nityasūris came out to receive him, after he had destroyed the entire species of demons after mounting garuda, his vehicle.  With the four long, beautiful, divine shoulders shining with the wealth of valour, he returned to his abode. He sat there, manifesting his greatness, as the sarvēśvaran. You people, who do not desire any benefit other than emperumān himself, praise that śrī rāma who has taken permanent residence in the divine abode of tiruccitra kūtam in tillai nagar, and uplift yourselves.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār enjoys in this pāsuram the narration of śrī rāma taking all the jīvātmās from ayōddi to paramapadam \[śrī vaikuṇṭam\] and how he was received by nityasūris when he returned to paramapadam.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**anṟu sarāsarangaḷai vaigundattēṟṟi** – śrīrāma knew that all the living beings, both mobile and immobile, would not be able to sustain themselves in separation from him, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam ślōkam 59-4 “*viśayē tē mahārāja rāma vyasana karśitā* l *api vrukśā*: *parimlānā*: *sa puśpa ankura kōraka*: ll” (Oh mahārāja! In your country, even trees with flowers, sprouts and buds have withered, due to separation from śrī rāma) and in śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam ślōkam 109-22 “*nōcvasat tadayōdyāyām susūkśamamapi druśyatē* l  
*tiryak yōni katāścānyē sarvē rāmam anuvratā*: ll” (Inside ayōddi, not even a small living being is seen. Animals and all other living creatures have followed śrī rāma). Hence, just like lakśmaṇa, who could not sustain himself in separation from śrī rāma, he made all the living beings in ayōddi to ascend to paramapadam.

**adalaravap pagai ēṟi ….. ammān tannai** – He mounted garuda, who is the natural enemy of the mighty snakes and won over the clan of demons. To manifest that wealth of valour, he mercifully came with his naturally present four divine shoulders so that the nityasūris there will feel pleased. He entered śrī vaikuṇṭam and mercifully displayed his greatness in all the splendour.

**tillainagar ….. toṇḍīr nīrē** – for our sake, he lives permanently in tiruccitra kūtam, without reducing any of the greatness which he displays in śrī vaikuṇṭam. āḻvār impels śrī rāma’s devotees, who do not desire any benefit other than emperumān himself, to praise śrī rāma’s auspicious qualities, eminence etc and surrender to him so that they will reap the benefit of having taken birth here.

Next we will take up the last pāsuram of this decad, as well as of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 10.11. Tillai nagar

*tillai nagart tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷ tiṟal viḷangu mārudiyōdu amarandān tannaiellaiyil sīrt tayaradan tan maganāyt tōnṟiṟṟ(u) adu mudalāt tannulagam pukkadū īṟākkolliyalum padaittānaik koṟṟra oḷ vāḷ kōḻiyar kōn kudaik kulasēkaran soṟceydanalliyalin tamiḻ mālai pattum vallār nalam tigaḻ nāraṇan adikkīḻ naṇṇuvārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tillai nagart tiruccitrakūdam tannuḷ – in tiruccitra kūtam inside tillai nagar; tiṟal viḷangum – manifesting his valour; mārudiyōdu – along with hanuman; amarndān tannai – about śrī rāma who is permanently residing; ellai il sīr dayaradan than – for daśarata cakravarti who has unbounded auspicious qualities; magan āy tōnṟiṟṟu adu mudal A – from the time that he was born as a son; than ulagam pukkadu īṟu A – till the time that he ascended his divine, splendorous abode (the narrative of śrī rāmāyaṇam which took place); kol iyalum padai tānai – having an army which has weapons engaged in killing \[enemies\]; koṟṟam oḷ vāḷ kōḻiyar kōn – the head of uṟaiyūr who wields the victorious and beautiful sword; kudai kulasēkaran – śrī kulaśēkhara āḻvār who has the white coloured, regal umbrella; sol seyda – mercifully composed; nal iyal – fitting with tamiḻ grammar for good poems; in tamiḻ mālai pattum – the ten pāsurams which are in sweet tamiḻ garland of verses; vallār – those who are capable of learning; nalam tigaḻ – shining in paramapadam; nāraṇan – sarvēśvaran’s; adi kīḻ naṇṇuvār – will attain the divine feet

**Перевод:**

**kulaśēkhara āḻvār mercifully composed these ten pāsurams in sweet tamiḻ garland of verses, fitting with tamiḻ grammar for good poems, about śrī rāma, who is permanently residing in tiruccitra kūtam in tillai nagar, along with hanuman, who is manifesting his valour. These pāsurams narrate the incidents which happened from the time that he was born as a son for daśarata cakravarti, who has unbounded auspicious qualities, to the time when he ascended his divine abode. Those who are capable of learning these pāsurams will attain the divine feet of sarvēśvaran who is shining in paramapadam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār brings the decad (and the prabandam) to an end by enunciating the benefits to those who recite this decad.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tillainagar …… amarndān tannai** – hanuman had told śrī rāma \[when śrī rāma asked him too to come to śrī vaikuṇṭam along with the rest of the living beings in ayōddi\] that he would not like to come to paramapadam and that he would remain in leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm), just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 40-15 “*snēhō mē paramō rājan tvayi nityam paratiśtita*: l *bhaktiśca niyatā vīra bhāvō nānyatra gaccati* ll” (Oh svāmy! My esteemed affection remains firm always, towards your highness. Oh valorous one! My devotion towards your highness is also permanent. My thoughts do not go towards the lord of śrī vaikuṇṭam too). Just as hanuman stays for ever in leelā vibhūti, śrī rāma too remains permanently in tiruccitra kūtam in tillai nagar.

**ellaiyil sīrt tayaradan …… than ulagam pukkadu īṟā** – emperumān desired that daśarata cakravarti will be his father during the time of śrī rāmāvatāram, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam ślōkam 15-31 “*tata*: *padma palāśākśa*: *krutvātmānam caturvidam* l *pitaram rōcayāmāsa tadā daśaratam nrupam* ll” (Then, the one with eyes like lotus petals (viṣṇu) agreeing to manifest himself four-fold, predisposed towards king daśarata as his father). Thus, he incarnated as the son of daśarata, who had many auspicious qualities. From that time, until the time when he ascended paramapadam, the entire narrative of śrī rāmāyaṇam

**kolliyalum padaittānai** – having an army which has as its weapon, the spear which engages in killing enemies. The word *padai* refers to the weapon spear and the word *tānai* refers to army.

**koṟṟa oḷ vāḷ** – having the sword which is victorious as well as beautiful

**kōḻiyr kōn** – one who is the leader of cōḻa king who ruled from *kōḻi*, also known as uṟaiyūr, as capital

**kudaik kulasēkaran soṟceyda** – mercifully composed by śrī kulaśēkharap perumāḷ, who has the regal, white umbrella as an identification to confirm that he is the head of cēra, cōḻa and pāṇḍiya kingdoms.

**nalliyal mālai pattum vallār** – those who are capable of learning the ten beautiful pāsurams composed as a garland of hymns, fitting with the grammar for tamiḻ poems.

**nalam tigaḻ nāraṇan adikkīḻ naṇṇuvārē** – will attain the divine feet of sarvēśvaran who is radiantly remaining in paramapadam with all the auspicious qualities.

This brings us to the end of this prabandam.

periya perumāḷ periya pirātti tiruvdigaḷē śaraṇam  
kulaśēkharap perumāḷ tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
periyavāccāṇ piḷḷai tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
